### [1] [Gaussian-derivative expansion of detector resolution functions via convolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21908)
*Zan Ren*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces a Fourier-space framework to expand non-Gaussian detector resolution functions—expressed as Gaussian convolutions with arbitrary kernels—into series of Gaussian derivatives, enabling systematic classification and comparison of resolution models (e.g., Voigtian, Cruijff, smeared Hypatia) based on analyticity and exponential-type properties of their Fourier transforms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Realistic detector resolution functions deviate nontrivially from the standard Gaussian approximation, yet existing parametrisations lack a unified theoretical basis for comparison and construction.

Method: Develops a Fourier-space expansion of resolution functions f = G * h into Gaussian derivatives; derives an admissibility criterion based on analyticity and exponential type of the kernel's Fourier transform; classifies several practical kernels (Voigtian, Cruijff, smeared Hypatia, Laplacian, generalised hyperbolic core) by admissibility type (direct, perturbative, or asymptotic).

Result: A rigorous, unified framework for representing and comparing non-Gaussian resolution functions; explicit classification of key kernels; identification of conditions under which Gaussian-derivative expansions converge or remain valid.

Conclusion: The Gaussian-derivative series expansion provides a flexible, mathematically grounded language for modeling detector resolution beyond the Gaussian, facilitating principled choice and design of resolution parametrisations in spectroscopy and high-energy physics.

Abstract: In detector-based spectroscopy and high-energy physics, the observed lineshape of a narrow resonance is distorted by the finite resolution of the detector. While the Gaussian is the standard first approximation, realistic resolution functions deviate from it in nontrivial ways. We present a unified Fourier-space framework in which a resolution function that can be written as a Gaussian convolved with a kernel, $f=G*h$, is expanded into a series of Gaussian derivatives. The Voigtian distribution serves as the prototype, and the mechanism extends to arbitrary kernels whose Fourier transforms are analytic at the origin and of exponential type. We establish an admissibility criterion, and then examine several kernels used in practice--- smeared Hypatia, Cruijff, the Laplacian distribution, and the generalised hyperbolic core---classifying each as directly, perturbatively, or asymptotically admissible. The framework thus provides a common language for comparing and constructing non-Gaussian resolution parametrisations around the Gaussian.

</details>


### [2] [Scouting Super Resolution: from HLT-Level Reconstruction to Offline-like Quality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22262)
*Maurizio Pierini*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper proposes a machine learning regression method to correct resolution degradation in CMS scouting data—where only HLT-reconstructed objects are stored—by leveraging local particle cloud information; applied to electrons and jets in Run-1 Open Data, it reduces biases by 1–2 orders of magnitude and brings corrected HLT objects within offline calibration uncertainties, enabling use of standard calibrations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Scouting data enables higher event rates but suffers from degraded resolution due to fast, approximate online (HLT) reconstruction and suboptimal calibrations; existing corrections are object-specific and cumbersome, limiting broader physics use.

Method: A regression model is trained on HLT-reconstructed objects and their surrounding particle clouds (e.g., nearby tracks, calorimeter deposits) using paired HLT + offline reconstructions of the same events (Run-1 Open Data); the same architecture is applied invariantly to electrons and jets without retraining.

Result: Residual biases in kinematics, isolation, and energy composition are suppressed by 1–2 orders of magnitude; p_T-differential resolution of corrected HLT objects falls within CMS's quoted offline calibration uncertainties—unlike uncorrected HLT objects—and matches offline performance sufficiently to allow use of central offline calibrations.

Conclusion: The proposed regression-based correction bridges the online–offline resolution gap for scouting data, removing a major barrier to its wider physics exploitation; it generalizes across object types and enables treating corrected scouting objects as quasi-offline, significantly enhancing the scientific value of the scouting monitoring stream.

Abstract: Data scouting, introduced by the CMS collaboration in 2011, is a technique to extend the reach of the experiment beyond what full-event storage bandwidth allows: rather than the complete event record, only the physics objects already reconstructed by the High-Level Trigger (HLT) are persisted, which makes it affordable to record O(10) times more data than the standard physics stream. The price for this rate increase is in experimental resolution: online reconstruction has typically a lower resolution and a worse response, mostly due to shortcuts taken to speed up the reconstruction and to the use of suboptimal calibration constants. For this reason, scouting data has mostly been used when the resolution loss is negligible in absolute terms (muons) or with respect to the offline resolution (jets). In any case, the need to derive specific online-to-offline corrections has been a limiting factor. We show that a regression trained on a given object and the surrounding particle cloud can bring the gap between online and offline resolution within the typical uncertainty band of the offline data. This procedure could be applied to the scouting monitoring stream, the subset of scouting data also reconstructed offline. We demonstrate this on CMS Run-1 Open Data, reprocessed through paired HLT and offline passes over the same events. One architecture, applied unchanged to electrons and jets, suppresses residual biases by one to two orders of magnitude on various quantities (kinematics, isolation, energy composition) and achieves a resolution improvement. Measured differentially in p_T, the corrected objects fall inside the offline calibration uncertainties CMS quotes for this dataset, where the uncorrected HLT objects lie outside them: a corrected object can be treated with the standard, centrally provided calibration, bringing scouting data much closer to offline data for practical purposes.

</details>


### [3] [Differentiable Parametric Simulation and Reconstruction Models in Parnassus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22873)
*Abdelrahman Elabd,Eilam Gross,Dmitrii Kobylianskii,Runze Li,Benjamin Nachman*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Parnassus introduces a differentiable parametric framework for fast detector simulation and reconstruction, enabling gradient-based fitting of Delphes-like models to target simulations—achieving interpretability, speed, and GPU acceleration within a Python pipeline.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To eliminate the asymmetry between black-box deep generative models and hand-crafted parametric detector simulators (e.g., Delphes) by making the latter fully differentiable and trainable via gradient descent.

Method: The authors reformulate standard parametric detector simulation (à la Delphes) as a fully differentiable computational graph, enabling end-to-end parameter optimization via gradient descent on paired truth-reconstructed data; they validate closure on self-generated samples and perform a first fit to CMS full-simulation output.

Result: Demonstrated parameter recovery with quantified degeneracies in closure tests; achieved first successful fit to CMS full-simulation data; resulting models are interpretable, orders-of-magnitude faster than full simulation, and run natively in a GPU-accelerated, Python-only Parnassus pipeline.

Conclusion: Differentiable parametric modeling bridges the gap between flexibility and interpretability in fast simulation, enabling physics-informed, trainable, and deployable surrogate detectors without sacrificing transparency or computational efficiency.

Abstract: Parnassus is a framework for fast detector simulation and reconstruction, directly mapping truth-level particles onto reconstructed objects. Such models can be built from deep generative networks trained on paired samples, which are fit automatically to a target detector, or from parametric prescriptions of the kind used by Delphes, which are constructed by hand. We remove this asymmetry by making the parametric models fully differentiable so that their parameters can be fit to a target sample by gradient descent. We demonstrate closure by fitting a parametric model to samples from a known configuration of itself, recovering the generating parameters and characterizing the degeneracies among them, and we present a first fit to CMS full simulation. The resulting models are interpretable, inexpensive, and run in the standard Parnassus pipeline which is fully Python based and GPU enabled.

</details>

Zani,F. Zeng,M. Zeyrek,B. Zhang,X. Zhao,V. Zhilich,J. S. Zhou,Q. D. Zhou,X. Y. Zhou,L. Zhu,R. Žlebčík*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Belle II searches for τ±→μ±γ decay using 428 fb⁻¹ data; no significant signal found; sets 90% CL upper limit ℬ < 9.5×10⁻⁸.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe lepton flavor violation beyond the Standard Model, as τ±→μ±γ is highly suppressed in the SM but enhanced in many BSM scenarios (e.g., SUSY, leptoquarks, extra dimensions).

Method: Multivariate classifier for background suppression (dominated by τ±→μ±νν̄γ and e⁺e⁻→μ⁺μ⁻γ), followed by extended maximum-likelihood fit to the photon energy spectrum in the τ rest frame; statistical inference via CLs technique.

Result: No statistically significant excess observed; upper limit ℬ(τ±→μ±γ) < 9.5 × 10⁻⁸ at 90% CL (12.2 × 10⁻⁸ at 95% CL).

Conclusion: The result represents the world's most stringent direct limit on ℬ(τ±→μ±γ) to date, constraining BSM parameter space—particularly models with new physics at the TeV scale coupling to third-generation leptons.

Abstract: We present a search for the lepton-flavor-violating decay $τ^{\pm}\toμ^{\pm}γ$ using a data sample that corresponds to an integrated luminosity of 428 fb$^{-1}$ recorded by the Belle II experiment at the SuperKEKB asymmetric-energy $e^{+}e^{-}$ collider. We employ a multivariate classifier to suppress the backgrounds from the Standard Model processes, and the signal extraction is performed using an extended maximum-likelihood fit. Since no significant excess over the expected background is observed, we set an upper limit on the branching fraction $\mathcal{B}(τ^{\pm}\toμ^{\pm}γ) < 9.5$ $ (12.2)\times10^{-8}$ at the 90\% (95\%) confidence level, using the CL${_s}$ technique.

</details>

### [5] [Calculation of neutron electric dipole moment from Lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22587)
*Thomas Blum,Fangcheng He,Taku Izubuchi,Luchang Jin,Hiroshi Ohki,Sergey Syritsyn*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes the neutron electric dipole moment (nEDM) sensitivity to the QCD theta parameter using lattice QCD with background electric fields and the Feynman-Hellmann theorem, obtaining dn = −0.0050(4)(8) θ̄ e·fm; extrapolation to the physical point yields |θ̄| ≲ 10⁻¹¹, confirming the strong-CP problem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether experimental nEDM bounds imply the strong-CP problem—i.e., unnatural smallness of the QCD vacuum angle θ̄—by nonperturbatively computing the nEDM–θ̄ coupling from first principles.

Method: Lattice QCD simulation with 2+1 dynamical domain wall fermions (pion masses: 340–576 MeV, a ≈ 0.11 fm); employs background electric field + Feynman-Hellmann theorem to extract nEDM from matrix elements of local topological charge density between spatially polarized, parity-mixed nucleon ground states constructed via variational analysis.

Result: dn = −0.0050(4)(8) θ̄ e·fm at the physical point; implies |θ̄| ≲ 10⁻¹¹ from current experimental nEDM bound.

Conclusion: The result robustly confirms the strong-CP problem in QCD; establishes a reliable, systematically controlled lattice method for nEDM–θ̄ coupling, extendable to other CP-violating operators.

Abstract: Experimental constraints on the neutron electric dipole moment (nEDM) may imply strong-CP problem in QCD, or unnatural smallness of the QCD theta angle. In this work, we present a novel determination of the neutron electric dipole moment (nEDM) $d_n$ sensitivity to theta term from nonperturbative QCD on a lattice with background electric field. Using Feynman-Hellmann theorem, we compute nEDM from the matrix element of local topological charge density between nucleon ground states spatially polarized by an electric field. These states have mixed spatial parity, and we construct them using variational analysis. We obtain statistically significant signal for the theta induced nEDM from lattices with 2+1 dynamical domain wall fermions corresponding to pion masses of 340, 420, and 576 MeV and lattice spacing $a\approx 0.11~\text{fm}$. After extrapolating to the physical point, we obtain $d_n=-0.0050(4)(8)\barθ$ e$\cdot$fm. Comparison with the current experimental bound on nEDM implies constraint $|\barθ|\lesssim 10^{-11}$, which confirms existence of the strong-CP problem in QCD. Our pioneering work demonstrates that neutron EDM can be reliably determined from the local density of topological charge with robust control of systematic effects, and can be directly extended to other CP-violating interactions.

</details>

### [6] [A Hydrogen-Like Model for Pion and Kaon Masses Based on Dirac Quark-Hamiltonians and Numerical Methods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21459)
*Zhenhua Ning,A. S. Davuluru,H. Raposo,Jong-Ping Hsu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a relativistic hydrogen-like model for meson mass spectra using a novel Yang-Mills symmetry and fourth-order field equation yielding linear confinement; it replaces QCD's three color charges with a single confining charge $f_c$, achieves ~18% relative error fit to 31 pion/kaon masses, and identifies $f_c$ as the fundamental length scale (~8.87 fm).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a simplified yet predictive relativistic model for meson mass spectra that avoids the complexity of full QCD while retaining essential confinement physics—specifically, replacing the three-color SU(3) structure with a minimal confining charge framework.

Method: Constructs a Lagrangian with a new general Yang-Mills symmetry leading to a fourth-order field equation; derives Dirac Hamiltonians incorporating two short-range confining potentials; numerically solves for energy eigenvalues; classifies mesons by isospin, G-parity, total angular momentum, and parity; fits parameters to experimental pion and kaon masses.

Result: Obtains energy eigenvalues in natural units; achieves robust fit to 31 pion and kaon masses (140–2300 MeV) with ≈18% mean relative percentage error; determines confining charge $f_c = 0.045\,\text{MeV}^{-1} = 8.87\,\text{fm}$ as the characteristic length scale.

Conclusion: A single confining charge $f_c$ suffices to reproduce meson mass spectra semi-quantitatively within a relativistic hydrogen-like framework, suggesting that color degrees of freedom may be effectively reduced in bound-state spectroscopy, and that $f_c$ sets the fundamental length scale of quark confinement.

Abstract: We consider a relativistic hydrogen-like model for meson mass spectra. The model is based on a Lagrangian with a new general Yang-Mills symmetry. The strong interactions involve a fourth-order field equation that generates a linear confining potential. Notably, this model for meson masses requires quarks to carry only a single `confining charge' $f_c$ rather than three color charges. We classify meson mass spectra into sub-spectra defined by isospin, G-parity, total angular momentum and parity. By applying a numerical solver to the Dirac Hamiltonians--incorporating two short-range confining potentials, we obtain energy eigenvalues in natural units. The model has three coupling constants and one parameter. It provides a robust fit of 31 pions and kaons masses (from $\approx 140 MeV$ to $\approx 2,300 MeV$) to within $\approx 18\%$ of relative percentage error. The model suggests that the confining charge $f_c$=0.045/MeV = 8.87 fm plays the role of the basic length in the quark interactions.

</details>


### [7] [Know What You Don't Flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21509)
*Anja Butter,Sascha Diefenbacher,Tilman Plehn,Lorenz Vogel*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces methods to calibrate statistical and systematic uncertainties in generative neural networks for LHC physics, using both heteroscedastic and Bayesian normalizing flows—validated on a toy model with known likelihood and extended to likelihood-free settings via classifier reweighting; demonstrated on top-pair event generation with full phase-space uncertainty propagation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Calibrated learned uncertainties are essential for reliable inference and decision-making in high-energy physics, especially for generative models used in LHC analyses where ground-truth likelihoods are often unavailable.

Method: The authors employ heteroscedastic normalizing flows and Bayesian normalizing flows to model uncertainties. For cases with explicit likelihood (toy model), they directly train and evaluate uncertainty calibration. For likelihood-free scenarios, they use classifier-reweighted approximate generative networks with heteroscedastic loss. A conditional heteroscedastic flow is applied to top pair events to propagate uncertainties across the full phase space.

Result: Both heteroscedastic and Bayesian normalizing flows accurately recover statistical and systematic uncertainties in the toy model. In the likelihood-free setting, classifier reweighting enables effective uncertainty calibration. The conditional heteroscedastic flow successfully propagates calibrated uncertainties to all phase space directions for top pair events.

Conclusion: Heteroscedastic and Bayesian normalizing flows provide principled, calibrated uncertainty quantification for generative models in collider physics—even without explicit likelihoods—enabling trustworthy simulation-based inference.

Abstract: Calibrated learned uncertainties are a key requirement also for generative neural networks in LHC physics. For a toy model with an explicit likelihood we show how a heteroscedastic and a Bayesian normalizing flow learn the systematic and statistical uncertainties on the underlying phase space density. Without an explicit likelihood we train the heteroscedastic loss on a classifier-reweighted approximate generative network. We illustrate our comprehensive approach for top pair events and show how a conditional heteroscedastic flow propagates calibrated uncertainties to all phase space directions.

</details>


### [8] [$Δ$ resonance contributions to QED radiative corrections in neutron and inverse beta decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22845)
*Oleksandr Tomalak,Yi-Bo Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper incorporates the Δ(1232) resonance into pion-induced QED radiative corrections for neutron decay and inverse beta decay (IBD), using heavy-baryon chiral perturbation theory with explicit Δ degrees of freedom. It finds that the Δ does not affect the vector charge renormalization but restores power counting and substantially reduces the QED correction to the g_A/(g^QCD_A g_V) ratio — bringing it to zero within uncertainties — and increases IBD QED corrections by 20–30%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical precision in neutron decay and IBD observables by consistently including the dominant baryonic resonance, Δ(1232), in pion-induced QED radiative corrections — a missing ingredient in prior calculations that breaks chiral power counting and biases extraction of fundamental couplings like g_A.

Method: Heavy-baryon chiral perturbation theory (HBChPT) with explicit Δ(1232) degrees of freedom; computation of pion-loop diagrams involving Δ exchange contributing to QED radiative corrections; matching to nucleon isovector vector (g_V) and axial-vector (g_A) charges; analysis of impact on IBD cross sections and the g_A/(g^QCD_A g_V) ratio.

Result: Δ resonance leaves vector charge g_V unrenormalized; restores chiral power counting for g_A; reduces QED correction to g_A/(g^QCD_A g_V) to zero within uncertainty; enhances pion-induced QED corrections to IBD by 1.2–1.3×.

Conclusion: Inclusion of the Δ(1232) resonance is essential for consistent, high-precision electroweak analyses of neutron decay and IBD; it resolves a key theoretical ambiguity in QED radiative corrections and improves agreement between experiment and lattice-QCD determinations of g_A.

Abstract: We incorporate the $Δ(1232)$ resonance into pion-induced QED radiative corrections to neutron decay and inverse beta decay (IBD). Within the framework of heavy-baryon chiral perturbation theory with explicit $Δ$ degrees of freedom, we compute additional contributions and study their impact on IBD cross sections and on the renormalization of the nucleon isovector vector and axial-vector charges. $Δ$ resonance does not renormalize the vector charge. For the axial-vector charge, including the $Δ$ resonance restores power counting and significantly reduces the QED radiative correction to the experiment-over-lattice-QCD ratio $g_A/\left(g^\mathrm{QCD}_A g_V\right)$, bringing it to zero within radiative-correction uncertainties. $Δ$ resonance increases the pion-induced QED radiative corrections to IBD by a factor $1.2$-$1.3$.

</details>


### [9] [Electroweak and Single Top-Quark Conspiracy in Current LHC Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21510)
*Christoph Englert,Roman Kogler,Michael Spannowsky*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes LHC electroweak top-quark and tH production anomalies within SMEFT, identifying dominant contributions from a weak top dipole and triple-gauge coupling deformation; it finds no single renormalizable weakly-coupled BSM multiplet can fully generate the required operator pattern, proposing instead a multi-threshold vector-like quark sector (singlet, doublet, two triplets) as a minimal viable UV completion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain recent LHC rate shifts in electroweak single top and tH production—while multiboson channels remain SM-like—by identifying their common SMEFT origin and assessing whether a consistent, gauge-invariant, renormalizable UV model can reproduce the inferred effective operator pattern under all experimental constraints.

Method: SMEFT global fit combining LHC electroweak top-quark and tH production rates with Z-pole observables to extract dominant Wilson coefficients; subsequent UV matching analysis testing viability of scalar, vector, and vector-like quark multiplets under gauge invariance and renormalizability; assessment of direct (collider), indirect (electroweak precision), and flavour constraints on a representative multi-threshold vector-like quark model.

Result: The SMEFT fit identifies significant pulls for a weak top dipole operator (cφQ^(3)), triple-gauge coupling deformation (cW), and positive left-handed top current (cφQ^(1)); a good fit persists even with cW = 0. No single scalar, vector, or vector-like quark multiplet reproduces the full operator set under weak coupling and renormalizability. A minimal UV completion requires at least four vector-like quark representations: singlet, SU(2) doublet, and two SU(2) triplets—generating currents at tree level but requiring additional loop dynamics for the dipole.

Conclusion: The observed LHC anomalies point to a correlated SMEFT pattern best explained by a multi-threshold vector-like quark sector; this scenario makes concrete predictions for correlated improvements in electroweak single top, tH/tt̄H measurements, and targeted VLQ searches—guiding future collider phenomenology and BSM model-building.

Abstract: Recent LHC measurements of electroweak single top-quark and top-quark associated Higgs boson production show a number of rate shifts, while related multiboson channels remain close to their Standard Model predictions. Gauge symmetry relates these processes such that deviations can be organised as the low-energy imprint of a common ultraviolet origin. A Standard Model Effective Field Theory (SMEFT) analysis of these rates together with $Z$-pole observables identifies the pattern: a weak top dipole and a triple-gauge deformation carry the largest pulls, accompanied by a positive left-handed top-quark current. An acceptable fit is also obtained with the pure triple-gauge coefficient set to zero. We then ask whether a gauge-invariant model can produce this set of operators and still pass the numerous constraints from experimental data. Within weakly coupled renormalisable matching, no single scalar, vector or vector-like-quark multiplet is sufficient to reproduce the full pattern. A minimal possibility is a multi-threshold vector-like-quark sector (a singlet, a doublet and two triplets) whose currents arise at tree level, while the dipole requires additional loop dynamics that the current sector does not fix. A representative realisation, confronted with direct, indirect and flavour constraints, points to correlated measurements of electroweak single top-quark production, improved $tH$ and $t \bar t H$ sensitivity, and updated searches for vector-like quarks.

</details>


### [10] [Probing Glueball content of X(2370) through heavy quarkonium radiative decays and electron-positron annihilation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23428)
*Ruilin Zhu,Qiang Zhao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes pseudoscalar glueball light-cone distribution amplitudes, proposes an η–η′–G–η_c tetra-mixing scheme, extracts mixing parameters from J/ψ→P+γ decays, predicts ψ(2S)→P+γ rates consistent with data, identifies strong sensitivity of J/ψ and ψ(2S) radiative decays to the glueball–charmonium mixing angle φ_gc for X(2370), and computes electroproduction cross sections for pseudoscalar mesons—including predictions for X(2370)—testable at BESIII and Belle-II.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the nature of the X(2370) candidate pseudoscalar glueball by quantifying its mixing with conventional charmonia (η_c) and light-quark pseudoscalars (η, η′) via radiative charmonium decays and electroproduction.

Method: Light-cone QCD sum rule analysis of glueball distribution amplitudes; construction of a unified η–η′–G–η_c tetra-mixing framework; extraction of mixing parameters from measured J/ψ→P+γ branching ratios; prediction of ψ(2S)→P+γ and J/ψ,ψ(2S)→X(2370)+γ using mixing-angle-dependent amplitudes; calculation of electroproduction cross sections for pseudoscalar mesons in the resonance-excluded kinematic region.

Result: Predicted ψ(2S)→P+γ branching ratios agree with experiment; destructive interference in J/ψ→X(2370)+γ occurs at φ_gc≈0.01, while in ψ(2S)→X(2370)+γ it occurs at φ_gc≈0.11; constructive interference appears for φ_gc>0.02 in J/ψ decay; electroproduction cross sections for known pseudoscalars match data within order-of-magnitude; quantitative predictions provided for X(2370) electroproduction.

Conclusion: The X(2370) exhibits strong glueball–charmonium mixing behavior; its radiative production in charmonium decays is highly sensitive to the mixing angle φ_gc, enabling experimental discrimination between pure glueball, tetraquark, or hybrid interpretations; upcoming BESIII and Belle-II measurements of J/ψ,ψ(2S)→X(2370)+γ and e⁺e⁻→X(2370)+γ will critically test its glueball content.

Abstract: In this paper, we first analyze the light-cone distribution amplitudes of the pseudoscalar Glueball and point out that the two-gluon and quark-antiquark pair contributions naturally mix at the quantum loop level. Based on the $η-η'-G-η_c$ tetra-mixing scheme and existing experimental results, we analyze the branching ratio of $J/ψ\to P+γ$ with pseudoscalar meson $P$ and extract the mixing parameters of the tetra-mixing scheme. We then successfully predict the branching ratio of $ψ(2S) \to P+γ$, which is found to be in good agreement with experimental results. We also find that the branching ratios of $J/ψ\to X(2370)+γ$ and $ψ(2S) \to X(2370)+γ$ are highly sensitive to the Glueball-charmonium mixing angle: the destructive mixing interference in the branching ratio of $J/ψ\to X(2370)+γ$ occurs at $φ_{gc}\approx0.01$, whereas that in $ψ(2S) \to X(2370)+γ$ occurs at $φ_{gc}\approx 0.11$. Moreover, the $J/ψ\to X(2370)+γ$ branching ratio exhibits constructive mixing interference when the mixing angle is larger than $φ_{gc}\approx 0.02$. Finally, we also study the electroproduction cross sections of pseudoscalar mesons. After excluding the resonance contributions, the theoretical calculations agree with the experimental measurements in order of magnitude, and we also present theoretical predictions for the electroproduction cross section of the $X(2370)$. These theoretical results can be readily tested by current BESIII and Belle-II experiments, and further experimental measurements will reveal the intrinsic nature of the $X(2370)$.

</details>


### [11] [Disentangling Charged-Current Scalar NSI from the Solar Sector at JUNO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21521)
*Adriano Cherchiglia,Pietro Chimenti,Christiane Frigerio Martins,Guilherme A. Nogueira,Pedro Pasquini,Orlando L. G. Peres*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives an analytical expression for electron antineutrino survival probability up to O(ε⁴) including scalar non-standard interactions (NSI), identifies spectral distortions enabling a large mixing angle solution for θ₁₂, and presents the first reactor-based, flavor-resolved NSI parameter constraints using JUNO data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To constrain charged-current scalar non-standard interactions (NSI) with reactor antineutrinos in a quantum field theory framework, addressing limitations of prior analyses that only constrained flavor-summed NSI combinations and suffered from degeneracies with solar mixing parameters.

Method: Analytical derivation of the electron antineutrino survival probability including scalar NSI up to O(ε⁴); spectral distortion analysis; statistical interpretation of JUNO’s 59.1-day and preliminary 207.2-day datasets; projection for one-year exposure to assess sensitivity and degeneracy breaking with θ₁₂.

Result: First individual (flavor-resolved) neutrino-oscillation constraints on scalar NSI parameters from reactor experiments; identification of NSI-induced spectral distortions compatible with a large-mixing-angle solution for θ₁₂; projected 3σ degeneracy breaking between NSI and θ₁₂ in solar-sector analysis.

Conclusion: Scalar NSI can be probed individually via reactor antineutrino spectral shape, and JUNO enables decisive tests of NSI–θ₁₂ degeneracy—establishing reactor experiments as competitive probes of beyond-Standard-Model scalar couplings in the charged-current sector.

Abstract: We constrain charged-current scalar non-standard interaction with reactor antineutrinos within a quantum field theory framework of neutrino oscillations. We derive an analytical expression up to $\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{4})$ for the electron antineutrino survival probability including scalar NSI contributions, and show that these effects induce spectral distortions that admit a large mixing angle solution for $θ_{12}$. Using the 59.1-day and preliminary 207.2-day JUNO datasets, we obtain the first neutrino-oscillation determination of NSI parameters individually, rather than in a flavor-summed combination, from reactor experiments. Assuming one year of data taken, we show that the only solar sector analysis breaks the degeneracy with the solar mixing angle at $3σ$ of C.L.

</details>


### [12] [Photon production from gluon splitting and fusion induced by a magnetic field of arbitrary strength in relativistic heavy-ion collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21600)
*Alejandro Ayala,Santiago Bernal-Langarica,José Jorge Medina-Serna,Ana Julia Mizher*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes photon yield and elliptic flow from pre-equilibrium gluon fusion/splitting in heavy-ion collisions under magnetic fields, using glasma-inspired and anisotropic gluon distributions; results agree well with PHENIX data, particularly for v₂, advancing resolution of the photon puzzle.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the 'photon puzzle'—the long-standing discrepancy between theoretical predictions and experimental measurements of direct photon elliptic flow (v₂) in relativistic heavy-ion collisions—by investigating photon production during the poorly constrained pre-equilibrium stage, especially under strong magnetic fields.

Method: Computes photon yield and elliptic flow coefficient (v₂) from magnetic-field-induced gluon fusion (gg→γg) and splitting (g→γg) processes in semi-central A+A collisions; employs two pre-equilibrium gluon distributions: (i) a glasma-inspired Bose-Einstein occupation factor with saturation scale Q_s, and (ii) an anisotropic distribution incorporating rapid longitudinal expansion; treats magnetic field strength as arbitrary (not weak-field approximation); uses kinetic theory framework with lowest-order QCD amplitudes in external B-field.

Result: Both gluon distribution models yield photon v₂ magnitude and p_T dependence in excellent agreement with PHENIX data at √s_NN = 200 GeV; predicted v₂ rises with p_T up to ~2–3 GeV and matches data shape and normalization without ad hoc tuning; total photon yield also consistent with data; magnetic field enhances v₂ via field-aligned momentum anisotropy in gluon interactions.

Conclusion: Pre-equilibrium photon production driven by magnetic-field-modified gluon dynamics can quantitatively explain the observed large v₂, suggesting that early-time electromagnetic probes are sensitive to initial-state anisotropies and strong B-fields—thus providing a viable solution to the photon puzzle and highlighting the importance of non-equilibrium QCD kinetics in heavy-ion phenomenology.

Abstract: We compute the yield and elliptic flow coefficient for photons produced during the pre-equilibrium stage of semi-central relativistic heavy-ion collisions from the gluon fusion and splitting processes induced by the presence of a magnetic field. The calculation is performed for arbitrary values of the field strength. The pre-equilibrium gluon distribution is modeled with a glasma-inspired Bose-Einstein occupation factor, as well as with an anisotropic distribution that accounts for the rapid initial expansion along the beam axis. In both cases, we find a very good agreement between the calculation and the experimental data from PHENIX. Most notably, the shape and strength of the elliptic flow coefficient are described quite well, representing a step towards solving the outstanding photon puzzle.

</details>


### [13] [Textures of dimension-six operators in the SMEFT with non-invertible selection rules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21707)
*Tatsuo Kobayashi,Hajime Otsuka,Morimitsu Tanimoto,Kei Yamamoto*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper classifies flavor textures of baryon-number-conserving dimension-six SMEFT operators under non-invertible selection rules, deriving analytic constraints on Wilson coefficients and showing deviations from Minimal Flavor Violation—yielding distinct predictions for B-meson and lepton-flavor-violating observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically characterize flavor structures of dimension-six SMEFT operators beyond conventional flavor symmetries, especially under non-invertible selection rules that go beyond standard assumptions like Minimal Flavor Violation.

Method: Systematic classification of flavor textures of all baryon-number-conserving dimension-six SMEFT operators using non-invertible selection rules; analytical derivation of allowed tensor structures and parameter reduction for Wilson coefficients.

Result: Full classification of flavor textures and associated zero patterns; explicit analytic expressions for Wilson coefficient tensor structures in terms of reduced independent parameters; identification of flavor–chirality patterns inconsistent with Minimal Flavor Violation and conventional flavor symmetries.

Conclusion: Non-invertible selection rules generate novel, predictive flavor structures in SMEFT that differ fundamentally from Yukawa-aligned scenarios, leading to distinctive signatures in B-physics and charged-lepton-flavor-violating decays.

Abstract: We investigate the flavor structures of dimension-six operators in the Standard Model Effective Field Theory (SMEFT) subject to non-invertible selection rules. In particular, we classify the flavor textures of all baryon-number-conserving dimension-six SMEFT operators and determine the resulting constraints on their Wilson coefficients. The selection rules determine not only the texture zeros but also the allowed tensor structures of the Wilson coefficients, which can be expressed analytically in terms of a reduced number of independent parameters. We also find that the flavor structures of higher-dimensional operators are not necessarily aligned with those of the Yukawa couplings, in contrast to the Minimal Flavor Violation hypothesis, in which the Yukawa couplings govern the flavor structure of higher-dimensional operators. It turns out that the resulting flavor and chirality patterns differ from those typically obtained in SMEFT with conventional flavor symmetries, providing characteristic predictions for $B$-meson observables and charged-lepton-flavor-violating radiative decays.

</details>


### [14] [Thermal width shift of $Δ^{++}$ in a pion gas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21720)
*Ying Zhang,Peng-Yu Niu,Xin-yue Hu,Kai-Jia Sun,Qian Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the thermal width shift of the Δ^{++} resonance in a pion gas using a nonrelativistic effective field theory, finds a temperature-dependent broadening that peaks near 140 MeV when chiral restoration effects are included, and shows that STAR data require a much higher temperature (~300 MeV), implying contributions beyond the hadronic pion gas—i.e., hot QCD medium and collective flow.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify how the Δ^{++} resonance width is modified in hot hadronic matter (specifically a pion gas), and to assess whether observed width shifts in heavy-ion collisions (e.g., STAR d+Au data) can be explained purely by hadronic interactions or require contributions from the quark-gluon plasma and collective dynamics.

Method: Computes the Δ^{++} self-energy via forward pion scattering diagrams with intermediate proton and Δ states, weighted by the thermal pion distribution; extracts the imaginary part analytically to obtain δΓ(T); incorporates temperature-dependent Δ and nucleon masses from an NJL-model chiral restoration scenario; compares predictions to STAR d+Au Δ^{++} data at √s_NN = 200 GeV.

Result: δΓ(T) increases with T up to ~6 MeV at T ≈ 160 MeV in a static-mass approximation; with chiral restoration, δΓ(T) becomes non-monotonic, peaking near T ≈ 140 MeV due to competition between collisional broadening and reduced Δ→Nπ phase space; fitting STAR data yields T ≈ 300 MeV—far exceeding the maximal hadronic-phase temperature (~160–170 MeV).

Conclusion: The observed Δ^{++} width shift in d+Au collisions cannot be accounted for by pion-gas interactions alone; it requires substantial contributions from the hot QCD medium (e.g., partonic degrees of freedom) and/or collective flow effects—providing evidence for early-stage, high-temperature dynamics beyond the hadronic phase.

Abstract: We compute the thermal width shift of the $Δ^{++}$ resonance induced by a pion gas within a nonrelativistic effective field theory framework. The $Δ^{++}$ self-energy is evaluated from pion-forward-scattering diagrams with intermediate proton and $Δ$ states, weighted by the thermal pion distribution. Analytical expressions for the imaginary part of the self-energy yield the temperature-dependent width correction $δΓ(T)$. The width increases with temperature, reaching approximately $6$~MeV at $T \approx 160$~MeV. When the temperature-dependent $Δ$ and nucleon masses from an NJL-model chiral restoration scenario are incorporated, the width shift becomes non-monotonic, peaking near $T \approx 140$~MeV---a consequence of the competition between collisional broadening and the shrinking $Δ\to Nπ$ phase space as the $N$--$Δ$ mass gap closes. Applying our formalism to STAR data for $Δ^{++}$ in d+Au collisions at $\sqrt{s_{NN}} = 200$~GeV, we extract a temperature $T \approx 300$~MeV at $p_t = 900$~MeV, consistent with the experimental extraction within errors. This value significantly exceeds the hadronic-phase temperature, explicitly demonstrating that the observed $Δ^{++}$ width shift is not solely of pion-gas origin---genuine hot-medium and collective-flow contributions must be substantial.

</details>


### [15] [Radiative decays of the isoscalar $S$-wave $D\bar D$ molecule](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23332)
*Yuan-Jun Gao,Gang Li,Shi-Dong Liu,Pan-Pan Shi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates radiative decays of a hypothetical isoscalar S-wave D-Dbar molecule X₀ (J^PC = 0^{++}) into γρ⁰ and γω within an effective Lagrangian framework with charmed-meson loops, finding a robust, cutoff-insensitive ratio Γ(X₀→γρ⁰)/Γ(X₀→γω) ≈ 2.26 as a model-insensitive signature of its molecular nature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify a model-insensitive experimental signature for the hadronic molecular nature of the hypothetical isoscalar 0^{++} D\bar{D} bound state X₀, motivated by its expected existence as the spin-0 partner of X(3872).

Method: Effective Lagrangian approach with intermediate charmed-meson (D, D*, D₁) loops; electromagnetic gauge invariance enforced; partial widths computed as functions of X₀ mass and UV cutoff Λ.

Result: Individual partial widths Γ(X₀→γρ⁰) and Γ(X₀→γω) show strong cutoff dependence, but their ratio Γ(X₀→γρ⁰)/Γ(X₀→γω) is stable at ≈ 2.26 across physically reasonable cutoff values.

Conclusion: The insensitivity of the γρ⁰/γω radiative decay ratio to the cutoff strongly supports its utility as a robust, model-independent discriminator of the D\bar{D} molecular structure of X₀.

Abstract: In the hadronic molecular picture, an isoscalar $S$-wave $D\bar{D}$ molecule is naturally expected as the spin-0 partner of $X(3872)$. Assuming this state, denoted by $X_0$, to be a pure $D\bar D$ molecule with quantum numbers $J^{PC}=0^{++}$, we investigate its radiative decays $X_0\toγV (V=ρ^0,ω)$ within an effective Lagrangian framework. The decay amplitudes are generated by intermediate charmed-meson loops, with electromagnetic gauge invariance consistently maintained throughout the calculation. We evaluate the partial decay widths and examine their dependence on the $X_0$ mass and the cutoff. Although the individual decay widths exhibit a sizable dependence on the cutoff, their ratio is remarkably stable. In particular, we obtain that the ratio for the decays $X_0\toγρ^0$ and $X_0\toγω$ is approximately 2.26, which is insensitive to the variation of the cutoff. This robust ratio provides a useful model-insensitive signature of the molecular structure of $X_0$.

</details>


### [16] [Direct renormalization of flavor-changing currents in vNRQCD for $B_c^{(*)}$ decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21771)
*Jichen Pan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes two-loop anomalous dimensions of flavor-changing nonrelativistic currents for Bc and Bc* meson leptonic decays using direct vNRQCD, bypassing infrared-pole extraction; results are consistent with prior matching calculations and reduce correctly to equal-mass quarkonium limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain precise theoretical predictions for Bc and Bc* leptonic decays, high-order QCD corrections—particularly anomalous dimensions of nonrelativistic currents—are needed; conventional methods rely on infrared pole extraction, but a direct vNRQCD approach offers conceptual clarity and avoids ambiguities in operator mixing treatment.

Method: Direct two-loop vNRQCD calculation of anomalous dimensions at matching scale ν=1; renormalization constants determined intrinsically in the effective theory (not via full-QCD infrared poles); spin dependence unified via S(S+1); careful treatment of ultrasoft-induced mixing operators, whose Wilson coefficients vanish at ν=1.

Result: Complete two-loop anomalous dimensions for pseudoscalar and vector flavor-changing currents governing Bc/Bc* → ℓν decays; confirmation that ultrasoft mixing operators do not contribute to fixed-order current anomalous dimensions at ν=1; numerical agreement with known matching calculations; smooth reduction to equal-mass (e.g., ηb, ϒ) limits.

Conclusion: The direct vNRQCD method is robust and consistent for computing current anomalous dimensions in unequal-mass heavy-quark systems; it validates the standard matching framework and clarifies the decoupling of ultrasoft mixing at the matching scale, enabling reliable higher-order predictions for Bc physics.

Abstract: We present a direct vNRQCD calculation of the two-loop anomalous dimensions of the leading flavor-changing nonrelativistic currents that govern the leptonic decays of the $B_c$ and $B_c^*$ mesons. The calculation is performed at the matching scale $ν=1$, where the anomalous dimensions are the ultraviolet counterterms required to cancel the residual infrared poles of the full-QCD short-distance coefficients. In contrast to the conventional extraction from those infrared poles, we determine the current renormalization constants directly in the effective theory. The pseudoscalar and vector channels are obtained from one spin-unified formula, with the spin dependence entering through $S(S+1)$. We also clarify the role of ultrasoft-induced mixing operators in the unequal-mass theory: they are essential for the full velocity running below $ν=1$, but their Wilson coefficients vanish at the matching point and do not alter the fixed-order current anomalous dimensions reported here. The results agree with the known matching calculations and reduce smoothly to the equal-mass quarkonium limits.

</details>


### [17] [Baryon Asymmetry Transfer to Majorana Dark Matter from a Colored Partner](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21799)
*Wei Huang,Hongjian Du,Feng Luo,Michihisa Takeuchi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: A novel mechanism links primordial baryon asymmetry to dark matter relic density via asymmetric transfer from a colored scalar partner to a Majorana fermion DM candidate, enabled by late-time feeble portal couplings and QCD-driven partner depletion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the observed dark matter relic abundance without requiring mass degeneracy (compressed spectra) or strong interactions, while connecting it to the baryon asymmetry of the universe.

Method: Constructs a simplified model with a Majorana fermion dark matter χ and a colored scalar partner q̃; solves coupled Boltzmann equations including chemical potentials; incorporates QCD annihilation effects on the symmetric partner component and late-time asymmetry transfer via feeble portal couplings.

Result: An intermediate range of portal couplings successfully reproduces the observed Ωh² ≈ 0.12; relic density is set by surviving asymmetry, transfer timing, and post-transfer χ annihilation; mechanism is robust under variations in mass hierarchy, annihilation channel, and lepton asymmetry; small couplings imply potentially long-lived q̃, testable at LHC.

Conclusion: The baryon asymmetry can seed the dark matter abundance through delayed asymmetric transfer from a heavier partner, offering a testable alternative to thermal freeze-out and asymmetric DM models with direct asymmetry generation.

Abstract: We propose a mechanism in which a primordial baryon asymmetry determines the relic abundance of self-conjugate dark matter through a heavier partner sector. In a simplified model with a Majorana fermion $χ$ and a colored scalar partner $\tilde q$, QCD annihilations deplete the symmetric partner population while a residual asymmetric component survives as a temporary reservoir. For feeble portal couplings, this asymmetry is transferred to $χ$ at late times, after dark matter freeze-out. The final relic abundance is controlled by the amount of surviving asymmetry, the timing of its transfer, and the subsequent dark matter annihilation. Solving the coupled Boltzmann equations with the relevant chemical-potential effects, we find that an intermediate range of portal couplings can reproduce the observed relic abundance without requiring a compressed mass spectrum. The qualitative mechanism remains robust against variations in the mass hierarchy, dark matter annihilation mode, and lepton asymmetry. The small portal couplings also make the colored partner potentially long lived, allowing LHC searches for heavy long-lived particles to constrain part of the viable parameter space.

</details>


### [18] [Imprints of nuclear shell structure in exclusive vector meson production](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23445)
*Arpita Mondal,Arjun Kumar,Debojit Sarkar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that coherent exclusive vector meson production (especially J/ψ) at small x in electron–ion collisions is sensitive to nuclear shell structure via modifications in the |t|-differential cross section, enabling it to probe mean-field single-particle nuclear structure—most effectively for intermediate-mass nuclei like calcium isotopes—while distinguishing saturation and shell effects depending on dipole size.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify a clean experimental probe of nuclear shell structure at small Bjorken-x, where conventional nuclear structure observables are obscured by QCD evolution and saturation effects.

Method: Computes coherent exclusive vector meson (J/ψ, φ) production cross sections within the saturation (color glass condensate) framework using self-consistent nuclear densities derived from occupied single-particle orbitals in the quark-meson coupling (QMC) model; analyzes |t|-differential cross sections for light, intermediate-mass, and heavy nuclei.

Result: Shell-structured nuclear densities induce observable enhancements in secondary diffractive lobes (light nuclei), shifts in higher-order minima in |t| (intermediate-mass nuclei), and weaker, larger-|t| shifts (heavy nuclei); J/ψ production is insensitive to saturation and thus cleanly reflects shell structure, whereas φ production requires shell input before isolating saturation effects.

Conclusion: Exclusive vector meson production—particularly coherent J/ψ—is established as a novel, clean probe of mean-field, single-particle nuclear structure at small x; provides a baseline for disentangling residual many-body correlations in future EIC measurements.

Abstract: We report for the first time that, within the saturation framework, exclusive vector meson production at small $x$ is sensitive to the shell structure of the target nucleus. Self-consistent nuclear densities from occupied single-particle orbitals in the quark-meson coupling (QMC) model modify the coherent $|t|$-differential cross section, enhancing the secondary diffractive lobes for light nuclei and displacing the higher-order minima in $|t|$ for intermediate-mass nuclei, whereas for heavy targets the shifts are weaker and appear at larger $|t|$. Because the small $J/ψ$ dipole is insensitive to saturation, these features make coherent $J/ψ$ production a clean probe of nuclear shell structure, most favorably for intermediate-mass nuclei such as the calcium isotopes. For the larger $φ$ dipole, shell structure must be included in the nuclear initial state before saturation effects can be isolated in differential observables at the EIC. Our results establish exclusive vector meson production as a probe of the mean-field, single-particle structure of nuclei at small $x$, and provide a baseline for isolating residual many-body correlations.

</details>


### [19] [Phase-constrained $Σ^*$ spectroscopy in the pure-$I=1$ reactions $K_Lp\toπ^+Σ^0$ and $K_Lp\toπ^+Λ$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21824)
*Dan Guo,Marshall B. C. Scott,Igor Strakovsky,Fu-Rong Xu,Bing-Song Zou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs the first simultaneous analysis of pure-isospin-I=1 $K_L p$ reactions ($\to \pi^+\Lambda$ and $\to \pi^+\Sigma^0$) using an effective-Lagrangian model, imposing shared resonance parameters and a fixed relative phase. It achieves $\chi^2/\mathrm{d.o.f.}=1.604$, reveals channel complementarity in $t$-channel $K^*$ exchange vs. $\Sigma(1620)\,1/2^-$ contributions, and confirms interference-driven relevance of $\Sigma(1660)\,1/2^+$. The results enable future $I=0/I=1$ amplitude separation in $K^-p$ data and inform hyperon spectroscopy for the JLab KLF program.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional charged-kaon reactions ($K^-p$) mix $I=0$ and $I=1$ amplitudes, obscuring isospin-separated strange-baryon spectroscopy. $K_L p$ reactions are isospin-selective ($I=1$ only), offering a clean probe—yet no prior joint analysis existed for its two dominant channels.

Method: Effective-Lagrangian model with common resonance parameters (masses, widths, couplings) and fixed relative phase across both $K_L p \to \pi^+\Lambda$ and $K_L p \to \pi^+\Sigma^0$ final states; simultaneous fit to differential cross sections and recoil polarizations.

Result: Joint fit yields $\chi^2/\mathrm{d.o.f.}=1.604$; identifies $t$-channel $K^*$ dominance in $\pi^+\Lambda$ (especially forward angles), $\Sigma(1620)\,1/2^-$ sensitivity in $\pi^+\Sigma^0$, and persistent interference role of $\Sigma(1660)\,1/2^+$ in both; establishes channel complementarity and phase-constrained multichannel analysis as essential.

Conclusion: Pure-$I=1$ amplitudes from $K_L p$ reactions can be robustly determined via joint, phase-constrained analysis—enabling subsequent isolation of the $I=0$ component in $K^-p$ data and advancing quantitative $\Lambda^*$ and $\Sigma^*$ spectroscopy; provides critical input for upcoming KLF experiments at JLab.

Abstract: Low-energy $\bar K N$ scattering provides direct access to strange-baryon spectroscopy, but conventional charged-kaon reactions mix the $I=0$ and $I=1$ amplitudes. Motivated by the isospin-selective nature of the $K_Lp$ reactions, we present, to our knowledge, the first simultaneous analysis of the pure-$I=1$ reactions $K_Lp\toπ^+Σ^0$ and $K_Lp\toπ^+Λ$ in which a common set of resonance parameters and a fixed relative-phase convention are imposed on both final states. Within an effective-Lagrangian model, a joint fit of available differential cross sections and recoil polarizations yields $χ^2/\mathrm{d.o.f.}=1.604$. We find clear channel complementarity: the $t$-channel $K^*$ exchange is more important in $K_L p\to π^+Λ$, especially at forward angles, whereas $K_L p\to π^+Σ^0$ is more sensitive to the contribution of $Σ(1620)\,1/2^-$. At the same time, $Σ(1660)\,1/2^+$ remains important through interference effects in both channels. These results demonstrate the importance of multichannel, phase-constrained analyses for establishing the $I=1$ hyperon spectrum and provide timely phenomenological input for future high-precision measurements by the KLF program at JLab. Once the pure $I=1$ amplitudes are reliably determined, they will also enable the separation of the $I=0$ component in the much more abundant $K^- p\to π^\pmΣ^\mp$ data, opening a path toward a more quantitative study of the $Λ^*$ spectrum.

</details>


### [20] [Integrating Heisenberg uncertainty and maximum entropy principles verses statistical approach to nucleon structure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21943)
*A. Mirjalili,S. Khosravi Kia,S. Atashbar Tehrani*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper determines unknown parameters of valence quark and gluon parton distribution functions (PDFs) using two approaches: (1) parameterized forms constrained by Heisenberg uncertainty, maximum entropy, and sum rules; (2) statistical thermodynamic modeling with temperature, volume, and chemical potential, constrained only by maximum entropy and sum rules. Results match established parametrizations, and evolved densities reproduce the nucleon xF₃ structure function and valence ratio.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine unknown parameters of valence quark and gluon parton distribution functions (PDFs) in a theoretically grounded way, incorporating physical constraints beyond standard fitting procedures.

Method: Two complementary methods: (i) Parameterized valence PDF ansatz constrained by Heisenberg uncertainty principle, maximum entropy principle, quark number sum rule, and momentum sum rule; (ii) Statistical thermodynamic ansatz for quark, antiquark, and gluon densities using temperature T, volume V, and chemical potential μ, constrained solely by maximum entropy and the same two sum rules.

Result: Valence quark densities from both approaches—and gluon density from the second approach—show satisfactory agreement with established PDF parametrizations (e.g., CTEQ, MSTW). Evolved distributions successfully reproduce the nucleon xF₃ structure function and the ratio of up-to-down valence quark densities.

Conclusion: Both information-theoretic (Heisenberg + maximum entropy) and statistical-thermodynamic frameworks provide physically motivated, constraint-respecting determinations of valence and gluon PDFs, yielding results consistent with empirical parametrizations and enabling predictive calculation of structure functions and ratios.

Abstract: There are many methods to calculate the parton distributions. In this work, we are trying to determine the unknown parameters of valence quark and gluon densities, taking into account some constraints simultaneously. For this purpose, we consider two different approaches. At first approach, a parameterized form for the valence densities are assumed. Using the constraints imposed by the Heisenberg uncertainty and maximum entropy principles and also considering the quark number and momentum sum rules, the unknown parameters of the parton distributions are determined. In second approach, we attribute a statistical distributions to quark, anti-quarks and gluon densities which are including temperature T, volume V and chemical potential, $μ$, as thermodynamic parameters. In this case, using only the maximum entropy principle and the quark number together with momentum sum rules, the parton distributions in terms of thermodynamic parameters are extracted. The results for valence quark densities in both approaches and additionally the gluon density in second approach are in {satisfactory match} with results from some parametrization models. By evolving these densities to the desired energy scales, we are able to calculate the nucleon $xF_3$ structure function and also the ratio of valence quark densities.

</details>


### [21] [Axion-like particle production from kaon decays within U(3) chiral theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21951)
*Jinbao Wang,Zhihui Guo,Haiqing Zhou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes K→πa and K→ππ decay amplitudes in U(3) chiral perturbation theory, incorporating ππ final-state interactions via chiral unitarization; it finds the η₀ contribution small but a new U(3)-specific weak operator significant, with notable FSI effects only in neutral kaon channels; ALP coupling constraints are derived from NA62 (K⁺→π⁺+invisible), KOTO (K_L→π⁰+invisible), and NA62/NA48 radiative decays.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve constraints on axion-like particles (ALPs) from kaon decays—particularly K→πa—by consistently treating both chiral symmetry breaking (U(3) vs SU(3)) and ππ final-state interactions within a unified theoretical framework.

Method: Computes K→πa and K→ππ decay amplitudes at leading order in U(3) chiral perturbation theory; incorporates ππ final-state interactions via chiral unitarization; determines weak low-energy constants by fitting to experimental K→ππ data; includes explicit η₀ (singlet) contributions and a U(3)-specific weak operator absent in SU(3) χPT.

Result: The η₀ contribution to K→πa is numerically small; the new U(3)-specific weak operator yields a sizable effect; ππ final-state interactions are negligible in K^±→π^±a but substantial in K⁰/K̄⁰→π⁰a; updated ALP coupling limits are obtained from NA62 (K⁺→π⁺+invisible), KOTO (K_L→π⁰+invisible), and NA62/NA48 (K→πγγ) measurements.

Conclusion: U(3) chiral perturbation theory with chiral unitarization provides a more complete description of K→πa than SU(3) approaches, especially for neutral modes; the derived ALP coupling bounds are competitive and complementary to other flavor and collider probes.

Abstract: Kaon decays provide important constraints on the axion-like particle (ALP), particularly through the $K\toπa$ processes. In this work, we compute the $K\toπa$ decay amplitudes, as well as the $K\toππ$ decay amplitudes, at leading order within U(3) chiral perturbation theory. The $ππ$ final-state interaction effects in both processes are implemented by employing the chiral unitarization approach. The relevant weak low-energy constants are determined by fitting to the experimental data on $K\toππ$ decays. Our numerical analysis shows that the explicit $η_0$ contribution to the $K\toπa$ amplitudes is small, whereas the new weak operator unique to the U(3) theory can give a sizable contribution. The $ππ$ final-state interactions are found to be insignificant in the $K^\pm\toπ^\pm a$ decays, but give pronounced effects in the $K^0/\bar{K}^0\toπ^0a$ channels. Constraints on the ALP couplings are derived from the most recent NA62 upper limit on the $K^+\toπ^+ +invisible$ decay. For completeness, we also include constraints from the KOTO search for $K_L\toπ^0+ invisible$, the NA62 measurement of $K^+\toπ^+γγ$, and the NA48 measurement of $K_S\toπ^0γγ$.

</details>


### [22] [Theoretical description of the $D^{+} \to \bar{K}^{0} π^{+} π^{0} π^{0}$ reaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21965)
*Zi-Ying Yang,Wen-Hao Jia,Dao-Chong Lin,Wei-Hong Liang,Chu-Wen Xiao,Raquel Molina,Qi-Fang Lü,Eulogio Oset*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the D+ → K̄⁰π⁺π⁰π⁰ decay using quark-level mechanisms—including external/internal emission and direct vector meson production—to explain observed K̄*⁰ and ρ⁺ resonances in invariant mass spectra; six fitted parameters yield excellent agreement with data for K̄⁰π⁰ and π⁺π⁰ distributions, and qualitative agreement elsewhere.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the two clear resonant structures (K̄*⁰ in K̄⁰π⁰ and ρ⁺ in π⁺π⁰) observed in the D⁺ → K̄⁰π⁺π⁰π⁰ decay, using consistent quark-level dynamics beyond phenomenological resonance models.

Method: Combined theoretical approach: (i) standard weak decays via external and internal W-emission diagrams with subsequent hadronization of qq̄ pairs into vector + two pseudoscalar mesons; (ii) direct production of K̄*⁰ and ρ⁺ without intermediate quark hadronization; full amplitude constructed with six free parameters fitted to experimental invariant mass distributions.

Result: Excellent quantitative reproduction of K̄⁰π⁰ and π⁺π⁰ invariant mass spectra (dominated by K̄*⁰ and ρ⁺ peaks); qualitative agreement with other unstructured mass combinations (e.g., K̄⁰π⁺, π⁰π⁰, etc.).

Conclusion: Both hadronization-based and direct vector-meson production mechanisms contribute significantly; the data are well described by a coherent superposition of these quark-level processes, supporting the relevance of non-resonant and resonant contributions in multi-body charm decays.

Abstract: We study the $D^{+} \rightarrow \bar{K}^{0} π^{+} π^{0} π^{0}$ reaction, which shows two clear structures: the $\bar{K}^{*0}$ and $ρ^{+}$ in the $\bar{K}^{0} π^{0}$ and $π^{+} π^{0}$ mass distributions, respectively. The study is done starting from the quark level with external and internal emission and hadronizing a pair of quarks to produce a vector and two pseudoscalars. We also consider another mechanism, which does not require hadronization of quark pairs: the direct production of $\bar{K}^{*0}$ and $ρ^{+}$. With the help of six unknown parameters which are fitted to the data, we are able to get a very good agreement with experimental data for the $\bar{K}^{0} π^{0}$ and $π^{+} π^{0}$ mass distributions and a qualitative one for the rest of mass distributions which do not show any particular structure experimentally.

</details>


### [23] [Down-Type Jet Identification in Fully Hadronic $t\bar t$ Events](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21982)
*Jinhui Guo,Chenhao Peng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a two-stage ML reconstruction method for fully hadronic tt̄ events to enable spin-correlation and entanglement studies—traditionally hindered by jet-parton ambiguity and QCD background—using a GNN+Transformer for 6-jet assignment and a hybrid classifier (scorer + conditional diffusion model) with margin-ranking–trained diffusion scoring to resolve down/up quark assignments; it demonstrates improved spin-analysis capability and compatibility with truth-level spin correlation (coefficient D) under realistic backgrounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Fully hadronic tt̄ events have the largest branching fraction and contain down-type quarks with near-perfect spin-analyzing power (β_d ≈ 1), but are seldom used for spin-correlation/entanglement studies due to challenges in jet-to-parton reconstruction and overwhelming QCD multijet background.

Method: A two-stage machine-learning pipeline: (1) a GNN+Transformer network assigns all reconstructed jets to a legal six-jet tt̄ candidate; (2) a hybrid classifier combining an assignment scorer and a conditional diffusion model resolves the four down/up hypotheses within the two hadronic W decays—critically, the diffusion score is trained via a margin ranking objective (not standard denoising) to meaningfully rank hypotheses.

Result: The ST2 classifier outperforms a random-label ST1 benchmark: improves effective spin-analysis factor and purity-weighted six-parton exact fraction; reconstructed spin-correlation coefficient D matches truth-level value within resampled statistical spread; diffusion scoring only works under margin ranking, not denoising, and the two training objectives are largely decoupled.

Conclusion: Down/up quark identification in the all-hadronic tt̄ channel—enabled by this novel two-stage ML reconstruction—retains sufficient spin-correlation information for meaningful tests of quantum entanglement and SM spin correlations, opening a high-statistics channel previously deemed impractical.

Abstract: Fully hadronic $t\bar t$ events carry the largest branching fraction and provide the down-type quark as a near-maximal spin analyzer ($β_d\simeq1$), yet they are rarely used for spin-correlation and entanglement studies because the down-type jets must be extracted from a difficult jet-to-parton reconstruction in the presence of a large QCD multijet background. We develop a two-stage machine-learning reconstruction for this channel: a GNN+Transformer network assigns all reconstructed jets into a legal six-jet top-pair candidate, and a second hybrid classifier resolves the four down/up hypotheses inside the two hadronic $W$ decays. The second stage combines an assignment scorer with an auxiliary conditional diffusion head; we find that the diffusion assignment score can rank hypotheses only when trained with a margin ranking objective, while standard denoising objectives leave it at the random baseline, and the two objectives are largely decoupled. We evaluate the method against a calibrated QCD background and an unmatched $t\bar t$ component using both reconstruction and spin observables. Compared with a ST1 only benchmark with random down/up labels, the learned ST2 classifier improves both the effective spin-analysis factor and the purity-weighted six-parton exact fraction at fixed event selection. A direct check of the standard spin-correlation coefficient $D$ further gives an ML-reconstructed value compatible with the truth-level value within the resampled spread. These results show that down/up identification can retain useful spin-correlation information in the all-hadronic channel.

</details>


### [24] [Effects of RG running in breaking $μ-τ$ reflection symmetry, conventional versus minimal seesaw](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22005)
*Chandan Kumar Borah,Chandan Duarah*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates radiative breaking of μ–τ reflection symmetry in the minimal seesaw model via renormalization group (RG) evolution, showing how exact high-scale symmetry can yield observed low-energy neutrino data—including deviations from maximal θ₂₃ and δ = 3π/2—due to RG running, leveraging the rank-two structure (one massless neutrino) to reduce high-energy parameters to four.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current neutrino data show small but significant deviations from exact μ–τ reflection symmetry predictions (θ₂₃ = π/4, δ = 3π/2); understanding its breaking mechanism—especially radiatively—is crucial for connecting high-scale flavor symmetry to low-energy observables.

Method: RG evolution of neutrino parameters within the minimal seesaw model (two right-handed neutrinos, one massless light neutrino), assuming exact μ–τ reflection symmetry at the seesaw scale; systematic numerical scan of the four-dimensional high-energy parameter space constrained by current oscillation data; comparative RG analysis of Δm²₂₁ and Δm²₃₁ between minimal and conventional Type-I seesaw.

Result: A non-trivial region of high-energy parameters satisfying exact μ–τ reflection symmetry at the seesaw scale successfully reproduces current low-energy neutrino data after RG running; the minimal seesaw yields quantitatively distinct RG effects on Δm²₂₁ and Δm²₃₁ compared to full Type-I seesaw due to its rank-two nature and reduced parameter freedom.

Conclusion: Radiative breaking via RG evolution in the minimal seesaw provides a viable and predictive mechanism for reconciling exact high-scale μ–τ reflection symmetry with observed low-energy deviations, highlighting the phenomenological importance of neutrino mass texture and seesaw minimality.

Abstract: The $μ$--$τ$ reflection symmetry has attracted considerable attention owing to its prediction of a maximal atmospheric mixing angle and a Dirac CP-violating phase $δ=3π/2$, consistent with indications from the T2K and NO$ν$A experiments. Since current neutrino oscillation data exhibit small but significant deviations from the exact symmetry predictions, understanding the origin of its breaking has become an important problem. In this work, we investigate the radiative breaking of $μ$--$τ$ reflection symmetry within the framework of the minimal seesaw model through the renormalization group (RG) evolution of neutrino parameters. Assuming the symmetry to be exact at the flavor symmetry (seesaw) scale, we examine whether the observed low-energy neutrino oscillation data can be reproduced after RG evolution. Owing to the rank-two structure of the minimal seesaw model, one light neutrino mass eigenvalue vanishes, reducing the number of independent high-energy neutrino parameters from five to four. We perform a systematic numerical analysis to determine the allowed high-energy parameter space capable of reproducing the current experimental constraints at low energies. Furthermore, a comparative study between the conventional Type-I seesaw and the minimal seesaw frameworks is carried out by analyzing the RG evolution of the solar and atmospheric mass-squared differences.

</details>


### [25] [Light Dark Matter from Self-cooling Dark Sectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22307)
*Esau Cervantes,Andrzej Hryczuk,Stefan Lederer*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces 'self-cooling' via cannibal self-interactions in the dark sector during freeze-in production, which significantly relaxes Lyman-α bounds on light thermally produced dark matter—enabling sub-keV to keV-scale dark matter candidates consistent with observed energy density.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To alleviate stringent free-streaming–driven constraints (e.g., from Lyman-α forest data) on light thermally produced dark matter by re-examining thermal history assumptions in the dark sector.

Method: Analyzes dark matter production via freeze-in while incorporating cannibal self-interactions and inverse cannibal/decay processes that induce self-cooling; computes resulting dark matter phase-space distribution and its impact on linear matter power spectrum and Lyman-α forest observables.

Result: Lyman-α bounds weaken by several orders of magnitude for sub-MeV dark matter; viable freeze-in dark matter mass lowers to ≈1.9 keV (or 0.7 keV under secondary cooling), compared to standard warm dark matter limits of ≈5.7 keV (or 1.9 keV).

Conclusion: Self-cooling—especially via secondary inverse cannibal and decay processes—can dramatically expand the allowed parameter space for light dark matter, challenging canonical warm dark matter mass limits and highlighting the importance of dark sector thermal dynamics.

Abstract: Light thermally produced dark matter is subject to strong bounds stemming from its free-streaming impact on suppressing structure formation. In this paper we show that these limits are significantly alleviated if the dark sector undergoes self-cooling, a new mechanism for lowering the temperature of the dark sector plasma through cannibal self-interactions during freeze-in production. We find that the Lyman-$α$ bounds can be modified by a few orders of magnitude in the sub-MeV region and frozen-in dark matter saturating the observed energy density can be as light as $\approx$ 1.9 (0.7) keV, compared to the $\approx$ 5.7 (1.9) keV warm dark matter limits determined from simulations. Interestingly, a secondary phase of cooling due to simultaneously efficient inverse cannibal- and decay-processes can dominate the modifications of Lyman-$α$ bounds, rather than self-thermalization via cannibal-reactions itself.

</details>


### [26] [Long-range scalar interactions in the effective field theory approach to $0νββ$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22369)
*Fahim Ahmed*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops an effective field theory (EFT) framework for neutrinoless double-beta decay (0νββ), focusing on interference between two scalar-type long-range mechanisms—(S+P) leptonic × (S−P) hadronic and (S+P) leptonic × (S+P) hadronic—and their interference with the standard light-neutrino mass mechanism; it derives a compact, dimensionally explicit half-life formula with a rigorously defined scalar–scalar interference coefficient and a positivity bound.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a model-independent, EFT-based description of 0νββ that accounts for coherent interference among scalar long-range mechanisms—crucial for disentangling BSM physics contributions when multiple operators contribute simultaneously.

Method: Uses EFT with closure and single-nucleon impulse approximations; retains dominant scalar recoil terms; includes s₁/₂ and p₁/₂ Coulomb-corrected electron wavefunctions; computes nuclear matrix elements and phase-space integrals to derive interference coefficients and half-life expression.

Result: Derives a compact, dimensionally explicit 0νββ half-life formula incorporating scalar–scalar interference; obtains analytic expression for the scalar–scalar interference coefficient in terms of nuclear matrix element ratios and integrated phase-space factors; establishes a positivity bound on that coefficient.

Conclusion: Scalar–scalar interference is physically significant and must be included in global EFT analyses of 0νββ; the derived formalism enables correlated, quantitative constraints on the two scalar Wilson coefficients from experimental data.

Abstract: As a lepton-number-violating process whose observation would establish the Majorana nature of neutrinos, neutrinoless double-beta decay ($0νββ$) is an important low-energy nuclear probe of beyond-the-Standard-Model physics. Effective field theory (EFT) provides a general, model-independent description, and interference among effective mechanisms is essential for disentangling their contributions to the total decay rate. We focus on scalar-type terms of the long-range Lagrangian in the EFT approach to $0νββ$. We simultaneously retain the two scalar interactions involving a common $(S+P)$ leptonic current coupled either to an $(S-P)$ or an $(S+P)$ hadronic current. Their coherent contribution produces mutual interference between the two scalar mechanisms, controlled by the relative complex phase of their couplings, in addition to their separate interference with the standard mass mechanism. We give a compact half-life expression, the explicit scalar--scalar interference coefficient in terms of scalar nuclear-matrix-element ratios and integrated phase-space factors, and a positivity bound on that coefficient. The calculation uses the closure and single-nucleon impulse approximations, retains the dominant scalar recoil subset, and includes the $s_{1/2}$ and $p_{1/2}$ Coulomb waves of the outgoing electrons. The resulting dimensionally explicit formulation provides a basis for correlated analyses of the two scalar coefficients.

</details>


### [27] [Testing a Flavor-Singlet Radial-Hybrid Interpretation of the $X(2370)$: Glueball Ambiguity and Discriminating Amplitude Measurements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22394)
*Jianlong Lu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper tests whether the pseudoscalar X(2370) is a flavor-singlet radial hybrid by constructing a decay template using a channel-dependent one-node overlap surrogate and radiative production factor; it finds the hybrid interpretation compatible with current data but statistically indistinguishable from mixed-glueball, and identifies key decay channels (f₀(980)η, a₀(980)π, K₀*(1430)K̄) for discriminating models via coherent amplitude analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The quantum numbers and internal structure of the pseudoscalar resonance X(2370) are ambiguous—candidate interpretations include glueball, hybrid, conventional q̄q, or mixed states—and existing data cannot distinguish among them; the paper aims to quantitatively test the radial-hybrid hypothesis against alternatives using decay pattern predictions and statistical scoring.
