Method: Employs a channel-dependent one-node overlap surrogate for the radial-hybrid wavefunction at m_X = 2.359 GeV, combined with a separate flavor-singlet radiative production factor for J/ψ → γX; constructs decay templates, computes conditional normalizations and suppression patterns (e.g., K* K̄), performs nuisance-parameter–integrated χ² scoring across hybrid/mixed/glueball hypotheses, and evaluates discriminative power via coherent amplitude fits in selected final states including theory uncertainties.

Result: The hybrid template naturally suppresses K* K̄, matches the measured total width under conditional normalization, and reproduces the central J/ψ → γX rate only if the effective singlet-production amplitude is ~4.4× larger than the N_f=2 lattice-anchored prediction; integrated-score differences relative to baseline are −1.29 (hybrid), +0.04 (mixed), +0.41 (glueball), indicating a conditional tie; joint analysis of f₀(980)η, a₀(980)π, and K₀*(1430)K̄ yields Δχ² ≈ 6.5 vs. glueball and ≈ 7.6 vs. radial-q̄q template.

Conclusion: A flavor-singlet radial hybrid interpretation of X(2370) is consistent with current experimental constraints but not statistically distinguishable from a mixed-glueball configuration; definitive discrimination requires high-precision coherent amplitude measurements in key scalar–pseudoscalar final states, incorporating full theory covariance.

Abstract: The nature of the pseudoscalar $X(2370)$ remains unresolved: glueball, hybrid, conventional $q\bar q$, and mixed configurations are all viable at the level of present information. We test a flavor-singlet $J^{PC}=0^{-+}$ radial-hybrid interpretation using a channel-dependent one-node overlap surrogate at $m_X=2.359~\mathrm{GeV}$ and a separate flavor-singlet radiative production factor. The resulting decay template naturally suppresses $K^{*}\bar K$ and can be conditionally normalized to the measured width, although its dominant scalar and tensor channels remain model dependent. Reproducing the inferred central $J/ψ\toγX$ rate requires an effective singlet-production amplitude about $4.4$ times the fixed-$V(0)$ continuation of the available $N_f=2$ lattice anchor. In a common-nuisance screening comparison, the hybrid-mixed-glueball integrated-score difference is $-1.29$, $+0.04$, and $+0.41$ for tight, baseline, and wide nuisance settings, respectively; these values constitute a conditional-score tie rather than a composition measurement. We identify coherent amplitudes in $f_0(980)η$, $a_0(980)π$, and $K_0^{*}(1430)\bar K$ as particularly informative. Under the stated proxy forecast covariance, including theory uncertainty for both templates, their joint separation is $Δχ^2\simeq6.5$ from the shared glueball template and $\simeq7.6$ from the radial-$q\bar q$ surrogate. A flavor-singlet radial hybrid is therefore compatible with current data but remains indistinguishable from a mixed-glueball interpretation; a coherent amplitude analysis can test the competing decay patterns.

</details>


### [28] [Analytical Solution of the Sudakov--BFKL Interpolation Equation for Small-$x$ Gluon TMDs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22408)
*Yanbing Cai,Wenchang Xiang,Mengliang Wang,Daicui Zhou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analytically solves a small-$x$ gluon TMD evolution equation that interpolates between the BFKL and Sudakov regimes, using Mellin-space diagonalization and a $\xi$-dependent evolution factor $F(Y,\xi)$; it identifies a refined matching region $\xi^* \simeq 0.04{-}0.15$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between the high-energy BFKL regime ($\xi \ll 1$) and the Sudakov (collinear/soft-gluon) regime ($\xi \gg 1$) for small-$x$ gluon transverse-momentum-dependent distributions, where existing approximations fail in the transition region.

Method: Derives limiting forms of the evolution equation (BFKL for $\xi \ll 1$, Sudakov for $\xi \gg 1$); solves them via Mellin-space diagonalization of the BFKL kernel and direct integration, respectively; then solves the full interpolation equation using a Mellin-diagonalization ansatz with $\xi$-dependent modification $F(Y,\xi)$, reducing the 2D integral to 1D and enabling analytical evaluation of the evolution kernel.

Result: Obtains an analytical solution featuring an exponential interpolation factor $\exp[H(\xi,\gamma)]$; determines the structure of $H(\xi,\gamma)$ and quantifies the BFKL–Sudakov transition region, yielding a refined matching point $\xi^* \simeq 0.04{-}0.15$, significantly smaller than naive estimates.

Conclusion: The proposed interpolation framework provides a consistent analytical description across both dynamical regimes, with implications for precision QCD phenomenology of small-$x$ TMDs in DIS and hadronic collisions.

Abstract: We analytically solve the evolution equation for small-$x$ gluon transverse-momentum-dependent distributions, which describes the interpolation between the Sudakov and BFKL regimes. We first derive its two limiting forms: the BFKL equation for $ξ= ασs{\bm z}^2/4 \ll 1$ and the Sudakov equation for $ξ\gg 1$. The analytical solutions in these limits are obtained through Mellin-space diagonalization of the BFKL kernel and direct integration of the Sudakov evolution equation, respectively. We then solve the full interpolation equation using a Mellin-space diagonalization ansatz, in which the evolution factor $F(Y,ξ)$ describes the nontrivial $ξ$-dependent modification of a Mellin eigenfunction of the BFKL kernel. This procedure reduces the original two-dimensional integral to a one-dimensional form and permits an analytical evaluation of the resulting evolution kernel. The obtained solution interpolates consistently between the BFKL and Sudakov regimes through an exponential factor $\exp[H(ξ,γ)]$. An analysis of the structure of $H(ξ,γ)$ allows a quantitative estimation of the transition region between the two dynamical regimes. Our calculation implies a potential matching point in the range of $ξ^{*}\simeq 0.04-0.15$, which is substantially smaller than the naive evaluation value.

</details>


### [29] [Eikonal Expansion for Classical Gluon Fields: from Weak to Strong](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22536)
*Shane Brown,Alex DeBrizzi,Alex Kovner*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops an eikonal expansion for classical gluodynamic fields, showing its validity in the weak-field limit but inconsistency in the strong-field regime due to nonlinear order mixing; it proposes a classical 'integrating out' of high longitudinal momentum modes to restore consistency, yielding a renormalized low-momentum effective action.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a consistent eikonal expansion framework for classical gluon fields across both weak and strong field regimes, addressing breakdowns caused by nonlinearities in equations of motion and reconciling with the Born-Oppenheimer high-energy evolution and its classical field interpretation.

Method: Analytical derivation of eikonal expansion for classical Yang-Mills fields; comparison with Born-Oppenheimer evolution; identification of eikonal order mixing in strong fields via nonlinear analysis; implementation of classical mode separation ('integrating out' high longitudinal momentum components) to construct an effective low-momentum action with renormalized currents.

Result: 1) Eikonal expansion is well-defined and perturbative in weak fields. 2) In strong fields, naive eikonal expansion fails due to unsuppressed subeikonal feedback on leading order. 3) A consistent effective theory emerges after classically integrating out high-longitudinal-momentum modes, featuring suppressed self-interactions but strongly renormalized color currents encoding high-momentum contributions.

Conclusion: The eikonal expansion for classical gluodynamics requires careful treatment beyond the naive ordering in strong fields; the proposed classical coarse-graining procedure provides a physically sound and mathematically consistent framework compatible with high-energy evolution schemes like Born-Oppenheimer.

Abstract: We develop a formal procedure of eikonal expansion for classical fields in gluodynamics. We show that in the weak field limit the expansion is straightforward and determines the eikonal components of vector potentials in terms of eikonal components of color currents via classical equations motion. We demonstrate that the recently proposed Born-Oppenheimer high energy evolution yields the ground state wave function interpretable in terms of the classical fields. The relevant classical field is indeed consistent with the eikonal expansion, and contains the leading eikonal and subeikonal components. For strong fields we find that the naive eikonal expansion is inconsistent, as nonlilearities in equations of motion lead to mixing of eikonal orders. Thus the effect of subeikonal components on the leading eikonal component is not suppressed by the eikonality parameter. We show that this problem can be rectified by classically "integrating out" high longitudional momentum components of gluon fields. This leads to a classical action for the low momentum components with small self interaction, but strongly renorlamized currents, which contain contribution due to high momentum modes.

</details>


### [30] [Amplitude analysis and branching fraction calculations of $B_c$ meson decays into $B_sπ$, $DKπ$ and $D_sKK$ final state mesons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22540)
*Elnaz Amirkhanlou,Behnam Mohammadi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reports first observations of three new $B_c^+$ decay modes ($B_c^+\rightarrow D^{+}K^+\pi^{-}$, $D^{*+}K^+\pi^{-}$, $D_s^+K^+K^{-}$) by LHCb and provides theoretical branching ratios using the factorization approach; results show good agreement with experiment, validating the formalism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret newly observed $B_c^+$ decays and test the applicability of the factorization approach in describing multi-body hadronic decays involving charmed mesons.

Method: Theoretical calculation of relative branching fractions $\mathcal{R}(B_c^+\rightarrow Dh^+h^-)$ using the factorization approach, benchmarked against LHCb's experimental measurements relative to $B_c^+\rightarrow B_s^0\pi^+$.

Result: Computed theoretical ratios: $\mathcal{R}(B_c^+\rightarrow D^{+}K^+\pi^{-}) = (1.70\pm0.08)\times 10^{-3}$, $\mathcal{R}(B_c^+\rightarrow D^{*+}K^+\pi^{-}) = (3.63\pm0.45)\times 10^{-3}$, $\mathcal{R}(B_c^+\rightarrow D_s^+K^+K^{-}) = (1.09\pm0.08)\times 10^{-3}$ — all consistent within uncertainties with LHCb data.

Conclusion: The factorization formalism successfully describes these $B_c^+$ three-body hadronic decays, supporting its use for precision QCD studies in heavy-meson physics.

Abstract: In this study, several newly observed decay modes of the $B_c^+$ meson of the form $B_c^+\rightarrow Dh^+h^-$, where $D$ denotes a charmed meson and $h^{\pm}$ represents a charged pion or kaon, have been investigated. The decay channels $B_c^+\rightarrow D^{+}K^+π^{-}$, $B_c^+\rightarrow D^{*+}K^+π^{-}$ and $B_c^+\rightarrow D_s^+K^+K^-$ were observed for the first time. The LHCb Collaboration has measured their branching fractions relative to the reference decay $B_c^+\rightarrow B_s^0π^+$, and reported experimental values including $\mathcal{R}^{\exp}(B_c^+\rightarrow D^{+}K^+π^{-})=(1.96\pm0.23\pm0.08\pm0.10)\times 10^{-3}$ , $\mathcal{R}^{\exp}(B_c^+\rightarrow D^{*+}K^+π^{-})=(3.67\pm0.55\pm0.24\pm0.20)\times10^{-3}$ and $\mathcal{R}^{\exp}(B_c^+\rightarrow D_s^+K^+K^{-})=(1.61\pm0.35\pm0.13\pm0.07)\times 10^{-3}$. In this regard, by performing precise calculations using the factorization approach, we have computed similar branching ratio values, including $\mathcal{R}(B_c^+\rightarrow D^{+}K^+π^{-})=(1.70\pm0.08)\times 10^{-3}$, $\mathcal{R}(B_c^+\rightarrow D^{*+}K^+π^{-})=(3.63\pm0.45)\times 10^{-3}$ and $\mathcal{R}(B_c^+\rightarrow D_s^+K^+K^{-})=(1.09\pm0.08)\times 10^{-3}$. The obtained theoretical results show good consistency with the recent experimental measurements reported by the LHCb collaboration, confirming the effectiveness of the factorization formalism in describing these $B_c^+$ decay modes.

</details>


### [31] [Relic Density of Asymmetric Dark Matter with Breit-Wigner Enhancement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22542)
*Fangyu Liu,Hoernisa Iminniyaz,Qiquan Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the Breit-Wigner Enhancement mechanism to asymmetric dark matter (ADM), analyzing how resonance effects modify cosmic-ray positron excesses observed by PAMELA, ATIC, and PPB-BETS, and constraining both resonance and ADM model parameters against observational bounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous positron excess ('enhancement factor') reported by PAMELA, ATIC, and PPB-BETS experiments using a resonance-based mechanism (Breit-Wigner), and to assess its viability in the context of asymmetric dark matter, which avoids stringent constraints from anti-proton and gamma-ray data.

Method: Theoretical modeling of Breit-Wigner resonance enhancement applied to ADM annihilation or decay channels; parameter space scanning and constraints imposed by matching the observed enhancement factor and satisfying astrophysical upper limits (e.g., on DM density, annihilation cross section, or indirect detection signals).

Result: Resonant enhancement can reproduce the required positron excess within ADM frameworks; viable regions of resonance mass, width, and coupling are identified, subject to consistency with cosmological ADM abundance and indirect detection limits.

Conclusion: The Breit-Wigner Enhancement mechanism remains phenomenologically viable for asymmetric dark matter, offering a consistent explanation of leptonic cosmic-ray anomalies while evading traditional symmetric-DM constraints—provided resonance parameters lie within tightly constrained ranges.

Abstract: The Breit-Wigner Enhancement mechanism provides a novel interpretation for the ``enhancement factor'' derived from data of the PAMELA, ATIC, and PPB-BETS experiments, and we further extends this research to the case of asymmetric Dark Matter. We investigate the impact of resonance mechanism to the asymmetric Dark Matter case and constrain the resonance model parameters to satisfy the ``enhancement factor" and the asymmetric dark matter model parameters to meet the upper bound requirement imposed by the astronomical experiment.

</details>


### [32] [Three-Technology Ultra-High-Energy Neutrino Flavor Measurement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22556)
*Alba Burgos-Mondéjar,William G. Thompson,Carlos A. Argüelles*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a novel three-technology strategy—combining in-ice radio, Earth-skimming, and in-ice optical Cherenkov detectors—to measure ultra-high-energy (UHE) neutrino flavor composition with unprecedented sensitivity, enabling tests of source physics and beyond-Standard-Model propagation effects across TeV–EeV energies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: UHE neutrinos (>100 PeV) are key probes of fundamental physics and BSM models, but measuring their flavor composition—which encodes source production mechanisms and potential new physics during propagation—remains a major experimental challenge.

Method: Integrates complementary detection techniques: in-ice radio arrays (ARA, ARIANNA, RNO-G, IceCube-Gen2 radio), Earth-skimming detectors (TAMBO, POEMMA, Trinity, Auger, GRAND200k), and in-ice optical Cherenkov (IceCube-Gen2 optical); performs joint sensitivity analysis to reconstruct flavor ratios across the UHE regime.

Result: Demonstrates significantly enhanced sensitivity to UHE neutrino flavor composition, enabling discrimination of energy-dependent flavor transitions at the source and during propagation from TeV to EeV scales.

Conclusion: A coordinated, multi-technology observational program is essential—and feasible—to unlock discovery potential in UHE neutrino astrophysics and fundamental physics.

Abstract: Ultra-high-energy (UHE) neutrinos, with energies above $100\,\mathrm{PeV}$, are powerful probes of fundamental physics, offering unique opportunities to test and constrain models beyond the Standard Model. A central challenge is achieving sensitivity to their flavor composition, which encodes information about production mechanisms at the source as well as potential new physics during propagation. We propose, for the first time, a three-technology strategy to measure the flavor composition of UHE neutrinos by leveraging the complementary strengths of planned and existing experiments. This approach integrates in-ice radio (ARA, ARIANNA, RNO-G, the IceCube-Gen2 radio array), Earth-skimming (TAMBO, POEMMA, Trinity, AUGER, GRAND200k), and in-ice optical Cherenkov detectors (the IceCube-Gen2 optical array). We demonstrate the potential to achieve unprecedented sensitivity to UHE neutrino flavors. The method can be used to probe an energy-dependent neutrino flavor transition at the source across energies ranging from TeV to EeV. As such, this article highlights the discovery potential of a coordinated, multi-technology program for advancing both high-energy and ultra-high-energy neutrino astrophysics.

</details>


### [33] [Charmonia at Finite Momentum and Spatial Correlators in Quark-Gluon Plasma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22596)
*Thomas Hardin,Ralf Rapp*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a thermodynamic T-matrix approach with a separable-potential approximation to interpret spatial quarkonium correlation functions from lattice QCD; it incorporates medium effects via heavy-quark selfenergies and screening constrained by Euclidean correlators, and identifies the particle-hole (zero-mode) contribution as essential for reproducing momentum-dependent enhancement of Euclidean correlators—but not spatial correlator suppression.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the longstanding challenge of extracting physically meaningful spectral functions from highly oscillatory, momentum-dependent spatial quarkonium correlation functions computed in finite-temperature lattice QCD.

Method: Lorentz-covariant T-matrix formalism with separable potential; inclusion of medium-modified heavy-quark selfenergies and lattice-constrained screened potential; explicit treatment of both two-particle cut and particle-hole (zero-mode) excitations in the scattering kernel.

Result: Qualitative reproduction of two key lattice-QCD observations: (i) increase of Euclidean correlators with 3-momentum (driven dominantly by zero-mode contributions), and (ii) suppression of spatial correlators (dominated by two-particle cut, insensitive to zero mode).

Conclusion: The zero-mode (particle-hole) channel is crucial for momentum-dependent features of Euclidean correlators but not for spatial correlator suppression—highlighting distinct physical origins for these two observables and validating the extended T-matrix framework as a robust tool for quarkonium spectroscopy in the QGP.

Abstract: Spatial correlation functions of quarkonia in the quark-gluon plasma are available from finite- temperature lattice-QCD with good precision. However, their interpretation in terms of microscopic spectral functions has been challenging due to a highly oscillating momentum integral in their Fourier transform and the need for a reliable evaluation of their 3-momentum dependence. Utilizing the thermodynamic T-matrix approach we first obtain a Lorentz-covariant scattering equation by taking advantage of a separable-potential approximation. Medium effects are included through state-of-the- art heavy-quark selfenergies and a potential screening which we constrain by euclidean correlators from lattice QCD. In addition to the usual two-particle cut in the scattering equation we also include particle-hole excitations, also referred to as zero mode. We find that both the increase of euclidean correlators with 3-momentum and the suppression of the spatial correlators found in lattice-QCD can be qualitatively reproduced, with the zero mode playing an essential role for the former but not for the latter.

</details>


### [34] [On the Four-Loop Higgs--Gluon Form Factor in Nonlocal Quantum Field Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22623)
*E. J. Thompson*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a nonlocal UV completion to render four-loop QCD amplitudes for gluon-fusion Higgs production finite in four dimensions, enabling analytic loop integration and direct numerical evaluation of remaining parameter integrals—bypassing dimensional regularization, UV subtractions, and divergent master integrals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the technical challenges of evaluating singular four-loop multi-scale QCD amplitudes for Higgs production in dimensional regularization—specifically, difficulties in integral reduction and master-integral evaluation at N³LO.

Method: Introduces a nonlocal UV completion that ensures full convergence of each four-loop Feynman diagram in four dimensions while preserving LSZ-analytic structure; derives a closed-form Schwinger-parameter representation where all loop-momentum integrals are performed analytically, yielding finite parameter integrals; defines the strong coupling via a finite normalization condition in an entire-function regularization scheme, with matching to the \overline{\mathrm{MS}} scheme.

Result: A convergent, four-dimensional, analytic–numerical framework for the collider-hard form factor in $gg\to H$ at four loops; regulator-dependent corrections vanish in the local limit $E_M \to \infty$ after finite matching to \overline{\mathrm{MS}}.

Conclusion: Nonlocal UV completion provides a viable, subtraction-free route to high-precision Higgs cross section calculations at N³LO, with implications for robust renormalization-group evolution and lattice–perturbation theory interface.

Abstract: In particle physics the virtual N$^3$LO contribution to gluon-fusion Higgs production in the Standard Model requires four-loop multi-scale QCD amplitudes that in dimensional regularization are dominated by singular integral reduction and difficult master-integral evaluations. In this note we show that a nonlocal UV completion makes each fixed-order four-loop Feynman diagram fully convergent in four dimensions while keeping the analytic structure needed for Lehmann-Symanzik-Zimmermann (LSZ) reduction formula. For gluon-fusion $gg\!\to\!H$ we derive a closed form Schwinger-parameter representation where all loop-momentum integrals are performed analytically and thus leaving finite parameter integrals viable for direct numerical evaluation without needing the usual UV subtractions. This method heps to isolate the collider-hard contribution as a convergent four-loop form factor. The strong coupling then is defined by a finite normalization condition in the entire-function regularization scheme and it is related to the $\overline{\mathrm{MS}}$ coupling by finite matching where after this matching, the regulator-dependent corrections decouple in the local limit $E_M\!\to\!\infty$.

</details>


### [35] [Toward a Unified Axion Cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22667)
*Takeshi Fukuyama*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a unified multi-axion framework rooted in supersymmetric SO(10) grand unification and heterotic string theory, linking two ultralight axion-like particles (ALPs) — one at ~10⁻²² eV (cold dark matter + BEC-driven SMBH seeds) and another at ~10⁻²⁸ eV (transient early-universe modification to resolve Hubble tension) — via aligned eigen-directions and multi-instanton potentials.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile three cosmological puzzles — cold dark matter nature, origin of high-redshift supermassive black holes, and the Hubble tension — within a theoretically consistent, UV-motivated axion framework bridging grand unification and string theory.

Method: Constructs a multi-axion system from supersymmetric SO(10) GUT with Peccei–Quinn symmetry and heterotic string–inspired ALPs; identifies two cosmologically selected mass scales; analyzes BEC collapse for SMBH seeding; computes sound-horizon suppression from transient expansion boost; estimates required axion fraction and decay constant; demonstrates feasibility of alignment and transient potential via multi-instanton effects.

Result: Shows that a two-scale axion spectrum can simultaneously explain dark matter (10⁻²² eV), seed early SMBHs via attractive self-interaction–driven condensate collapse, and alleviate Hubble tension (10⁻²⁸ eV) by transiently enhancing pre-recombination Hubble rate; derives required effective decay constant ~10¹⁸ GeV and confirms theoretical plausibility of alignment and transient dynamics in multi-instanton models.

Conclusion: A unified, string- and GUT-motivated multi-axion framework provides a coherent explanation for distinct cosmological phenomena across vastly different mass scales, demonstrating that UV-complete axion physics can address multiple low-energy observational anomalies without fine-tuning.

Abstract: We explore a unified axion framework connecting grand unification, string-inspired axion-like particles (ALPs), and cosmology. Starting from the QCD axion associated with Peccei--Quinn symmetry in supersymmetric SO(10) GUT, we consider the broader spectrum of ultralight ALPs motivated by heterotic string theory. Within this multi-axion structure, we focus on two ultralight scales selected by cosmological requirements. A mode of order $10^{-22}$ eV is assumed to constitute the dominant dark-matter component and can form a Bose--Einstein condensate whose attractive self-interaction leads to nonlinear collapse, providing possible seeds for high-redshift supermassive black holes. A lighter eigen-direction with a characteristic dynamical scale of order $10^{-28}$ eV can transiently increase the pre-recombination expansion rate and reduce the sound horizon. An order-of-magnitude estimate shows that addressing the Hubble tension requires a sound-horizon-weighted axion fraction of order 0.1, corresponding, for a large initial displacement, to an effective decay constant of order $10^{18}$ GeV. We show that a multi-axion--multi-instanton system can in principle provide both the required aligned light direction and a higher-order effective potential that makes the $10^{-28}$ eV contribution transient. The framework links theoretically motivated axionic degrees of freedom to the distinct scales required by dark-matter, early-black-hole, and Hubble-tension phenomenology.

</details>


### [36] [Orbital Angular Momentum as a Transverse Probe of Elliptic Small-$x$ Gluon Tomography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22744)
*Wei Kou,Xurong Chen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes using orbital angular momentum (OAM) lepton wave packets as tunable transverse probes to image elliptic small-x gluon geometry in diffractive dijet DIS, revealing OAM-dependent response nodes arising from spatial cancellations—not vanishing scattering—and enabling controllable gluon tomography.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a more sensitive and tunable probe for imaging the transverse spatial (elliptic) structure of small-x gluons in hadrons, beyond limitations of conventional plane-wave probes.

Method: Theoretical analysis of hard diffractive dijet deep inelastic scattering using localized lepton wave packets carrying definite orbital angular momentum (OAM) M; computes transverse response densities, defines elliptic observable S_M = D_M − C_M, identifies and interprets response nodes via signed density decomposition and beam-target offset variation.

Result: Finite-yield response nodes appear in S_M for specific OAM channels (e.g., M=1 node from direct-response zero; M=3 node from nontrivial cancellation D_3 = C_3 ≠ 0); signed transverse densities confirm these nodes stem from spatial interference, not suppression of scattering amplitude; node position is controllable via beam-target offset.

Conclusion: Localized OAM lepton wave packets introduce a controllable, probe-side degree of freedom for small-x gluon tomography—enabling selective, high-resolution mapping of target gluon geometry through tunable radial/azimuthal sensitivity.

Abstract: We propose a new way to probe elliptic small-$x$ gluon geometry in hard diffractive dijet deep inelastic scattering: a localized lepton wave packet carrying orbital angular momentum is used as a tunable transverse analyzer of the target. Unlike the conventional plane-wave setup, where the probe provides a fixed transverse projection, the OAM mode supplies additional radial and azimuthal structure while leaving the target dipole matrix element unchanged. We characterize the resulting elliptic response through $S_M=D_M-C_M$ and find finite-yield response nodes whose origin depends on the OAM channel. In the present benchmark, the $M=1$ crossing is associated mainly with a zero of the direct response, whereas the $M=3$ channel exhibits a nontrivial cancellation $D_3=C_3\neq0$. Signed transverse response densities show that this node results from a spatial cancellation rather than from a disappearance of the underlying scattering strength, and its position can be shifted by changing the beam--target offset. The specific sensitive value of $M$ depends on the analyzer profile and kinematics; the broader result is that localized OAM wave packets provide a controllable probe-side degree of freedom for small-$x$ gluon tomography.

</details>


### [37] [Revisiting the $Λ(1405)$ pole structure with convolutional neural networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22747)
*Julius B. Pagayon,Vince Angelo A. Chavez,Denny Lane B. Sombillo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses CNNs to resolve the pole structure of the Λ(1405) resonance, finding strong evidence for two poles on the [bt] sheet and one on the [bb] sheet—supporting a two-state nature: a subthreshold molecular $ar{K}N$ state and a suprathreshold non-molecular $Σπ$-coupled state.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing ambiguity in the complex pole structure of the Λ(1405) resonance—specifically, whether it arises from one or two nearby poles in the complex energy plane—by moving beyond model-dependent analyses to a data-driven, model-agnostic classification approach.

Method: Trains convolutional neural networks on differential features extracted from empirical CLAS differential cross-section lineshapes; employs analytic continuation-informed input representation and inference over large ensembles to classify pole configurations on unphysical Riemann sheets ([bt] and [bb]).

Result: CNN inference robustly favors a three-pole configuration: two poles on the [bt] sheet and one on the [bb] sheet; pole parameter extraction confirms a two-state picture—molecular (below $ar{K}N$ threshold) and non-molecular (above $Σπ$ threshold).

Conclusion: The Λ(1405) is conclusively a two-state system with distinct dynamical origins, reconciling prior conflicting interpretations; the CNN-based classification provides a reproducible, data-driven criterion for resonance pole structure determination in hadron spectroscopy.

Abstract: We revisit the long-standing ambiguity surrounding the complex pole structure of the $Λ(1405)$ resonance by reframing it as a classification problem for convolutional neural networks (CNNs). By training our models to recognize subtle geometric variations on nearly degenerate lineshapes using targeted differential feature on empirical CLAS data, we establish a data-driven consensus on large inference samples. Our results show that the analytic structure characterized by two poles on the $[bt]$ sheet and additional pole on the $[bb]$ sheet globally dominates. The inference-guided pole parameter extraction reveals that the $Λ(1405)$ is a two-state system, characterized by a molecular state sitting below the $\bar{K}N$ threshold and a non-molecular state lying above the $Σπ$ threshold.

</details>


### [38] [Impact of Bubble Nucleation History and Friction on Primordial Black Hole Formation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22846)
*Tathagata Ghosh,Kousik Loho,Sudip Manna*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how cosmological first-order phase transitions (FOPTs) can produce primordial black holes (PBHs) via delayed vacuum patches, analyzing the impact of bubble nucleation profiles (exponential vs. Gaussian) and plasma friction on bubble wall dynamics; it finds friction strongly alters PBH mass and formation time, and shows that the stochastic gravitational-wave spectrum can distinguish frictional effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how FOPTs in beyond-Standard-Model scenarios generate PBHs through stochastic bubble nucleation and delayed vacuum collapse, and to assess how microphysical details—especially bubble nucleation history and plasma friction—affect observable PBH and gravitational-wave signatures.

Method: Adopts a model-independent framework with fixed mean bubble separation scale R_*; compares exponential and Gaussian nucleation profiles; incorporates relativistic bubble wall dynamics including friction from plasma interactions; computes resulting PBH mass and formation time distributions; analyzes associated stochastic gravitational-wave spectra.

Result: Friction significantly modifies bubble wall velocity and evolution, leading to substantial changes in predicted PBH mass and formation time for both nucleation profiles; the gravitational-wave spectrum exhibits distinguishable features between frictional and frictionless cases, enabling observational discrimination.

Conclusion: PBH properties from FOPTs are highly sensitive to bubble nucleation statistics and plasma friction; gravitational-wave observations provide a viable, complementary probe to break degeneracies and constrain microphysical phase-transition dynamics.

Abstract: Cosmological first-order phase transitions (FOPTs) in the early Universe are an exciting prediction of many beyond the Standard Model scenarios. Owing to the stochastic nature of bubble nucleation, some causal patches may remain trapped in the false vacuum long after the surrounding regions have completed the transition and started to redshift. Under suitable conditions, these delayed patches can become sufficiently overdense to collapse into primordial black holes (PBHs). One therefore expects characteristic parameters, such as the PBH mass and PBH formation time to be sensitive to the underlying phase-transition dynamics. Here, we adopt a model-independent framework with a fixed mean bubble separation scale ($R_*$) to directly compare the impact of exponential and Gaussian nucleation profiles on PBH formation. We further incorporate the effects of friction on the bubble walls arising from interactions with the surrounding plasma. We find that friction significantly modifies the bubble dynamics, leading to significant changes in the PBH mass and formation time for both nucleation histories. Moreover, the stochastic gravitational-wave spectrum generated by the FOPT can provide a complementary probe of the underlying dynamics, allowing the effects of friction on the bubble wall evolution to be distinguished from the frictionless case.

</details>


### [39] [Semileptonic Decay of $Λ_b \rightarrow N(1520)\ell^-\barν_{\ell}$ from QCD Light-cone Sum Rules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22851)
*Ke-Sheng Huang,Ao-Sheng Xiong,Hua-Yu Jiang,Fu-Sheng Yu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes vector and axial-vector form factors for the Λ_b^0 → N(1520)^+ semileptonic decay using QCD light-cone sum rules with Λ_b LCDAs, separates contributions from negative- and positive-parity N* resonances (N(1520) and N(1720)) via a two-pole hadronic ansatz, extrapolates form factors using a pole-improved z-expansion, and predicts differential/total branching fractions, lepton asymmetries, and baryon/lepton polarizations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide theoretical predictions for the poorly explored Λ_b^0 → N(1520)^+ℓ⁻ν_ℓ decay — a channel sensitive to QCD dynamics in baryonic transitions and potentially useful for testing SM and probing new physics — by reliably modeling overlapping 3/2⁺ and 3/2⁻ nucleon resonances in the hadronic current.

Method: QCD light-cone sum rules (LCSRs) with Λ_b baryon light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs); tree-level hard-scattering kernels; two-pole hadronic ansatz incorporating both N(1520) (J^P = 3/2⁻) and N(1720) (J^P = 3/2⁺); Lorentz structure matching to isolate N(1520) contribution; pole-improved z-expansion (linear truncation) for q² extrapolation.

Result: Predicted total branching fractions: ℬ_e = ℬ_μ = (12.4 ± 10.5) × 10⁻⁵, ℬ_τ = (5.0 ± 4.3) × 10⁻⁵; differential branching fractions dℬ/dq²; lepton forward-backward asymmetry A_FB; charged-lepton polarization P_ℓ; and N(1520) polarization P_N.

Conclusion: The computed form factors and observables constitute the first dedicated LCSR-based predictions for Λ_b^0 → N(1520)^+ℓ⁻ν_ℓ, enabling future experimental tests at LHCb and Belle II; the methodology successfully disentangles overlapping parity states, enhancing reliability of baryonic transition predictions within QCD.

Abstract: We investigate the complete set of vector and axial-vector form factors for the charged-current transition $Λ_b^0\to N(1520)^+$ using QCD light-cone sum rules (LCSRs) based on the light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs) of the $Λ_b$ baryon, with the hard-scattering kernels evaluated at tree level. In the hadronic representation of the correlation function, we include the pole contributions of both the negative-parity $N(1520)$, with $J^P=3/2^-$, and the positive-parity $N(1720)$, with $J^P=3/2^+$. By matching a selected set of Lorentz structures, we derive sum rules that separate the $N(1520)$ contribution from the $N(1720)$ contribution within the adopted two-pole hadronic ansatz. After extrapolating the large-recoil LCSR results over the physical $q^2$ region using a pole-improved $z$ expansion truncated at linear order, we predict the differential branching fractions, the lepton forward-backward asymmetry, the charged-lepton polarization, and the $N(1520)$ polarization in $Λ_b^0\to N(1520)^+\ell^-\barν_{\ell}$ ($\ell=e,μ,τ$). The corresponding total branching fractions are $\mathcal{B}_{e,μ,τ} =(12.4\pm10.5,\,12.4\pm10.5,\,5.0\pm4.3)\times10^{-5}$. Within the stated approximations, these results may serve as theoretical benchmarks for future experimental studies of this channel.

</details>


### [40] [Disentangling isospin violation in charmonia decaying into $Ξ\overline Ξ$ pairs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22927)
*Huan-Ran Wen,Chun-Qiu Zhao,Xu Cao,Jian-Ping Dai*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes J/ψ and ψ(2S) → ΞΞ̄ decays using BESIII data and model-independent isospin decomposition, finding dominant electric isospin violation (~10%) over magnetic (~3%), with S-wave isospin violation comparable to D-wave.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the origin and magnitude of isospin violation in charmonium decays to baryon-antibaryon pairs, specifically J/ψ and ψ(2S) → ΞΞ̄, where isospin symmetry is expected to be small but measurable.

Method: Model-independent isospin decomposition combined with a full-observable, data-driven analysis of high-statistics BESIII data; supplemented by partial-wave analysis to separate S- and D-wave contributions.

Result: Electric isospin-violating amplitudes dominate (~10%), magnetic contributions are subdominant (~3%); S-wave isospin violation is comparable in magnitude to the D-wave contribution.

Conclusion: Isospin violation in J/ψ and ψ(2S) → ΞΞ̄ is primarily electromagnetic (electric couplings), not strong-interaction–driven, and exhibits non-negligible S-wave component—challenging simple theoretical expectations.

Abstract: Based on a model-independent isospin decomposition, we perform a data-driven analysis of $J/ψ$ and $ψ(2S)$ decays into $Ξ\barΞ$ pairs by leveraging the full set of observables from high-statistics BESIII data. We find that the isospin-violating amplitudes are predominantly driven by the electric couplings, with magnitudes reaching approximately 10%, contrasting with the smaller magnetic contributions of about 3%. A partial-wave analysis further reveals that $S$-wave isospin violation is comparable to the $D$-wave contribution.

</details>


### [41] [Neural Boltzmann Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23022)
*Jonas Spinner,Jack Shergold*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces Neural Boltzmann Equations (NBEs) — a physics-informed neural framework combining neural distribution functions, Monte Carlo importance sampling, and natural gradient evolution — to efficiently solve high-dimensional Boltzmann equations for early-universe particle dynamics, demonstrated via precision calculation of N_eff.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classical Boltzmann solvers suffer from poor scalability in high-dimensional phase-space integrals and fixed-grid discretization, hindering complex system modeling and parameter scans in early-universe cosmology.

Method: NBEs integrate (1) physics-inspired neural distribution functions with learnable parameters, (2) Monte Carlo integration with importance sampling adapted from collider physics, and (3) natural gradient descent for stable time evolution of the distribution.

Result: Achieves scalable, differentiable, and high-precision solution of Boltzmann equations; applied to compute the effective number of relativistic neutrino degrees of freedom (N_eff) with improved accuracy and efficiency over traditional methods.

Conclusion: NBEs provide a flexible, computationally efficient, and physically grounded alternative to classical Boltzmann solvers, enabling previously intractable parameter studies and complex interaction networks in early-universe thermodynamics.

Abstract: The dynamics of particles in the early universe are described by Boltzmann equations, which involve high-dimensional phase-space integrals. Classical approaches use quadrature integration and evolve the system on a fixed momentum grid, which scales poorly to complicated systems and parameter scans, severely limiting the complexity of processes that can be studied. We introduce Neural Boltzmann Equations (NBEs), which combine three coupled concepts to overcome these limitations. First, particle properties are encoded in physics-inspired neural distribution functions, with parameters that can be predicted using neural networks, enabling efficient parameter scans. Second, phase-space integrals are evaluated with Monte Carlo, using importance sampling tools from collider physics. Third, we use the natural gradient method to evolve the system. After demonstrating the individual benefits of NBEs, we use the framework to perform a precision calculation of the effective number of relativistic neutrino degrees of freedom in the early universe.

</details>


### [42] [Integration-by-parts identities in the presence of measurement delta functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23171)
*Saimeng Zhou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces a new IBP identity formulation for dimensionally regulated loop integrals with measurement constraints (delta functions), enabling systematic derivation of differential distributions—especially for transverse-momentum observables—in multi-scale QCD processes like $t\bar t H$ and $t\bar t$ production.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard IBP identities fail to handle measurement constraints involving non-linear delta functions (e.g., $δ(φ(ℓ))$) without distributional derivatives; a geometric, constraint-respecting IBP framework is needed for robust differential cross-section calculations in reverse unitarity.

Method: Constructs IBP identities using vector fields tangent to the hypersurface $φ(ℓ)=0$, ensuring the delta function $δ(φ(ℓ))$ remains undifferentiated and factorizes; implements this in reverse unitarity formalism for transverse-momentum-dependent observables.

Result: Derives constraint-preserving IBP relations applicable to non-linear measurement functions; successfully validates implementation on leading-order $t\bar t H$ and next-to-leading-order $t\bar t$ transverse-momentum spectra.

Conclusion: The proposed geometric IBP method provides a systematic, distribution-safe tool for computing differential distributions in multi-leg, multi-scale QCD processes, extending reverse unitarity to non-linear infrared-sensitive measurements.

Abstract: We derive a convenient form of integration-by-parts (IBP) identities for dimensionally regulated loop integrals that include a measurement constraint imposed by a delta distribution $δ(φ(\ell))$, where $φ$ is a regular and generally non-linear function of the loop momentum. Non-trivial IBP relations can be generated directly by vectors tangent to the hypersurface $φ=0$, leaving the measurement delta as an overall factor and avoiding derivatives of distributions. This construction provides a systematic route to differential distributions within reverse unitarity for non-linear measurement functions, in particular, for transverse-momentum distributions. We implement the construction and validate it for transverse-momentum distributions in $t\bar tH$ production at leading order and $t\bar t$ production at next-to-leading order in QCD.

</details>


### [43] [Search for Light Scalars in the Two Real Singlet Model at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23183)
*Aman Desai,Kristin Lohwasser,Mohamed Ouchemhou,Tania Robens,Prasenjit Sanyal*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies exotic decays h2 → h1h1 in the Two Real Singlet Model (TRSM) using associated production pp → Vh2 (V = W,Z) at the LHC, targeting light scalars (20–120 GeV); it finds that the 4b + ℓνℓ channel yields ~4σ (Run 3) and ~12σ (HL-LHC) significance for Wh2, offering a clean probe of light scalar cascades.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe light CP-even scalars in the TRSM via exotic h2 → h1h1 decays using electroweak-associated production (pp → Vh2), which is experimentally cleaner and complementary to traditional gluon-fusion or vector-boson-fusion channels.

Method: Computes cross sections and event yields for pp → Wh2/Zh2 followed by h2 → h1h1 → 4b, with h1 → bb̄; analyzes sensitivity of the 4b + ℓνℓ final state at √s = 13.6 TeV for LHC Run 3 (300 fb⁻¹) and HL-LHC (3000 fb⁻¹); uses standard cut-based or multivariate strategies appropriate for b-jet-rich, leptonic, MET signatures.

Conclusion: Associated production pp → Vh2 provides a highly sensitive and experimentally clean channel to discover light scalar cascades h2 → h1h1 in the TRSM, especially in the 4b + ℓνℓ final state, with compelling reach at both LHC Run 3 and HL-LHC.

Abstract: We investigate exotic scalar decays $h_2 \to h_1 h_1$ in the Two Real Singlet Model (TRSM), focusing on light CP-even scalars with $20 \le M_{1} \le 60$~GeV and $20 \le M_{2} \le 120$~GeV. Unlike previous studies focused on gluon--gluon fusion or vector-boson fusion production, we explore associated production with electroweak gauge bosons, $pp \to Vh_2$ {\sl($V = W,Z$)}, as a complementary and experimentally clean probe of light scalar cascades in the TRSM. The subsequent decay $h_1 \to b\bar{b}$ leads to final states containing four $b$-jets accompanied by a charged lepton and missing transverse energy. We evaluate the sensitivity of the $4b+\ellν_\ell$ channel at $\sqrt{s}=13.6$~\TeV for the LHC Run~3 and the HL-LHC.
  For an integrated luminosity of $300~\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$, the $W^+h_2$ channel provides the strongest sensitivity, reaching a significance of approximately $4σ$, while the $W^-h_2$ channel reaches approximately $3σ$. At the HL-LHC with $3000~\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$, the corresponding significances increase to approximately $12σ$ and $8σ$, respectively, highlighting the strong discovery potential of associated light-scalar production in the TRSM.

The nonresonant amplitudes are calculated within the color-singlet non-relativistic QCD framework, while the $J^{PC}=2^{++}$ resonance is combined coherently with the continuum. We find that continuum--resonance interference significantly modifies the invariant-mass distributions near $M_{HH}\simeq M_X$. The $J/ψJ/ψγ$ channel exhibits a pronounced phase-dependent resonant structure, with a near-resonance cross section of $0.038$--$0.111~{\rm fb}$. In contrast, the $η_cη_cγ$ channel is continuum dominated and shows only a localized interference effect with an extremely suppressed production rate, rendering its experimental observation extremely challenging. These channel-dependent features arise from the different effective decay structures and demonstrate the importance of an amplitude-level treatment of the $X(6900)$ contribution.

For χ_{cJ} → e⁺e⁻γ, NLO width reductions are 6% (J=0), 66% (J=1), 21% (J=2) when E_γ^cut increases from 100 MeV to 500 MeV; analogous reductions for μ⁺μ⁻γ are 17%, 66%, 39%.

Abstract: In this work, we comprehensively study the total and differential decay widths of the radiative Dalitz decays $H \to l^+l^-γ$ up to QCD next-to-leading order (NLO) accuracy within the framework of NRQCD factorization. Our calculation includes the decays of $S$-wave states ($η_c, η_b$) and the $P$-wave triplets ($χ_{cJ}, χ_{bJ}$ for $J=0,1,2$) to both electron ($l=e$) and muon ($l=μ$) final states. To match realistic experimental detection thresholds, systematic kinematic cuts are implemented on the final-state photon energy. Our analysis of the lepton-pair invariant mass distribution shows distinct singular behaviors across the multiplets originating from the lepton-pair threshold region, which is regularized by the lepton mass, and the soft-photon region, respectively. For the $S$-wave states, there is only one peak near the lepton-pair threshold region, while for the $J=0$ and $J=2$ $P$-wave states, the peaks show up in both regions. However, for the $J=1$ $P$-wave states, the peak only appears in the soft-photon region. Such features provide a rich venue to probe the $γ^{\ast}\to l^{+}l^{-}$ form factor in heavy quarkonium decay. Integrating over the bounded phase spaces reveals a distinct hierarchy among the $χ_{QJ}$ states in the sensitivity of our theoretical predictions to the soft-photon energy cuts, ordered as $χ_{Q1} >χ_{Q2}>χ_{Q0}$. In the $χ_{cJ}\to l^{+}l^{-}γ$ cases, as the energy cut increases from 100 $\mathrm{MeV}$, to 500 $\mathrm{MeV}$ the theoretical predictions at QCD NLO are reduced by $6\%(χ_{c0})$,$66\%(χ_{c1})$, and $21\%(χ_{c2})$ for $l=e$, and by $17\%(χ_{c0})$,$66\%(χ_{c1})$, and $39\%(χ_{c2})$ for $l=μ$. Comparing our predictions with the upcoming high-precision experimental tests at BESIII will definitely deepen our understanding of the predictive power of perturbative calculations.

  <summary>Details</summary>

Conclusion: A generalized chiral U(1)_X charge structure successfully reconciles a less constrained Z' sector, Dirac neutrino masses, and thermal scalar dark matter, demonstrating that anomaly-free charge freedom can address multiple BSM challenges in a unified framework.

Abstract: Extensions of the Standard Model by a $U(1)_{X}$ gauge symmetry predict a new $Z'$ boson, whose mass is severely constrained by high-mass dilepton resonance searches at the LHC. We show that these bounds can be relaxed by generalizing the chiral $(B-L)$ charge assignment while preserving gauge anomaly cancellation. The resulting $U(1)_X$ charges are parameterized by two independent parameters, and an appropriate choice of these parameters suppresses the $Z'$ branching fraction into charged leptons, substantially weakening the dilepton constraints. The framework accommodates Dirac neutrino masses through a Dirac type-I seesaw mechanism and a stable singlet scalar DM candidate without requiring an additional discrete symmetry. We find that the modified charge assignment, although beneficial for collider phenomenology, introduces a tension between obtaining the observed relic abundance and satisfying direct detection limits when DM annihilation proceeds solely through the $Z'$ portal. The inclusion of scalar-mediated annihilation channels resolves this tension and opens up a broad viable parameter space, with DM masses ranging from $M_{\rm DM}\simeq 60~{\rm GeV}$ to $10~{\rm TeV}$. The resulting parameter space is consistent with the observed relic density, direct detection, collider, and electroweak precision constraints, demonstrating that a generalized chiral charge structure can simultaneously accommodate a less constrained $Z'$ sector, neutrino masses, and viable dark matter phenomenology.

</details>


### [47] [Two-loop renormalisation and Higgs phenomenology in the five-dimensional MSSM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23555)
*Ammar Abdalgabar,Alan S. Cornell,Aldo Deandrea,Howeida M. Esmaeil,Mohammed Omer Khojali*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs a comprehensive two-loop renormalisation group analysis of the 5D MSSM, confirms perturbative consistency via cancellation of dangerous two-loop UV contributions due to N=2 supersymmetry, computes the radiatively corrected Higgs effective potential with full KK resummation, and demonstrates phenomenological viability—consistent with Higgs mass, EWPO, and coupling data—while preserving power-law running.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the 'little hierarchy problem' in the MSSM by generating a sufficiently large trilinear stop coupling via power-law RG evolution in five dimensions, thereby accommodating the observed 125 GeV Higgs mass without excessively heavy superpartners.

Method: Derivation of complete two-loop RGEs for gauge, Yukawa, and soft SUSY-breaking parameters in the 5D MSSM; rigorous analysis of UV behaviour exploiting N=2 supersymmetry; computation of the Higgs effective potential including full Kaluza-Klein tower via KK resummation; numerical scans constrained by Higgs mass, electroweak precision observables (S, T, U), Higgs coupling modifiers (κ-framework), and LHC signal strengths.

Result: Leading two-loop UV divergences cancel exactly due to N=2 SUSY; power-law RG running is preserved at two loops; full KK-resummed Higgs potential yields m_h ≈ 125 GeV for compactification scales M_c ∼ 10–30 TeV and moderate stop masses (∼1–3 TeV); viable parameter space satisfies all current collider and precision constraints.

Conclusion: The 5D MSSM remains a theoretically consistent and phenomenologically viable extension of the MSSM: two-loop corrections do not destabilise the power-law mechanism, and the model accommodates the Higgs mass and precision data without fine-tuning, offering a compelling alternative to high-scale SUSY breaking.

Abstract: The five-dimensional Minimal Supersymmetric Standard Model (5D MSSM) provides an attractive framework in which power-law renormalisation group evolution naturally generates a sizeable trilinear stop coupling, allowing the observed Higgs boson mass to be reproduced without requiring too heavy a supersymmetric spectra. In this work we present a comprehensive two-loop analysis of the 5D MSSM, deriving the complete renormalisation group equations governing the gauge, Yukawa and soft supersymmetry-breaking sectors and examining the perturbative consistency of the theory. Particular attention is given to the ultraviolet behaviour of the model, where we demonstrate that the potentially dangerous leading two-loop contributions cancel as a consequence of the underlying $\mathcal{N}=2$ supersymmetry, leaving a well-behaved perturbative expansion. Building upon this framework, we compute the radiatively corrected Higgs effective potential including the complete Kaluza-Klein (KK) contributions through KK resummation, and investigate the resulting implications for the Higgs sector. The phenomenological viability of the model is subsequently explored through detailed numerical studies of the Higgs boson mass, electroweak precision observables, Higgs coupling modifiers and signal strengths, allowing constraints on the compactification scale and supersymmetric parameter space to be established. We find that the inclusion of the two-loop corrections preserves the characteristic power-law behaviour of the five-dimensional theory while yielding a phenomenologically viable parameter space consistent with current collider and precision measurements.

</details>

### [48] [How Much Geometry Does Newtonian Dynamics Fix?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21642)
*C S López-Monsalvo*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper reconstructs Newtonian mechanics from first principles by dynamically constructing spacetime geometry—introducing a propagator and bilinear form only as needed—thereby deriving the Principle of Inertia as a theorem. It generalizes Newton’s Second Law to arbitrary torsion, identifies totally antisymmetric torsion as a classical spin three-form carried along worldlines, and proposes a torsion-driven spin transport law dependent solely on acceleration—no coupling constant—leaving clock identification (arc-length ↔ time) open.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To eliminate ad hoc geometric assumptions in Newtonian mechanics—particularly fixed spacetime structure—and instead derive inertial structure, inertia, and universality of free fall from minimal, operational constructs (propagator, bilinear form), revealing deeper geometric origins of physical principles.

Method: Geometric reconstruction using differential geometry: defines a propagator for parallel transport and a non-degenerate bilinear form; derives inertial motion as geodesic-like curves insensitive to scale and torsion; formulates Newton’s Second Law as derivative compatibility; imposes arc-length parametrization to constrain torsion to a totally antisymmetric 3-form; interprets its contraction with velocity as classical spin; postulates a spin transport law tied exclusively to acceleration.

Result: 1) Principle of Inertia emerges as a theorem, not an axiom; 2) Universality of free fall arises geometrically prior to gravity; 3) Newton’s Second Law holds for arbitrary torsion, with force differing from m·a by an exact term; 4) Force-free motion fixes torsion to a 3-form; 5) This 3-form yields a natural classical spin satisfying the spinning-particle constraint identically; 6) Spin precesses relative to a parallel-transported frame at rate determined solely by acceleration.

Conclusion: Newtonian mechanics can be fully reconstructed from a non-degenerate bilinear form and propagator, without presupposing Galilean spacetime. Inertial structure, spin, and dynamics are unified geometrically: spin is intrinsic torsion, its evolution is acceleration-driven and parameterization-independent, and gravitation remains external. The framework requires three constitutive postulates and one physical conjecture, all mathematically proven.

Abstract: Newtonian mechanics is usually written on a spacetime of fixed geometry. Here we build that geometry, introducing a propagator and a bilinear form only as the description demands. Within this reconstruction the Principle of Inertia emerges as a theorem rather than an independent postulate. Inertial motion is blind to the scale of the form and to the torsion the propagator defines, and that blindness is a geometric precursor of the \emph{universality of free fall}, before gravity enters. Newton's Second Law is a compatibility between two derivatives along a curve. The force exceeds the mass times the lowered acceleration by an exact term, and the law survives arbitrary torsion. Requiring force-free motion to be inertial along curves parametrised by arc length restricts the torsion to be totally antisymmetric. The remaining torsion is a three-form which we postulate the particle carries along its worldline. Its contraction with the velocity annihilates that velocity, so the supplementary condition of spinning-particle mechanics holds identically, and we propose that contraction as a classical spin. The algebra is familiar from relativistic spin hydrodynamics; the assignment, to the best of our knowledge, has not been proposed elsewhere. Since inertial motion resolves neither the mass nor the spin, the predictive content lies in the transport law we postulate, which turns the spin against a parallel-transported frame at a rate fixed by the acceleration alone and by no coupling constant. That rate becomes a laboratory number only once the arc-length parameter is identified with a clock, which we leave open. The dynamics asks non-degeneracy of the form, so this spacetime is not Newton--Cartan's Galilean one, and gravitation lies outside the construction. We prove every mathematical result, make three constitutive postulates and one physical conjecture.

</details>


### [49] [Finite-term recurrences in a generalized Bochner--Krall family](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21802)
*L. M. Anguas,D. Barrios Rolanía,B. Shapiro,M. Tater*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper classifies a family of differential operators of the form $T = z^j \partial_z^j + z^m \partial_z^\ell$ whose monic eigenpolynomials satisfy finite-term recurrence relations; it proves that such recurrences exist only when $j=1$ and $k=\ell-m$ divides $\ell$, determines all recurrence coefficients in closed form, shows the associated difference operator has order $\ell$, and confirms two conjectures of Horozov–Shapiro–Tater regarding operator type and order equality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize differential operators whose eigenpolynomials obey finite-term recurrence relations — a property linked to bispectrality and orthogonal polynomial theory — and to verify specific conjectures (Conjectures 1.9 and 1.10) by Horozov–Shapiro–Tater on operator classification and order correspondence.

Method: Algebraic analysis of eigenpolynomial structure under the given differential operators; derivation of necessary and sufficient conditions for finite-term recurrence via coefficient matching and divisibility constraints; explicit computation of recurrence coefficients; construction of an operator factorization to identify Type (2) structure; verification of differential-difference order equality.

Result: A complete classification: finite-term recurrence occurs iff $j=1$ and $k=\ell-m \mid \ell$; all recurrence coefficients are obtained in closed form; the associated difference operator is shown to have order $\ell$; every admissible $T$ admits an explicit factorization proving it is Type (2) per Horozov–Shapiro–Tater’s Conjecture 1.10; the equality of differential and difference orders confirms Conjecture 1.9.

Conclusion: The paper fully resolves the recurrence condition for this class of operators, provides explicit structural results (coefficients, factorization, order), and affirms key conjectures in the bispectral theory of differential operators with polynomial eigenfunctions.

Abstract: We classify the differential operators \(T=z^j\partial_z^j+z^m\partial_z^\ell\), where \(0\le m<\ell\) and \(1\le j<\ell\), whose monic eigenpolynomials satisfy a finite-term recurrence relation. Writing \(k=\ell-m\), such a recurrence exists if and only if \(j=1\) and \(k\mid\ell\). In that case we determine all recurrence coefficients in closed form and prove that the associated difference operator has order \(\ell\). We also give an explicit factorization showing that every admissible operator is of Type~(2) in Conjecture~1.10 of Horozov--Shapiro--Tater; the equality of the differential and difference orders is the conclusion predicted by their Conjecture~1.9.

</details>


### [50] [TENSKEL: A Combinatorial Observable Tensor for Structured Measurement and Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21999)
*Yvan Richard*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: TENSKEL is a combinatorial, tensor-based framework that explicitly models how latent discrete configurations are organized under structured measurement processes—via hierarchical aggregation/folding over binary ensembles—revealing observable partitions, combinatorial multiplicities, shell structures, and reconstruction geometry; it does not propose a new algorithm but provides a mathematical representation of observation-induced structure, with extensions to multinomial (simplex-supported) latent spaces and links to Pascal cellular automata and discrete color mappings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explicitly represent the observable organization induced by measurement processes in imaging and inverse problems—where latent configurations are often reconstructed from partial measurements without modeling how observation itself structures the latent space.

Method: Constructs a hierarchy of tensors over discrete domains from a binary latent ensemble using successive aggregation and folding operations; each measurement context induces an observable partition; the tensor formulation encodes combinatorial multiplicities, shell organization, degeneracies, and reconstruction geometry; similarity is captured via a tensor kernel based on measurement-context responses; reconstruction uses regularized inversion; extended naturally to multinomial latent representations on simplices.

Result: A mathematically rigorous, combinatorial observable framework (TENSKEL) that makes explicit the structural consequences of measurement on latent ensembles—including partitioning, multiplicity, symmetry, and geometry—enabling principled analysis of reconstruction feasibility, ambiguity, and structure; validated through connections to Pascal cellular automata (compressed realization) and structured discrete color mappings (multinomial realization).

Conclusion: TENSKEL establishes a foundational combinatorial language for reasoning about observation-induced structure in discrete inverse problems, bridging tensor algebra, discrete geometry, and measurement theory—with implications for computational imaging, structured sensing, and quantum-inspired measurement design.

Abstract: Many imaging problems seek to reconstruct underlying configurations from partial observable measurements. While reconstruction algorithms operate on these measurements, the observable organization induced by the measurement process is rarely represented explicitly.
  We introduce TENSKEL, a combinatorial observable framework for structured measurement and reconstruction based on tensor representations defined over discrete domains. Starting from a binary latent ensemble, the framework constructs a hierarchy of tensors coupling measurement contexts to a latent Pascal organization through successive aggregation and folding operations. Each measurement context induces an observable partition of the same latent ensemble, and the resulting tensor formulation makes explicit the associated combinatorial multiplicities, shell organization, degeneracies, and induced reconstruction geometry.
  Rather than introducing a new reconstruction algorithm, this framework provides a mathematical representation of how latent configurations become organized under observation. The induced tensor kernel characterizes similarities between latent coordinates through their measurement-context responses, while regularized inversion provides a structured reconstruction of the latent representation from observable measurements. The binary construction further admits a natural multinomial extension to discrete simplex-supported latent representations.
  Connections to Pascal cellular automata and structured discrete color mappings illustrate respectively compressed and multinomial realizations of the framework. More generally, TENSKEL provides a combinatorial basis for reasoning about the organization induced by observation, with potential relevance to computational imaging, inverse problems, structured sensing, and quantum-inspired measurement formulations.

</details>

TL;DR: This paper formulates interacting Bose--Hubbard dynamics on the Buchholz algebra in Fock space and establishes volume-uniform exponential bounds on local particle numbers—without assuming local moments—using a discrete functional integral under repulsive finite-range interactions and low-activity conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>

Method: Formulation of dynamics on the concrete Buchholz algebra; derivation of volume-uniform exponential local-number bounds via a discrete functional integral under repulsive finite-range interactions and low-activity gap condition.

Abstract: Interacting Bose--Hubbard dynamics is formulated on the concrete Buchholz algebra in Fock space. Under repulsive finite-range interactions and a low-activity gap, a discrete functional integral gives volume-uniform exponential local-number bounds at all temperatures. These bounds remove the local-moment hypothesis and yield finite-volume approximation and KMS accumulation states in arXiv:2505.13170.

</details>


### [52] [On the gauge invariant boundary condition in open XXZ chains: SoV bases, elementary blocks and overlaps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22365)
*G. Niccoli,V. Terras*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the open XXZ spin chain with unparallel boundary fields under a special gauge-invariant boundary condition at one end, enabling exact diagonalization via a generalized Separation of Variables (SoV) approach parameterized by α and β; it shows SoV bases depend only on α−β, constructs correlation function building blocks in the ungauged limit, and computes overlaps between eigenstates under modified boundary conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To solve the open XXZ spin chain with unparallel boundary fields—a nontrivial integrable model lacking standard Bethe ansatz applicability—by exploiting a special gauge-invariant structure of one boundary K-matrix to enable exact spectral and correlation analysis.

Method: Uses Vertex-IRF transformation to map the boundary problem into a tractable framework; applies generalized Sklyanin's Separation of Variables with two gauge parameters α (spectral shift) and β (dynamical parameter); proves α–β dependence of SoV bases; takes α−β→∞ limit to access ungauged regime; computes matrix elements and overlaps using SoV wavefunctions and scalar products.

Result: Demonstrates that SoV bases depend solely on α−β; derives explicit expressions for elementary correlation function building blocks in the ungauged limit; provides a systematic method to compute overlaps between eigenstates of models differing only in the boundary condition at site 1 while sharing the same condition at site N.

Conclusion: The gauge-invariant boundary condition enables full quantum integrability analysis of the open XXZ chain beyond standard approaches; the α−β reduction simplifies the SoV framework; the ungauged limit yields computable physical observables; overlap formulas extend applicability to quench and boundary perturbation problems.

Abstract: We consider the open XXZ spin chain with unparallel boundary fields, in the special case in which the Vertex-IRF transform of the K-matrix labelling the boundary field at site 1 is diagonal and invariant under the value of the gauge parameters, the other K-matrix being arbitrary. We discuss here several properties related to this special gauge invariant boundary condition. Such a model can be solved in the Vertex-IRF framework with arbitrary gauge parameters: we can diagonalise the transfer matrix by means of generalised Sklyanin's Separation of Variable (SoV) approach in a family of SoV bases that depend on two arbitrary gauge parameters $α$ and $β$, $β$ being the so-called dynamical parameter and $α$ an arbitrary shift of the spectral parameter. We show here that such SoV bases depend actually on $α$ and $β$ through the difference $α-β$ only. Considering the ungauged limit in which $α-β$ tends to infinity, we show how to compute all elementary building blocks for the correlation functions of this model. We finally show how to compute overlaps between eigenstates of this model and eigenstates of a spin chain sharing the same boundary condition at site $N$ and in which the gauge invariant boundary condition at site 1 has been changed to a more general one.

</details>
