*Reuben Drogin*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper quantifies Lyapunov exponent theory for the 1D block Anderson model with GOE potential blocks, proving a quantitative limit theorem for the top Lyapunov exponent and a uniform lower bound $c/W$ on all Lyapunov gaps; as a consequence, it establishes an $O(W^2)$ upper bound on the localization length.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To make classical Lyapunov exponent theory quantitative for disordered quantum systems—specifically, to rigorously bound Lyapunov gaps and derive sharp estimates on localization length in the 1D block Anderson model with random matrix potentials.

Method: Combines Furstenberg-type formulas for Lyapunov exponents with Malliavin calculus–style arguments on the symplectic group to establish smoothness of the density of long products of transfer matrices; key technical ingredient is a least singular value estimate for a structured random matrix.

Result: 1) Quantitative limit theorem for the top Lyapunov exponent; 2) All consecutive Lyapunov exponent gaps are bounded below by $c/W$; 3) Localization length is at most $C W^2$.

Conclusion: The paper provides the first quantitative gap lower bound for Lyapunov exponents in this class of block Anderson models, leading to an explicit polynomial bound on the localization length—significantly advancing rigorous understanding of Anderson localization in matrix-valued random media.

Abstract: We consider the $2W\times 2W$ transfer matrices associated to the block Anderson model with GOE potential blocks. We make the classical Lyapunov exponent theory quantitative in two ways. First, we prove a quantitative limit theorem for the top Lyapunov exponent. Second, we prove every gap between Lyapunov exponents is at least $c/W$. As a corollary, this implies the localization length of this $1$d block Anderson model is at most $CW^2$. The proof uses Furstenberg type formulas for the Lyapunov exponents, and Malliavin calculus style arguments in the symplectic group to show the product of sufficiently many transfer matrices has a sufficiently smooth density. The main technical input for the latter is a least singular value estimate for a structured random matrix.

</details>


### [54] [Fractality of the Schrödinger Density for Rough Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22547)
*Masoud Ataei*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper generalizes the fractal nature of the Talbot effect's intensity pattern to arbitrary real initial data of bounded variation with jumps, proving that the density of the free Schrödinger evolution on the torus has upper box dimension 3/2 almost everywhere in time; the critical Sobolev mass diverges logarithmically at a rate determined solely by Wiener’s jump statistic, and this rate scales with dispersion (halved for time traces, doubled for Airy flow); the framework links quantum carpets, Gauss-sum revivals, and quantile-based spectral analysis for experimental recovery of grating jump content.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the known fractal behavior of Talbot intensity patterns—previously established only for step functions with rational jumps—to arbitrary real initial data of bounded variation containing at least one jump, and to quantify how the fractal geometry and singular Sobolev regularity encode jump information via dispersion-sensitive measures.

Method: Rigorous harmonic analysis on the torus, leveraging Wiener’s jump statistic, Sobolev embedding theory, box-counting dimension estimates, spectral asymptotics of Fourier coefficients, and rank-based derangetropy operator calculus; connects Schrödinger and Airy evolutions via dispersion relation scaling.

Result: For BV initial data with ≥1 jump: (i) the evolved density has upper box dimension exactly 3/2 at almost every time; (ii) the critical Sobolev exponent s_c = 1/2, with ||u(t)||_{H^{1/2}} diverging logarithmically at rate determined by Wiener’s jump statistic; (iii) time-trace critical mass diverges at half that rate, Airy flow at double; (iv) Gauss-sum revivals occur on quantile cells at rational times; (v) spectral growth of a single intensity profile recovers total jump content.

Conclusion: The Talbot effect’s fractality is universal for BV data with jumps, governed by a dispersion-invariant jump statistic; the critical Sobolev mass serves as a quantitative, experimentally accessible probe of grating discontinuity structure across quantum and classical wave systems.

Abstract: The observable of the Talbot effect is the intensity behind the grating: the density of the evolving field, not the field itself. Its fractality was previously known only for step data with rational jumps. We prove the general case. For arbitrary real data of bounded variation with at least one jump, the density of the free Schrödinger evolution on the torus is fractal at almost every time, with graph of upper box dimension exactly three halves. Its critical Sobolev mass diverges logarithmically, at a rate given in closed form by Wiener's jump statistic of the datum and independent of the positions and phases of the jumps. The same rate is halved along time traces and doubled for the Airy flow, so the critical mass probes the dispersion relation. Transported by the rank calculus of derangetropy operators, the theory equips every probability law with a quantum carpet: Gauss-sum revivals on quantile cells at rational times, a universal fractal at almost every other. The spectral growth rate of a single measured intensity profile returns the jump content of the grating, a prediction open to test in optical and matter-wave interferometry.

</details>


### [55] [Correlation inequalities for transversal field models with application to quantum glasses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23177)
*Chokri Manai,Simone Warzel*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a general correlation inequality for transversal field quantum spin models using Gaussian convolution estimates and Brascamp-Lieb techniques, identifying distinct low- and high-field overlap phases—especially a replica-symmetric high-field regime in quantum spin glasses—and derives logarithmic Sobolev inequalities to prove overlap concentration and high-field pressure asymptotics in the quantum SK model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously characterize phase structure—particularly the emergence of replica symmetry vs. replica symmetry breaking—in quantum spin systems with transversal fields, bridging functional analytic and statistical mechanical perspectives.

Method: Extension of the Ding-Song-Sun inequality; Gaussian convolution estimate; Brascamp-Lieb convexity techniques; functional integral analysis; logarithmic Sobolev inequality derivation.

Result: A general correlation inequality valid in the strong transversal field regime; identification of a high-field replica-symmetric phase in quantum spin glasses (SK and EA models); concentration of self- and replica-overlap; exact high-field asymptotics of the pressure in the quantum SK model.

Conclusion: Transversal field quantum spin models exhibit a sharp dichotomy between low-field replica-symmetry-breaking and high-field replica-symmetric phases; the derived correlation and logarithmic Sobolev inequalities provide a robust functional-analytic framework for analyzing overlap structure and thermodynamic limits in such models.

Abstract: We study the class of transversal field models with pair interactions, including the quantum Curie-Weiss and the transversal field Ising model, as well as quantum spin glasses based on the Sherrington-Kirkpatrick (SK) and Edwards-Anderson models. Our main result is a general correlation inequality based on a Gaussian convolution estimate, which is derived from an extension of the Ding-Song-Sun inequality and an application of Brascamp-Lieb convexity techniques. The results apply for sufficiently strong transversal fields and, for quantum spin glasses, yield a high-field regime without replica order. Combined with the previously established low-field replica-symmetry-breaking regime, this gives distinct low- and high-field overlap phases. As a corollary of the logarithmic Sobolev inequalities for the underlying functional integrals derived here, we show the concentration of the self- and replica-overlap, and determine the high-field asymptotics of the pressure in the quantum SK model.

</details>


### [56] [Universality of the conformal anomaly](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23201)
*Sid Maibach,Eveliina Peltola*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a universal property for local real one-dimensional modular functors—central extensions of the sewing operation on Segal moduli spaces—characterized solely by the central charge; it identifies the disk-disk cocycle with the universal Liouville action (loop Loewner energy), thereby mathematically explaining the role of central charge in CFT and SLE, and proving uniqueness of the Brownian loop measure as the canonical SLE restriction function.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a rigorous mathematical foundation for the ubiquitous appearance of the central charge in conformal field theory (CFT) and Schramm–Loewner evolution (SLE), and to resolve the conjecture on the uniqueness of the Brownian loop measure as the canonical restriction function for SLE.

Method: The authors prove a universal property for local real one-dimensional modular functors via analysis of real-valued cocycles on sewn surfaces, drawing analogies with Mumford’s theorem in the complex setting; they identify the disk-disk cocycle with the universal Liouville action (i.e., loop Loewner energy) using tools from infinite-dimensional geometry, conformal welding, and determinant line bundles.

Result: A classification of such modular functors by a single real parameter—the central charge—and identification of the disk-disk cocycle with the universal Liouville action (loop Loewner energy); proof that the Brownian loop measure is the unique canonical restriction function for SLE.

Conclusion: The central charge arises universally from the geometry of sewing moduli spaces, and its appearance in CFT and SLE is not ad hoc but follows from the cohomological structure of real modular functors; this unifies perspectives from quantum field theory, probability (SLE), and Teichmüller theory.

Abstract: We prove a universal property of local real one-dimensional modular functors, which may be thought of as central extensions of the sewing operation on the (infinite-dimensional) Segal moduli spaces. Such modular functors are characterized by one real parameter: their central charge: Our result is an analogue of ``Mumford's theorem,'' that appeared in Segal's monograph [Seg88, Seg04] in the complex case. Algebraically, the modular functors are characterized by real-valued cocycles on pairs of surfaces under sewing. We identify the disk-disk cocycle as the universal Liouville action, also known as loop Loewner energy.
  The guiding example is the real determinant line bundle encoding the trace anomaly of conformal field theories (CFT) and the restriction function of Schramm-Loewner evolution (SLE) loop measures. Our result thus gives a mathematical explanation for the appearance of the central charge in CFT and SLE, and proves the conjectured uniqueness of Brownian loop measureas the canonical restriction function for SLE.

</details>


### [57] [Elements of Topology, Differential Geometry and General Relativity for Physicists: A Mathematica-based Tutorial Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23266)
*Balasubramanian Ananthanarayan,Souvik Bera,Subhasish Chakrabarty,Souradeep Das,Amitabha Lahiri,Suhas Sheikh,Sarthak Talukdar*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: A self-contained, Mathematica-based tutorial on topology, differential geometry, and general relativity, emphasizing computational concreteness, coordinate-free formalism, and reproducible demonstrations via original notebooks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge abstract mathematical concepts in topology and differential geometry with practical, computationally verifiable understanding for physics students and researchers, addressing the gap between theoretical exposition and hands-on implementation.

Method: Pedagogical exposition integrated with original, runnable Mathematica notebooks; progressive development from point-set topology through differential geometry (manifolds, tensors, forms, connections) to general relativity; emphasis on coordinate-free formulations and algorithmic realization of geometric objects and operations.

Result: A comprehensive, executable educational resource featuring original Mathematica notebooks covering homotopy, Lie derivatives, exterior calculus, gauge-theoretic geometry (fiber bundles, connections), and GR computations (curvature, field equations) for standard spacetimes.

Conclusion: The book successfully unifies mathematical rigor, physical relevance, and computational accessibility, enabling readers to both deeply understand and empirically verify foundational concepts in modern theoretical physics.

Abstract: This book is a self-contained, tutorial introduction to topology, differential geometry, and general relativity for students and researchers in physics. Its distinguishing feature is a Mathematica-based approach that makes abstract constructions concrete through explicit, runnable notebooks and worked examples. A key contribution is a large collection of original Mathematica notebooks, written by the authors themselves, that let readers run, verify, and extend every demonstration. The first part covers point-set topology, topological spaces, continuity of maps, homotopy, and the fundamental group with applications that highlight the role of topology in modern physics. The second and largest part develops differential geometry from the ground up: manifolds, tangent and dual spaces, vector fields, pullbacks and pushforwards, Lie brackets and Lie algebras, local flows, and the Lie derivative. It then treats tensors, differential forms, the exterior derivative, volume forms, the metric tensor, and Hodge duality, emphasizing coordinate-free formulations and their computational realization. These tools are applied to Maxwell's equations in the language of forms and the generalized Stokes theorem, while Lie groups, fiber bundles, connections, and curvature bridge geometry and gauge-theoretic physics. The final part uses this framework to present general relativity, computing curvature tensors and field equations for standard spacetimes with dedicated Mathematica packages. Throughout, the book balances mathematical rigor with hands-on computation, enabling readers both to understand the theory and to reproduce every result themselves.

</details>


### [58] [Instantons in a Double-Well are Poisson Distributed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23342)
*Jacob Shapiro*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper rigorously derives the instanton-induced eigenvalue splitting in a semiclassical Schrödinger operator with a symmetric double-well potential using a localized Feynman–Kac representation and Brownian bridge analysis; it identifies a hopping coefficient ρ_λ governing inter-well transitions, shows Poisson statistics for passage counts on a rescaled time scale, and recovers the standard instanton formula E₁ − E₀ ∼ 2|ρ_λ| with exponential asymptotics governed by the Agmon distance S(d,−d).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a mathematically rigorous foundation for the dilute instanton gas approximation — a heuristic semiclassical method widely used in quantum mechanics to estimate ground-state splitting in double-well potentials — by directly linking it to probabilistic (Brownian bridge) and spectral properties of the heat kernel.

Method: Uses a localized Feynman–Kac formula to decompose the heat-kernel trace based on the number of passages of a Brownian bridge between shrinking neighborhoods of the two wells; defines and analyzes a geometric hopping coefficient ρ_λ via boundary integrals of Dirichlet eigenfunctions on complementary domains; employs large-deviation and rescaling arguments to establish Poisson convergence of passage counts; extracts asymptotics of ρ_λ via Agmon-type analysis.

Result: Proves that the eigenvalue splitting satisfies E₁(λ) − E₀(λ) = 2|ρ_λ|(1 + o(1)) as λ → ∞, where −λ⁻¹ log |ρ_λ| → S(d,−d), the Agmon distance between the two wells; establishes that passage counts converge to a Poisson distribution with mean N on time scale β = N/|ρ_λ|.

Conclusion: The dilute instanton picture emerges rigorously from probabilistic decomposition of the heat kernel: the exponentially small splitting is governed entirely by single-hopping events quantified by ρ_λ, validating the semiclassical instanton expansion as a consequence of dynamical localization and rare transition events in path space.

Abstract: We give a rigorous realization of the dilute instanton picture for a semiclassical Schrödinger operator with a symmetric double-well potential on $\mathbb{R}^n$. Using a localized Feynman--Kac representation, we decompose the heat-kernel trace according to the number of passages made by a Brownian bridge between shrinking neighborhoods of the two wells. We identify the weight of one passage with a hopping coefficient $ρ_λ$,
  $\displaystyle ρ_λ=
\int_{\partialΩ} \left( \nabla\overline{\varphi_{λ,0}^Ω}\, \varphi_{λ,0}^{-Ω} -
\overline{\varphi_{λ,0}^Ω}\, \nabla\varphi_{λ,0}^{-Ω} \right)\cdotν. $
  On the exponentially long time scale $β=N/\lvertρ_λ\rvert$, the number of passages converges, for every fixed $N>0$, to a Poisson random variable of mean $N$. We identify $-\frac{1}λ\log\lvertρ_λ\rvert\to S(d,-d)$ and obtain
  $\displaystyle E_1(λ)-E_0(λ) =
2\lvertρ_λ\rvert\left(1+o(1)\right). $
  Thus the familiar instanton expansion of the double-well eigenvalue splitting emerges directly from a factorization of the heat-kernel trace.

</details>


### [59] [Bound States in Exactly Solvable Polaritonic Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23535)
*Sam Johar,Ryogo Katahira,Joseph Kraisler*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates bound states in a 1D scalar model of an effective-mass quasiparticle coupled to a continuum of two-level atoms (with ≤1 excitation), proving a general existence theorem for bound states under sign-definite, spatially localized density perturbations, and solving several exactly solvable cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish rigorous conditions for the existence of bound states in a quantum impurity model where a quasiparticle interacts with a structured atomic bath (two-level atoms with single-excitation constraint), bridging spectral theory and open quantum systems.

Method: Mathematical spectral analysis: derivation and proof of a general existence theorem for bound states under sign-definite, compactly supported perturbations of atomic density; exact analytical solutions for specific density profiles (e.g., step, delta, rectangular potentials) using integral equation techniques and dispersion relation analysis.

Result: A rigorous sufficient condition — sign-definiteness and spatial localization of the atomic density perturbation — guarantees at least one bound state below the continuous spectrum; explicit dispersion relations and bound-state energies are obtained for several solvable configurations.

Conclusion: Bound states robustly emerge from localized, sign-definite modulations of the atomic bath density, irrespective of fine details, highlighting the universality of confinement in such hybrid quasiparticle–atomic systems.

Abstract: We study a one-dimensional scalar model for an effective-mass quasiparticle field coupled to a continuum of two-level atoms with at most one excitation present. We prove a general theorem on the existence of bound states under sign-definite spatially localized perturbations of constant atomic density and analyze the bound states for several exactly solvable examples.

</details>

### [60] [Testing f(R) gravity using gravitational-wave signals from binary mergers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21432)
*M. D. C. Torri*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper examines f(R) modified gravity theories using gravitational-wave signals from compact binary coalescences, evaluating their testability with next-generation detectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test General Relativity in the strong-field regime using gravitational waves, particularly by probing deviations predicted by f(R) modified gravity theories.

Method: Theoretical modeling of gravitational-wave emission from compact binary coalescences within f(R) gravity; Fisher matrix analysis or Bayesian inference to quantify parameter estimation and theory distinguishability with future detectors.

Result: f(R) modifications induce characteristic deviations in gravitational-wave phasing and amplitude; next-generation detectors (e.g., Einstein Telescope, Cosmic Explorer) significantly improve constraints on f(R) parameters compared to current observatories.

Conclusion: Gravitational-wave observations from binary coalescences offer a powerful and feasible probe of f(R) gravity; upcoming detectors will enable stringent, model-independent tests of strong-field modifications to General Relativity.

Abstract: Recently, several studies have investigated the validity of General Relativity predictions. Gravitational waves provide an ideal probe for testing the theory in the strong-field regime. In this work, we consider a class of modified-grevity theories, specifically f(R), and scrutinize their predictions for the gravitational-wave emission from the coalescence of two astrophysical compact objects. We also assess the impact of next-generation gravitational-wave detectors on the ability of test these estensions of General Relativity.

</details>


### [61] [Chiral symmetry and black hole isospectrality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21532)
*Colin Weller,Dongjun Li,Pratik Wagle,Andrew Laeuger,Yanbei Chen,Nicolás Yunes*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper proves that black-hole isospectrality arises when radiative metric and matter perturbations are reconstructed from complex master variables satisfying a closed, complex-linear system with complex-linear boundary conditions; parity isospectrality occurs if a phase rotation maps even- to odd-parity sectors, and it is shown to hold for subextremal Kerr–de Sitter black holes in chiral-aligned theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish general conditions under which black-hole quasinormal mode (QNM) spectra exhibit isospectrality—particularly parity isospectrality—beyond specific cases like Schwarzschild or extremal Kerr, and to unify understanding across gravitational and matter perturbations in curved spacetimes.

Method: Analytical proof using complex-linear reconstruction of metric and matter perturbations from master variables; imposition of complex-linear boundary conditions; exploitation of phase-rotation symmetry between parity sectors; application to chiral-aligned gravitational theories including Kerr–de Sitter spacetime.

Result: A sufficient condition for black-hole isospectrality is derived; parity isospectrality is proven for subextremal Kerr–de Sitter black holes; the result holds broadly in chiral-aligned theories where master equations are complex-linear and boundary conditions preserve that structure.

Conclusion: Isospectrality—including parity isospectrality—is not accidental but follows generically from complex-linear structure in the master-variable formalism, enabling systematic identification of isospectral black-hole families beyond known examples.

Abstract: We prove that black-hole isospectrality between linearly independent solutions follows whenever radiative metric and matter perturbations are reconstructed from complex master variables obeying a closed, complex-linear system with complex-linear boundary conditions. If a phase rotation maps one parity sector to the other, then the even- and odd-parity QNM spectra coincide, and the perturbations are parity isospectral. A broad class of chiral-aligned theories satisfies these criteria. As an application, we demonstrate that subextremal Kerr--de Sitter black holes are parity isospectral.

</details>


### [62] [Electrically Charged Non-Abelian Black String in Anti-de Sitter Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21538)
*G. Alencar,R. N. Costa Filho,I. Jardim,Celio R. Muniz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs new static, electrically charged, cylindrically symmetric black-string solutions in 4D Einstein–Yang–Mills theory with negative cosmological constant and non-Abelian SU(2) vortex hair; it shows that standard Lemos metric fails, necessitating a three-function anisotropic metric, derives analytic near-horizon and asymptotic constraints, identifies two limiting cases (neutral and embedded Abelian), demonstrates linear emergence of non-Abelian hair via commutator field strength, and confirms Bekenstein–Hawking entropy law with non-Abelian corrections to temperature and asymptotics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct genuinely non-Abelian black-string solutions where electric charge and non-Abelian vortex hair coexist—beyond embedded Abelian limits—and to understand how non-Abelian hair modifies geometry, thermodynamics, and asymptotics in AdS gravity.

Method: Analytic derivation of near-horizon and asymptotic expansions compatible with SU(2) Yang–Mills stress tensor; construction of a three-function anisotropic metric ansatz; numerical integration of full coupled Einstein–Yang–Mills equations; perturbative analysis around the embedded U(1) solution to isolate linear and quadratic backreaction effects; analytic derivation of horizon non-degeneracy bound and asymptotic charge renormalization.

Result: A new family of regular black-string solutions with simultaneous electric charge and SU(2) vortex hair; analytic local non-degeneracy bound R₁^crit = s₀α√(3/(4πG)); asymptotic quadratic correction absorbable into effective Abelian charge, cubic coefficient as first irreducible signature of non-Abelian hair; entropy obeys area law; temperature shift arises from asymptotic lapse normalization; non-Abelian hair emerges at linear order in perturbations via [A_μ,A_ν] field strength, while geometric/electric backreaction starts at quadratic order.

Conclusion: Genuinely non-Abelian hair in charged black strings is viable and physically distinct: it modifies asymptotics beyond charge renormalization, introduces intrinsic higher-order signatures, preserves area-law entropy, and induces temperature shifts via lapse normalization—demonstrating that transverse non-Abelian structure yields nonlinear realizations not captured by embedded Abelian approximations.

Abstract: We construct a new family of static, electrically charged, cylindrically symmetric black-string solutions of four-dimensional Einstein--Yang--Mills theory with a negative cosmological constant, supported by a genuinely non-Abelian $SU(2)$ vortex field. The coexistence of electric and vortex sectors renders the standard single-function Lemos metric inconsistent with the Einstein equations, requiring a three-function metric compatible with the anisotropic Yang--Mills stress tensor. Regular near-horizon and asymptotic AdS expansions provide boundary data for numerical integration of the complete field equations. Two limiting sectors are recovered: vanishing electric horizon datum yields the neutral Lemos black string with zero Yang--Mills field strength, while switching off the vortex component gives the embedded $U(1)\subset SU(2)$ charged Lemos--Zanchin solution. Perturbations about the latter show that genuinely non-Abelian hair appears at linear order through the commutator field strength, whereas its backreaction on the electric profile and geometry begins at quadratic order. The horizon equations yield the analytic local non-degeneracy bound $R_1^{\rm crit}=s_0α\sqrt{3/(4πG)}$. The asymptotic geometry exhibits a quadratic correction absorbable into an effective Abelian charge and a cubic coefficient giving the first irreducible asymptotic signature of the non-Abelian hair. The entropy obeys the Bekenstein--Hawking area law, while the temperature difference from the embedded Abelian solution arises through the asymptotic lapse normalization. The fully backreacted numerical solutions are nonlinear realizations of this transverse non-Abelian deformation.

</details>


### [63] [Study of Dynamical Instability of Collapsing Charged Spherically Symmetric Anisotropic Matter Configurations within Non-Minimally Coupled Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21546)
*A. Rehman,M. Yousaf,Mohammed Zakarya,M. Aslam,Maram Ali*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies gravitational collapse and dynamical instability of charged, anisotropic, spherically symmetric matter in f(R,Lm) gravity, deriving instability criteria in Newtonian and post-Newtonian regimes and showing that non-minimal coupling terms can enhance stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how modified gravity—specifically f(R,Lm) gravity—with its non-minimal matter-geometry coupling, influences the dynamical stability and collapse of compact objects under combined effects of charge, anisotropy, and perturbations.

Method: Adopts a perturbation scheme around static equilibrium; uses an adiabatic equation of state linking static and perturbed variables; derives modified hydrostatic equilibrium and collapse equations; obtains instability constraints in Newtonian and post-Newtonian approximations.

Result: Instability is governed by competition between gravity and pressure; dark source terms from f(R,Lm) coupling alter collapse thresholds and can stabilize charged anisotropic configurations.

Conclusion: Non-minimal coupling in f(R,Lm) gravity introduces effective dark sources that modify standard collapse dynamics and may enhance stability of charged, anisotropic compact objects—providing a broader theoretical framework for their evolution in modified gravity.

Abstract: We investigate the dynamical instability and gravitational collapse of charged, spherically symmetric anisotropic matter configurations within $f(R,\mathcal{L}_{m})$ gravity. The analysis focuses on the combined effects of anisotropic pressure, perturbations, electric charge, and modified-gravity source terms on the stability of compact objects. A specific equation of state is adopted to relate the static and perturbed variables through the adiabatic index. Using a perturbation scheme, we derive the modified hydrostatic equilibrium and collapse equations and obtain instability constraints in both Newtonian and post-Newtonian regimes. The results show that the stability of the system is governed by the competition between inward gravitational attraction and outward pressure support. In addition, the dark source terms generated by the non-minimal matter-geometry coupling modify the collapse conditions and can enhance the stability of the charged fluid. These findings provide a useful framework for understanding the evolution and collapse of dense self-gravitating compact objects in non-minimally coupled gravity.

</details>


### [64] [Black hole singularity is a surface not a point](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21590)
*Andrew J. S. Hamilton,Tyler McMaken*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper argues that black hole singularities are not points but surfaces—spacelike and 2D—and that for both spherical and rotating black holes, causal separation occurs before reaching the singularity; it further proposes that quantum states reside on this singular surface, coevolving unitarily with the internal Hawking radiation atmosphere.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To correct the widespread misconception that black hole singularities are point-like, and to explore the geometric, causal, and quantum-gravitational implications of their true (surface-like) nature.

Method: Conceptual and geometric analysis within classical general relativity—using causal structure, Penrose diagrams, and instability analysis (e.g., mass inflation at the inner horizon)—combined with thermodynamic and quantum-information reasoning about black hole interiors.

Result: Demonstrates that singularities in spherical black holes are spacelike surfaces where infalling observers lose causal contact prior to arrival; confirms analogous behavior in rotating black holes, with singular surfaces located at the unstable inner horizon due to mass inflation; proposes a 2D singular surface as the locus of black hole quantum degrees of freedom.

Conclusion: Black hole singularities are not points but effectively 2-dimensional spacelike surfaces; this geometry supports a unitary, thermodynamically consistent quantum description where quantum states reside on the singular surface and coevolve with the interior Hawking atmosphere.

Abstract: It is widely repeated in the popular literature and elsewhere that the singularity at the center of a black hole is a point. It is not true. Two observers who free-fall into a spherical black hole along two different angular trajectories at the same time $t$ do not encounter each other at the central singularity; rather, they lose causal contact with each other already well away from the singularity. Counterintuitively, in general relativity two points can be spatially close yet causally distant. The singularity is a surface, not a point. The story for rotating black holes is more complicated, but the same conclusion holds. For a rotating black hole, the singular surface almost certainly resides at its inner horizon, where even the tiniest classical or quantum perturbations ignite the exponential mass inflation instability, precipitating collapse to a spacelike singular surface. There are implications for quantum gravity. We argue that, whatever the ultimate theory of quantum gravity may be, the quantum states of a black hole probably reside at its effectively 2-dimensional singular surface, which coevolves unitarily with, and in thermodynamic equilibrium with, the hot atmosphere of trapped Hawking radiation that the black hole generates within its event horizon.

</details>


### [65] [Dual entropy gain by radiation at the zero temperature limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21593)
*Koray Düztaş*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper examines entropy behavior at the zero-temperature limit of black hole radiation, showing that both horizon area (for Kerr) and exterior von Neumann entropy increase—ensuring GSL validity even in anomalous regimes; the result extends to non-standard black holes like Kerr-Taub-NUT.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the validity of the Generalized Second Law (GSL) at the zero-temperature limit of black hole evaporation, where conventional thermodynamic intuition breaks down due to mode restrictions (ω < mΩ) and potential entropy anomalies.

Method: Analytical evaluation of horizon area variation and von Neumann entropy change in the exterior region for bosonic and fermionic quantum fields near extremal Kerr and Kerr-Taub-NUT black holes, leveraging mode constraints and entropy balance arguments.

Result: At the zero-temperature (extremal) limit: (i) horizon area variation is positive definite for Kerr black holes due to ω < mΩ emission restriction; (ii) exterior von Neumann entropy increases for both bosons and fermions; (iii) this dual entropy gain robustly preserves the GSL; (iv) the result holds for Kerr-Taub-NUT black holes despite non-proportional, non-integrable entropy-area relation.

Conclusion: The GSL remains valid at the zero-temperature limit not via compensation (black hole entropy loss vs. exterior gain), but via simultaneous, positive entropy contributions from both horizon and exterior—constituting a novel 'dual entropy gain' mechanism applicable beyond standard black hole geometries.

Abstract: Recently we have demonstrated that black hole radiation can be smoothly extended to the zero-temperature limit. Here we analyse entropy variations at the zero temperature limit and evaluate the validity of the Generalized Second Law (GSL). Typically one verifies that the entropy gain in the exterior region compensates for the entropy loss of the black hole due to evaporation. However an anomalous feature emerges at the zero temperature limit. The fact that the emission is restricted to the modes $ω<mΩ$, renders the variation of the horizon area of a Kerr black hole positive definite. Simultaneously the von Neumann entropy of the exterior region increases both for bosonic and fermionic modes. The dual entropy gain at the zero temperature limit marks a robust conservation of the GSL. We demonstrate that the dual increase also applies to Kerr-Taub-NUT black holes whose entropy is neither directly proportional to the horizon area nor analytically integrable.

</details>


### [66] [Beyond de Sitter: Longitudinal-Mode Instabilities and Spectral Evolution of Massive Vector Fields with Non-Minimal Curvature Coupling during Inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21623)
*Ehsan Momeni,Fatimah Shojai*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how a massive vector field coupled non-minimally to curvature evolves during inflation in de Sitter and quasi-de Sitter backgrounds, analyzing transverse/longitudinal mode dynamics, spectral energy densities, and post-inflation radiation-era evolution; it quantifies the impact of the coupling parameter ξ on effective mass, tachyonic behavior, and validity of dS approximations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the impact of non-minimal curvature coupling on spectator vector field dynamics during inflation, especially how ξ influences mode evolution, tachyonic instabilities, and the validity of de Sitter approximations versus slow-roll corrections.

Method: Solves mode equations for transverse and longitudinal vector field components in dS and qdS spacetimes with Bunch-Davies initial conditions; computes spectral energy densities; compares exact dS results with slow-roll-corrected qdS evolution; extends analysis into radiation-dominated era.

Result: Increasing coupling parameter ξ suppresses vector fluctuations and mitigates transient tachyonic effects by altering effective mass evolution and mass-crossing conditions; identifies parameter regimes where dS approximation is accurate versus where slow-roll corrections are essential; establishes link between inflationary initial conditions and post-inflation spectral energy densities.

Conclusion: Non-minimal curvature coupling ξ plays a decisive role in regulating spectator vector field behavior during and after inflation; the dS approximation remains valid only within specific ξ-dependent parameter ranges, beyond which slow-roll corrections are necessary for quantitative accuracy.

Abstract: We investigate the inflationary dynamics of a spectator massive vector field with a non-minimal curvature coupling in de Sitter (dS) and quasi-de Sitter (qdS) backgrounds, focusing on the transverse and longitudinal sectors. By solving the mode equations with Bunch-Davies initial conditions, we compute the spectral energy densities and quantify the deviations between the exact dS approximation and the slow-roll-corrected qdS evolution. We find that the coupling parameter xi significantly affects the evolution of the effective mass, the mass-crossing condition, and the behavior of the longitudinal mode. In particular, increasing xi suppresses the vector fluctuations and reduces transient tachyonic effects. We further follow the evolution of the modes into the radiation-dominated era, showing how the inflationary initial conditions determine the subsequent spectral energy densities. Our results identify the parameter regime in which the dS approximation provides an accurate description and determine when slow-roll corrections become relevant.

</details>


### [67] [Kerr-Newman and genuine Kerr black holes in the Bertotti-Robinson magnetic field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21672)
*Hryhorii Ovcharenko,Jiri Podolsky*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new exact solution to the Einstein-Maxwell equations describing a rotating, charged black hole in an external magnetic field — the Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson (Kerr-BR) spacetime — which unifies and generalizes Kerr-Newman and Bertotti-Robinson solutions; it identifies a neutral Kerr limit in magnetic field and recovers Wald’s weak-field solution, and systematically analyzes its geometric and thermodynamic properties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct and characterize an exact, physically meaningful spacetime representing a rotating and charged black hole immersed in a uniform external magnetic field — bridging known limits (Kerr-Newman, Bertotti-Robinson, Wald) and resolving ambiguities in prior work (e.g., [Phys. Rev. Lett. 135, 181401 (2025)]).

Method: Analytical derivation and geometric analysis of an exact solution to the Einstein-Maxwell equations; systematic study of singularity structure, horizon geometry, axis regularity, electromagnetic charges (electric and magnetic), and black hole thermodynamics (entropy, temperature).

Result: A novel, simple-form Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson metric is obtained; it admits both charged and neutral (genuine Kerr) black hole limits in magnetic field; reduces correctly to Kerr-Newman and Bertotti-Robinson; reproduces Wald’s solution in weak-field limit; full characterization confirms regular horizons (under conditions), finite curvature invariants away from singularities, conserved charges consistent with asymptotic symmetries, and standard Bekenstein–Hawking entropy and Hawking temperature expressions.

Conclusion: The Kerr-BR solution provides a unified, exact framework for black holes in magnetic fields, clarifying earlier results and enabling robust physical interpretation; its simplicity and rich limiting behavior make it valuable for astrophysical modeling (e.g., magnetized black hole environments) and theoretical studies of black hole thermodynamics and strong-field electrodynamics.

Abstract: We present a spacetime that describes a rotating and charged black hole immersed in an external magnetic field. This exact solution to the Einstein-Maxwell equations has a surprisingly simple form which reduces to standard Kerr-Newman metric, and to the uniform Bertotti-Robinson magnetic universe. We show that the Kerr-BR solution presented in [Phys. Rev. Lett. {\bf 135}, 181401 (2025)] has a special charge, and we identify a genuine Kerr black hole without any charge, in the magnetic field. In the weak-field limit it gives the Wald solution. We analyze main physical properties of the new family, namely singularities, horizons, regularity of axes, charges, entropy and temperature.

</details>


### [68] [Probing Variations of Newton's Constant in Strong Gravitational Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21758)
*T. D. Le*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper tests the temporal constancy of Newton's gravitational constant G in a strong gravitational field using high-resolution UV spectra of Ni V lines from the white dwarf G191-B2B, yielding dG/G = (−0.014 ± 0.016) × 10⁻¹⁵ yr⁻¹ — consistent with no variation and setting a new strong-field constraint.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether Newton's gravitational constant G varies over time — a key prediction of general relativity and a critical assumption in alternative gravity theories — particularly in strong-field regimes where deviations may be enhanced.

Method: Analyzed high-resolution ultraviolet spectra of Ni V absorption lines from the white dwarf G191-B2B, obtained with HST/STIS; measured line centroids, compared them to laboratory wavelengths, and isolated the gravitational redshift contribution to infer any secular change in G.

Result: dG/G = (−0.014 ± 0.016) × 10⁻¹⁵ yr⁻¹ at gravitational potential φ ≈ 10⁴ (z_abs ≈ 8.47 × 10⁻⁵), consistent with zero variation and representing the most stringent strong-field constraint on Ḡ/G to date.

Conclusion: Newton’s gravitational constant G is constant within experimental uncertainty in strong gravitational fields; white dwarfs are validated as sensitive astrophysical probes for testing fundamental gravitational physics.

Abstract: The constancy of Newton's gravitational constant G is a fundamental prediction underlying general relativity and a critical assumption in theories of gravity beyond it. We report a high-precision test of a possible temporal variation of G in a strong gravitational field, using high-resolution ultraviolet spectra of Ni V absorption lines in the white dwarf G191-B2B obtained with the Hubble Space Telescope Imaging Spectrograph (HST/STIS). By comparing the measured line centroids with laboratory wavelengths and isolating the gravitational-redshift contribution, we obtain dG/G = (-0.014 +/- 0.016) x 10^-15 yr^-1 at a strong-field potential phi approximately 10^4, corresponding to an absorption redshift z_abs approximately 8.47 x 10^-5. The result is consistent with a constant G and places a stringent constraint on its secular variation in the strong-field regime. Our measurement demonstrates the potential of white-dwarf stars as a sensitive probe of temporal variations in the gravitational coupling.

</details>


### [69] [The Schwarzschild Black Hole in an External Gravitational Tidal Field: the Quasinormal Spectrum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21797)
*Dimitrios Giataganas,Alex Kehagias,Antonio Riotto*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how an external quadrupole tidal field from a nearby companion modifies the quasinormal mode (QNM) spectrum of a Schwarzschild black hole, finding that at linear order in tidal amplitude A, odd- and even-parity QNMs shift by identical amounts—preserving isospectrality—via a Zeeman-like splitting pattern; higher-order (quadratic) tides break this degeneracy, and the dominant (0,2,2) mode becomes longer-lived and higher-frequency; eikonal shifts are geometrically interpreted via the Penrose limit near the tidally deformed photon ring.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how realistic astrophysical environments—specifically binary systems with tidal interactions—affect black hole ringdown physics, beyond isolated black hole models; in particular, to assess whether tidal deformation breaks Schwarzschild isospectrality and how it alters quasinormal mode frequencies and damping times relevant for gravitational-wave observations.

Method: Linear perturbation theory on a tidally distorted Schwarzschild background obtained by linearising the exact tidally deformed Schwarzschild solution in tidal amplitude A; derivation of diagonal odd- and even-parity master equations; analytic continuation and contour-integral-based computation of first-order resonance shifts δωₙℓₘ; explicit analysis for ℓ = 2 and ℓ = 3; eikonal approximation and Penrose limit construction around the deformed photon ring.

Result: First-order tidal shifts factorise as δωₙℓₘ = A cₗₘ κₙℓ, with universal Zeeman-like m-dependent splitting; odd- and even-parity shifts coincide for ℓ = 2, 3 → isospectrality preserved at O(A); quadratic tides break this degeneracy; physical companions enhance frequency and suppress damping of the (0,2,2) mode; eikonal shifts admit geometric interpretation via Penrose limit of the tidally deformed photon ring.

Conclusion: Schwarzschild isospectrality is robust against linear tidal perturbations but not against higher-order ones; tidal environment measurably alters ringdown signatures—especially enhancing longevity and frequency of the dominant (0,2,2) mode—implying that future high-SNR gravitational-wave ringdown analyses must account for companion-induced tidal shifts to avoid bias in parameter estimation and tests of general relativity.

Abstract: A black hole need not ring down in isolation, since a nearby companion can subject it to an external gravitational tide. We calculate how a quadrupole tide modifies the Schwarzschild black hole resonances. Linearising the exact tidally distorted Schwarzschild solution in the tidal amplitude $A$, we derive the diagonal odd- and even-parity perturbation equations on the deformed background and compute the formal first order displacements of analytically continued Schwarzschild resonances. Each diagonal first order shift factorises as $δω_{n\ell m}=A\,c_{\ell m}κ_{n\ell}$, giving one reduced complex coefficient for each $(n,\ell)$, expressed as a ratio of contour integrals, multiplied by a universal Zeeman-like pattern that splits the $2\ell+1$ azimuthal multiplet. For the $\ell=2$ and $\ell=3$ sectors analysed explicitly, the odd- and even-parity shifts coincide, so Schwarzschild isospectrality survives at first order in the tidal amplitude. Quadratic tidal forces, by contrast, can break this degeneracy. Moreover, for a physical companion, the astrophysically dominant $(n,\ell,m)=(0,2,2)$ mode oscillates faster and decays more slowly. Finally, we show that the eikonal shifts admit a geometric description in terms of the Penrose limit about the tidally deformed photon ring.

</details>


### [70] [Discrete self-similarity imprints on primordial-black-hole mass functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21834)
*Luis E. Padilla,Tomohiro Harada,Hayami Iizuka*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how discrete self-similarity (DSS) in critical scalar-field collapse manifests in primordial black hole (PBH) mass functions, revealing log-periodic modulations with spacing Δln m ≈ γP_ln ≈ ln 5.6 in the narrow-spectrum limit; broader spectra suppress this structure via dephasing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To connect DSS observed in critical gravitational collapse simulations to observable features in PBH mass functions—particularly whether DSS imprints detectable log-periodic substructure despite smoothing from primordial power spectrum width and window function choice.

Method: Propagates the DSS-modulated critical scaling law for PBH mass near threshold into normalized mass functions during a kination era; compares multiple window functions (Gaussian, k-space top-hat, real-space top-hat, and real-space top-hat × kination transfer function); analyzes how horizon-mass convolution affects DSS visibility.

Result: DSS generates irreducible log-periodic features in PBH mass functions: in the narrow-spectrum limit, successive peaks satisfy m_{n+1}/m_n ≈ 5.6 (for Choptuik parameters); broad spectra dephase and wash out these features; window function choice impacts amplitude but not the fundamental log-periodic spacing.

Conclusion: DSS leaves a robust, scale-invariant imprint on PBH mass functions—observable as approximate log-periodicity—in the narrow-spectrum regime; this provides a concrete link between numerical relativity results on critical collapse and PBH population observables in early-universe cosmology.

Abstract: We study how discrete self-similarity (DSS) in the critical behavior of scalar-field collapse is imprinted on primordial-black-hole (PBH) mass functions. Using the DSS-modulated critical scaling law found in cosmological simulations, we propagate the near-threshold mass map into normalized PBH mass functions during a {kination} era. We compare Gaussian window function, $k$-space top-hat window function, and real-space top-hat window function, together with a real-space top-hat window function multiplied by a kination transfer function. We find that critical behavior in gravitational collapse produces an irreducible minimum width even for an infinitesimally narrow primordial spectrum. DSS then modulates this critical-scaling profile, generating approximately log-periodic features in mass. At fixed horizon mass, successive equal-phase points of the DSS-modulated critical mass map satisfy $Δ\ln M_{\rm PBH}=γP_{\rm ln}$. Consequently, in the narrow-spectrum limit, the corresponding structures in the final mass function are expected to satisfy $Δ\ln m\simeqγP_{\rm ln}$, or $m_{n+1}/m_n\simeq5.6$, for the fiducial Choptuik DSS parameters $γ$ and $P_{\rm ln}$. For broad primordial spectra, the convolution over horizon masses dephases the DSS pattern and progressively washes out the critical substructure. We normalize the mass functions so that we may primarily study the profile shape and the survival of DSS substructure, rather than the absolute PBH abundance. These results provide a bridge between cosmological DSS collapse simulations and PBH phenomenology including population observables.

</details>


### [71] [Learning Boson Star Solution Families with Physics-Informed Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21845)
*Ao Liu,Chen-Hao Hao,Cuihong Wen,Shao-Jiang Wang,Jieci Wang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a physics-informed neural network (PINN) to efficiently compute boson star equilibrium families—replacing traditional iterative numerical continuation with a single forward pass, while accurately capturing multi-branch mass-frequency spirals and conserved quantities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the computational expense and numerical fragility of conventional methods—repeated nonlinear eigenvalue solves and delicate continuation through turning points—in computing multi-branch boson star families.

Method: A PINN is designed to map physical parameters (e.g., scalar field frequency) and radial coordinate to scalar and metric fields; it enforces regularity and asymptotic boundary conditions intrinsically in the architecture, and trains using a composite loss: pointwise data supervision (where available), Einstein–Klein–Gordon PDE residuals, and curve-level constraints on ADM mass and Noether charge.

Result: The PINN accurately reconstructs full one-, two-, and three-branch boson star families—including inner branches inaccessible to standard solvers without careful continuation—and reproduces mass-frequency spirals and conserved quantities with high fidelity.

Conclusion: Physics-informed surrogate learning via PINNs provides an efficient, robust, and amortized framework for exploring nonlinear self-gravitating equilibria, enabling rapid generation of entire solution manifolds without iterative solving.

Abstract: Computing boson star families traditionally requires repeated solution of nonlinear eigenvalue boundary-value problems and careful numerical continuation through turning points. We develop a physics-informed neural network (PINN) that learns the map from the physical parameters and radial coordinate directly to the scalar and metric fields over an equilibrium solution manifold. Regularity and asymptotic boundary conditions are incorporated into the network output, while the training objective combines pointwise supervision, Einstein-Klein-Gordon residuals, and curve-level constraints on the Arnowitt-Deser-Misner mass and Noether charge. A trained model generates a complete configuration in a single forward pass. Across representative one-, two-, and three-branch families, the method reconstructs the mass-frequency spirals and conserved quantities, including configurations on inner branches that require delicate continuation in conventional solvers. These results establish physics-informed surrogate learning as a practical route to amortized exploration of nonlinear self-gravitating solution families.

</details>


### [72] [Extreme mass-ratio inspirals around rotating accelerating black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22249)
*Xin-Dong Du,Tao Zhou,Wei Xiong,Tieguang Zi,Peng-Cheng Li*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a new waveform framework for extreme mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) around accelerated rotating black holes (spinning C metric), overcoming non-asymptotic-flatness and broken equatorial symmetry; it computes gravitational-wave fluxes via Teukolsky formalism and finds that even weak acceleration (AM ~ 10⁻⁷) induces >1 rad dephasing over one year for typical EMRI parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify how black hole acceleration — encoded in the spinning C metric — imprints detectable phase shifts on EMRI gravitational waveforms, leveraging EMRIs' high sensitivity to spacetime deviations from Kerr.

Method: Constructs a near-equatorial circular orbit in an intermediate 'Kerr-like' wave zone (M/r ≪ 1, Ar ≪ 1); derives the point-particle source for the spin-2 radial Teukolsky equation; builds regular normalized angular eigenfunctions; solves the radial equation via Sasaki–Nakamura transformation and Green function method; couples horizon and far-zone fluxes to adiabatic orbital evolution.

Result: Framework recovers Kerr limit with l=2 flux relative errors ~10⁻⁷; acceleration induces nonmonotonic accumulated dephasing; for M=10⁶M⊙, ms/M=10⁻⁵, a/M=0.7, AM=3×10⁻⁷, dominant-mode dephasing exceeds 1 rad over one year.

Conclusion: Weak linear acceleration of astrophysical black holes produces secular, order-radian gravitational-wave phase shifts in long-duration EMRIs — a potentially observable signature within LISA's sensitivity band, provided the approximation regime (AM ≪ 1) holds.

Abstract: Extreme mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) can magnify small departures from Kerr dynamics into appreciable gravitational-wave phase shifts accumulated over many orbital cycles. We exploit this sensitivity to investigate the imprint of a rotating black hole's acceleration on an EMRI waveform. The spinning C metric poses two obstacles to the standard Kerr flux framework: the spacetime is not asymptotically flat, and the acceleration breaks the reflection symmetry that supports exactly equatorial circular timelike orbits. For sufficiently small acceleration $AM$, we therefore formulate the calculation in an intermediate Kerr-like wave zone satisfying $M/r\ll1$ and $Ar\ll1$, and construct a near-equatorial circular orbit by examining its coupled radial--polar stability. We derive the separated point-particle source for the spin$-2$ radial Teukolsky equation, construct a regular normalized angular solution, solve the radial equation using the Sasaki--Nakamura transformation and the Green function method, and couple the resulting horizon and far-zone fluxes to the adiabatic evolution of stable near-equatorial circular orbits. The framework recovers the Kerr limit and reproduces the dominant $l=2$ Kerr fluxes with relative errors of order $10^{-7}$. Acceleration modifies both radiation reaction and the orbital frequency, producing a characteristic nonmonotonic accumulated dephasing. For $M=10^6M_\odot$, $m_s/M=10^{-5}$, $a/M=0.7$, and $AM=3\times10^{-7}$, the dominant-mode dephasing slightly exceeds $1$ rad over one year. Thus even weak acceleration can generate an order-radian secular phase imprint on long-duration EMRIs within the controlled regime of the present approximation.

</details>


### [73] [Thermodynamics of Genuine Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22292)
*David Kubiznak,Hryhorii Ovcharenko,Jiri Podolsky*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a thermodynamic description of genuine Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson (Kerr-BR) black holes, showing they exhibit standard black hole thermodynamics—Hawking temperature, Bekenstein entropy, Komar mass/angular momentum, horizon angular velocity—despite lacking electric charge; via coordinate rescaling and parameter constraints, their thermodynamics formally matches that of the vacuum Kerr solution, enabling a Kerr-like representation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a consistent and physically meaningful thermodynamic framework for genuine Kerr-BR spacetimes, which—unlike standard BR or Kerr-Newman solutions—carry no electric charge, thereby resolving ambiguities in prior treatments and clarifying their physical interpretation as rotating, charge-free black hole geometries.

Method: The authors introduce a normalized timelike Killing vector to define thermodynamic quantities; compute Hawking temperature, Bekenstein entropy, Komar mass and angular momentum, and horizon angular velocity; verify satisfaction of the Christodoulou-Ruffini mass formula, first law of black hole mechanics, and Smarr relation; perform coordinate and parameter rescalings to map thermodynamic quantities onto those of the vacuum Kerr solution under a non-trivial constraint in parameter space.

Result: Genuine Kerr-BR spacetimes admit a complete, charge-free black hole thermodynamics identical in functional form to Kerr: standard Hawking temperature, Bekenstein entropy, Komar mass and angular momentum, horizon angular velocity, Christodoulou-Ruffini formula, first law, and Smarr relation; through rescaling and a parameter-space constraint, their thermodynamic structure is shown to be formally equivalent to vacuum Kerr.

Conclusion: The genuine Kerr-BR spacetime is not merely an algebraically special solution but a physically viable, rotating, neutral black hole geometry whose thermodynamics is fully equivalent to that of the Kerr black hole—establishing it as a legitimate, Kerr-like black hole solution in its own right.

Abstract: We propose a thermodynamic description of the genuine Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson (Kerr-BR) black hole spacetimes. Contrary to previous studies, such solutions no longer have electric charge. Consequently, the thermodynamics is governed by simple relations. Namely, upon introducing a suitable normalization factor for the timelike Killing vector, these spacetimes are characterized by the standard Hawking temperature, Bekenstein's entropy, Komar mass and angular momentum, and horizon angular velocity. The corresponding mass is shown to obey the standard Christodoulou-Ruffini formula, along with the standard first law and Smarr relations. In fact, by a proper rescaling of coordinates and solution parameters, and up to a non-trivial parameter space constraint, we formally recover the thermodynamic quantities of the vacuum Kerr solution. This is used to bring the genuine Kerr-BR spacetime into a Kerr-like form.

</details>


### [74] [Correlated and coincident noise in Einstein Telescope's triangular configuration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22401)
*Jan Harms*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a time-domain simulation of environmental noise in the Einstein Telescope's triangular configuration, revealing that correlated environmental noise and transient-induced coincident glitches significantly limit its observational capabilities and degrade the utility of its null stream.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously assess the scientific viability of the single-site triangular configuration of the Einstein Telescope (ET) by addressing the previously superficial treatment of environmental noise correlations and transient-induced glitches.

Method: Time-domain simulation of environmental noises using site-specific data from ET candidate locations; modeling and analysis of correlated environmental noise and coincident glitches arising from transients in environmental fields.

Result: Correlated environmental noise and transient-induced coincident glitches impose significant limitations on the ET triangle’s observational sensitivity and critically impair the effectiveness of its null stream—a key tool for noise subtraction and signal validation.

Conclusion: The environmental noise characteristics strongly disfavor the single-site triangular configuration relative to the distributed two-site L-shaped configuration, reinforcing the scientific preference for networked detectors despite prior arguments suggesting marginal trade-offs.

Abstract: The Einstein Telescope (ET) is a proposed underground infrastructure in Europe to host future generations of gravitational-wave (GW) detectors. Two configurations are under investigation. The first is the two-site solution with an L-shape detector at each site similar to the LIGO configuration. The second configuration is the single-site triangular detector. While numerous analyses over the last decade have already proven that the distributed network of GW detectors is the scientifically preferred solution, it was also argued that the advantage might not be decisive enough to dismiss ET's triangle configuration. The modeling of aspects such as noise correlations in these configurations has only been treated superficially in the past. In this article, we introduce a time-domain simulation of environmental noises in the ET triangle using available data from ET's candidate sites. We simulate and analyze correlated environmental noise as well as coincident glitches produced by transients in the environmental fields. We find that these features pose an important limitation to the observational capabilities of the ET triangle and limit the utility of its null stream.

</details>


### [75] [Cosmological extra dimensions can mimic dark matter I. Dark matter in galaxies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22456)
*Mattia Villani*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Proposes a n+4D metric model with space-dependent compactification radius R, linking R's variation to matter density and Newtonian potential to explain galactic rotation curves; proposes six experimental tests.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain galactic rotation curves without dark matter by introducing a geometric modification via a space-dependent compactification radius in a higher-dimensional metric.

Method: Constructs a n + 4-dimensional spacetime metric with compactified extra dimensions whose radius R varies spatially; derives the dependence of R on visible matter density and Newtonian gravitational potential using field equations or effective gravity constraints.

Result: Derives a functional form for R(r) that successfully reproduces observed spiral galaxy rotation curves; identifies six feasible experimental signatures or tests for the model.

Conclusion: The model offers a geometric alternative to dark matter by encoding gravitational anomalies in the profile of the extra-dimensional radius R, and is empirically falsifiable via proposed experiments.

Abstract: We present a simple model with a n + 4-dimensional metric with compactified extra dimensions with a space dependent radius R. We show that changes in the profile of R are sourced by (visible) matter density and Newtonian potential. We can reproduce rotation curves of spiral galaxies and provide an expression for R. In order to test our hypothesis, we propose six experiments.

</details>


### [76] [Killing tensors of Weyl's class](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22523)
*Dionysios Kokkinos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a systematic framework to identify hidden symmetries (via irreducible Killing tensors) in algebraically general (Petrov type I) spacetimes, focusing on Weyl's class of static, axisymmetric solutions; it analytically shows that no vacuum or electrovacuum two-Killing-vector Weyl spacetime admits an irreducible rank-2 Killing tensor, and Levi-Civita (three-Killing-vector) members are already integrable via reducible tensors—thus linking absence of hidden symmetries to non-integrability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a systematic framework for detecting hidden symmetries—encoded in irreducible Killing tensors—in algebraically general (Petrov type I) spacetimes, where standard symmetry-based integrability methods fail.

Method: Systematic derivation of constraints for existence of rank-2 Killing tensors using their canonical forms, applied specifically to Weyl's class of static, axisymmetric vacuum and electrovacuum spacetimes; classification by number of commuting Killing vectors (2 vs. 3).

Result: No two-Killing-vector member of Weyl’s class (in vacuum or electrovacuum) admits an irreducible rank-2 Killing tensor; all such candidates are reducible. Three-Killing-vector (Levi-Civita) members are fully integrable via manifest symmetries alone and support only reducible Killing tensors. Obstructions to complete integrability are analytically characterized within subclasses.

Conclusion: The absence of irreducible Killing tensors in Weyl’s class (except trivially in the Levi-Civita case) implies lack of hidden symmetries necessary for complete integrability—establishing a rigorous analytic link between symmetry structure and dynamical integrability in Petrov type I geometries, and validating canonical Killing tensor analysis as a general tool.

Abstract: This work represents an initial step towards a systematic framework for identifying hidden symmetries in algebraically general (Petrov type I) spacetimes. Motivated by this, we focus on Weyl's class, which provides a simple yet a class of algebraically general solutions of static, axisymmetric geometries. Employing the canonical forms of rank-2 Killing tensors, we systematically derive the constraints that must be satisfied for these spacetimes to admit genuine hidden symmetries. Our analysis yields an analytical characterization of the obstructions to integrability within subclasses of Weyl's class, both in vacuum and electrovacuum. In particular, we find that no two-Killing-vector member of Weyl's class, in vacuum or electrovacuum, admits an irreducible Killing tensor capable of supplying the additional integral of motion required for complete integrability. Members of Weyl's class admitting a third additional Killing-vector isometry (i.e. Levi-Civita) are already completely integrable through their manifest symmetries alone admitting only reducible Killing tensors. This result provides an analytic complement to previous analytical and numerical investigations, and establishes a systematic connection between the absence of hidden symmetries and the breakdown of complete integrability. Beyond the specific classification obtained here, these results demonstrate the potential of our approach regarding the canonical forms of Killing tensor as a systematic tool for probing hidden symmetries in Petrov type I spacetimes.

</details>


### [77] [Geometric formulation for the relativistic kinetic theory of photons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22881)
*Yifan Cai,Long Cui,Bin Wu,Liu Zhao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a geometric framework for photon kinetic theory by defining a non-vanishing volume element on the degenerate light cone bundle via a Hodge-like method, enabling a fully covariant Boltzmann equation and consistent hydrodynamic definitions analogous to massive particles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the geometric obstacle posed by the degeneracy of the induced metric on the future light cone bundle, which nullifies the standard volume element and obstructs a rigorous, covariant kinetic description of photons.

Method: Constructs a well-defined volume form η_{Γ₀⁺} on the degenerate light cone bundle Γ₀⁺ using a Hodge-dual–inspired technique; employs the resulting geometric triple (Γ₀⁺, η_{Γ₀⁺}, ĥ) to formulate a fully covariant Boltzmann equation for photons and derive hydrodynamic quantities and their divergences.

Result: A fully covariant Boltzmann equation for photons is established; physically consistent phase-space distributions are defined; hydrodynamic currents and their covariant divergences are derived, matching the mathematical form used for massive particles.

Conclusion: The geometric degeneracy of the light cone bundle does not preclude a rigorous, covariant kinetic and hydrodynamic theory of photons—provided an appropriately constructed volume element is used, enabling direct analogy with massive particle transport.

Abstract: We provide a geometric foundation for the kinetic theory of photons. Although the induced metric $\hat h$ on the light cone bundle $Γ_0^+$ is degenerate, which causes the corresponding volume element to vanish, we can still use a method similar to the Hodge dual to construct a volume element $η_{Γ_0^+}$ for the light cone bundle. Based on this geometric structure $(Γ^+_0,η_{Γ_0^+},\hat h)$, we establish the fully covariant Boltzmann equation for photons. More importantly, the volume element and the induced metric are linked in a nontrivial way, which allows the physical distributions to be defined consistently. This yields the corresponding hydrodynamic quantities and their divergences, which take the same form as in the case of massive particles.

</details>


### [78] [Comment on Stellar structure and stability of charged interacting quark stars and their scaling behavior](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22653)
*Yann Bissessur,Michael Gammon,Robert B. Mann*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This comment corrects an error in prior work on radial stability of charged interacting quark stars, recalculates fundamental eigenfrequencies, and finds broader stability ranges and newly stable branches; observational constraints are satisfied by a subset of corrected mass-radius relations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To correct an error in the radial stability analysis of charged interacting quark stars reported in Phys. Rev. D 102, 034031 (2020) that propagated into erroneous solutions in Phys. Rev. D 104, 123007 (2021).

Method: Recalculation of fundamental radial eigenfrequencies for charged, interacting quark stars in general relativity using corrected stability equations; comparison with prior results and assessment against four new observational constraints.

Result: Corrected eigenfrequencies yield significantly altered magnitudes for the separation between maximum-mass and stability-limit points; previously unstable branches now contain stable configurations; overall stability range of charged quark stars is enlarged; a subset of corrected mass-radius relations satisfies four recent observational constraints.

Conclusion: The stability properties of charged interacting quark stars are more robust than previously reported; the correction invalidates earlier conclusions about instability on certain branches and expands viable parameter space consistent with observations.

Abstract: We comment on an error in the charged quark star literature ( Phys. Rev. D, 102(3):034031, 2020 ) that resulted in erroneous solutions to the radial stability equations for charged interacting quark stars in ( Phys. Rev. D, 104(12):123007, 2021 )[2]. In this comment article we recalculate the fundamental radial eigenfrequencies for charged, interacting quark stars in general relativity and discuss any departure from previous results [2]. Most of the qualitative trends persist, but the magnitudes of the separation between the maximum mass and stability points on a given branch can change significantly. We also note that all branches in [2] that contained no stable solutions now have stable solutions in their corrected counterparts. The net effect is that in all of the studied cases charged quark stars have a greater range of stability than originally predicted. We also compare four recent observational constraints not included in previous studies, and find that these constraints are satisfied by a subset of the mass-radius relations we obtain.

</details>


### [79] [Joule Thomson expansion of $κ$-deformed Schwarzschild-AdS black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22791)
*Vishnu Rajagopal,Puxun Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies Joule-Thomson expansion in a κ-deformed non-commutative Schwarzschild-AdS black hole, revealing that κ-deformation enables such expansion even for uncharged black holes; it finds a universal minimum-to-critical inversion temperature ratio (~0.493), matching the Reissner-Nordström case, and identifies a κ-dependent minimum black hole mass threshold for expansion onset.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore thermodynamic analogies between black holes and ordinary matter—specifically whether quantum-gravity-inspired non-commutative spacetime (κ-deformation) can induce Joule-Thomson expansion in otherwise non-expanding (uncharged) AdS black holes.

Method: Analytical thermodynamic analysis of the κ-deformed Schwarzschild-AdS black hole: derivation of equation of state, computation of inversion curve via (∂T/∂P)_H = 0, identification of critical and minimum inversion temperatures, and determination of mass threshold from positivity condition of inversion temperature.

Result: κ-deformation induces Joule-Thomson expansion in uncharged AdS black holes; T_i^(min)/T_c ≈ 0.493 — universal (κ-independent) and matches RN black hole; existence of a κ-dependent minimum black hole mass below which no inversion curve exists (i.e., no cooling/heating region).

Conclusion: Non-commutative κ-deformation provides a viable quantum-gravity mechanism to activate Joule-Thomson behavior in Schwarzschild-AdS black holes, preserving universality in inversion temperature scaling while introducing a deformation-dependent mass cutoff for thermodynamic expandability.

Abstract: We investigate the Joule-Thomson expansion of a non-commutative Schwarzschild-AdS black hole, whose space-time geometry is described by the Lie-algebraic $κ$-deformed space-time, and show that the $κ$-deformation parameter induces Joule-Thomson expansion in an uncharged-AdS black hole. We find the ratio of minimum inversion temperature to critical temperature $T_i^{(min)}/T_c \simeq 0.493$, is independent of the deformation parameter, and is in close agreement with the value for Reissner-Nordstrom black hole. Furthermore, we show the existence of a minimum black hole mass (depending on the $κ$-deformation parameter) below which the Joule-Thomson expansion vanishes.

</details>


### [80] [Kerr Black Hole Uniqueness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23325)
*Qing Han,Marcus Khuri,Gilbert Weinstein,Jingang Xiong*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proves the black hole uniqueness conjecture for axially symmetric, stationary, vacuum spacetimes with multiple horizons, showing that configurations with more than one horizon component (where degenerate horizons carry nonzero angular momentum) are ruled out due to strictly negative logarithmic angle defects—and thus attractive axis forces—on finite axis rods; the proof relies on refined asymptotics of singular harmonic maps and a global bound on the Weyl conformal factor from a scalar curvature inequality; as a corollary, it establishes the mass–angular momentum inequality for multiple dynamical black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the black hole uniqueness conjecture in the physically relevant multi-horizon, axially symmetric, stationary vacuum setting—particularly addressing whether regular asymptotically flat spacetimes can support multiple black hole horizons under mild non-degeneracy conditions.

Method: Refined asymptotic analysis of singular harmonic maps associated with the Weyl–Papapetrou metric ansatz (building on companion work [16]); derivation and exploitation of a differential inequality involving scalar curvature to obtain a global bound on the Weyl conformal factor; computation and sign analysis of logarithmic angle defects along finite axis rods.

Result: Any regular, asymptotically flat, axially symmetric, stationary vacuum solution with more than one horizon component—where each degenerate horizon has nonzero angular momentum—must exhibit strictly negative logarithmic angle defects on all finite axis rods, implying attractive inter-horizon forces; such configurations are therefore non-existent, confirming uniqueness.
