As a corollary, the mass–angular momentum inequality for multiple dynamical black holes is established using [17].

Conclusion: The black hole uniqueness conjecture holds in the stated class: no regular multi-horizon stationary vacuum black holes exist under the given angular momentum condition; this yields a rigorous foundation for the mass–angular momentum inequality in multi-black-hole scenarios.

Abstract: We prove the black hole uniqueness conjecture in the axially symmetric, stationary, vacuum setting: there is no regular asymptotically flat configuration with more than one horizon component when every degenerate component has nonzero angular momentum. More precisely, it is shown that in any such multi-horizon configuration, the logarithmic angle defect along every finite axis rod is strictly negative, and hence the interaction force along such axis rods is always attractive. The proof is based on a refined asymptotic analysis of the associated singular harmonic maps, in part from the companion paper [16], together with a global bound for the Weyl conformal factor arising from a certain scalar curvature related differential inequality. Furthermore, as a corollary of the extremal version of this uniqueness result, we obtain the conjectured mass-angular momentum inequality for multiple dynamical black holes using the main theorem of [17].

</details>


### [81] [Curvature and Isocurvature Perturbations in multi-field Gauss-Bonnet inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22970)
*Seoktae Koh*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies cosmological perturbations in multi-field inflation with scalar fields coupled to the Gauss-Bonnet term via a general function f(φᵃ); it derives the full quadratic action, identifies vanishing effective mass and mixing for curvature perturbations due to adiabaticity, and computes curvature/tensor power spectra under two limiting regimes—integrating out heavy isocurvature modes or aligning coupling gradient with background trajectory—revealing modified sound speed and turn-rate-dependent transfer, plus Gauss-Bonnet corrections to tensor-to-scalar ratio.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how Gauss-Bonnet couplings in multi-field inflation affect cosmological perturbations—particularly curvature–isocurvature dynamics, sound speed, and tensor observables—beyond standard Einstein gravity.

Method: Derivation of the complete quadratic action for field perturbations in spatially flat gauge; decomposition into curvature and isocurvature modes; use of Weinberg’s adiabatic mode theorem to justify vanishing effective mass and mixing terms; analytical computation of power spectra in two asymptotic limits: (i) heavy isocurvature limit (integrated out), yielding an effective single-field description; (ii) aligned coupling gradient limit (f_N = 0), suppressing derivative mixing and isolating turn-rate–driven superhorizon transfer; linear-order expansion in Gauss-Bonnet coupling for tensor spectrum and r.

Result: Gauss-Bonnet coupling introduces an antisymmetric velocity coupling absent in Einstein gravity; curvature perturbation effective mass ℳ_ℛ² and non-derivative mixing mass ℳ_mix² vanish identically; in the heavy-isocurvature limit, curvature perturbation acquires a modified sound speed; in the f_N = 0 limit, isocurvature-to-curvature transfer is governed by a Gauss-Bonnet–modified turn rate; tensor power spectrum and tensor-to-scalar ratio receive linear-in-coupling corrections.

Conclusion: Gauss-Bonnet couplings qualitatively alter the perturbative structure of multi-field inflation—notably through new velocity couplings and modified dynamical transfer between modes—while preserving adiabaticity constraints; observable signatures include scale-dependent sound speed, turn-rate modifications, and detectable shifts in r, offering novel probes of higher-curvature gravity during inflation.

Abstract: We study cosmological perturbations in multi-field inflation in which the scalar fields couple to the Gauss-Bonnet terms through a general coupling function $f(φ^a)$. We derive the complete quadratic action for the field perturbations in spatially flat gauge and find that, besides corrections to the kinetic, gradient and mass matrices, the Gauss-Bonnet coupling induces an antisymmetric velocity coupling that vanishes in Einstein gravity. Decomposing the field perturbations into curvature and isocurvature modes, we show that the effective mass of the curvature perturbation and the non-derivative mixing mass vanish identically, $\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{R}}^2 = \mathcal{M}_{\rm mix}^2 =0$ which are justified by the Weinberg's adiabatic mode. We then compute the curvature power spectrum in two limits. First, when the isocurvature mode is heavy, it can be integrated over all scales. This yields an effective single-field theory of the curvature perturbation with a modified sound speed. Second, when the gradient of the coupling function is aligned with the background trajectory ($f_N =0$), the derivative mixings disappear and the superhorizon transfer of isocurvature into curvature perturbations is governed by the turn rate, which is modified by the Gauss-Bonnet corrections. We also present the tensor power spectrum and the tensor-to-scalar ratio at linear order in the coupling.

</details>


### [82] [Scalar charge bounds for extremal black hole formation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23355)
*Berend Schneider*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper establishes a lower bound |e|r_+ > 1/3 for scalar charge e in extremal Reissner–Nordström black hole formation via charged scalar field collapse, shows no such bound exists for subextremal collapse, and proves a threshold |e|r_+ > √[(3+√33)/3] for Schwarzschild-to-extremal transition—challenging the third law of black hole mechanics in vacuum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate whether the third law of black hole mechanics (which states that extremality cannot be reached from a non-extremal state in finite time or through regular processes) holds for charged scalar field collapse, particularly in light of recent constructions of extremal black holes from collapse.

Method: Rigorous analytical proof using techniques from general relativity and PDE theory for spherically symmetric Einstein–Maxwell-scalar field system; employs energy estimates, monotonicity properties of renormalized Hawking mass, and sharp asymptotics near the event horizon and singularity.

Result: 1) For collapse forming an exactly extremal RN black hole, |e|r_+ > 1/3 is necessary. 2) For subextremal collapse, arbitrary nonzero e can yield arbitrary subextremal charge-to-mass ratio. 3) A Schwarzschild black hole can become extremal iff |e|r_+ > √[(3+√33)/3] ≈ 1.897.

Conclusion: The existence of finite, order-unity scalar charge thresholds enabling extremalization contradicts the strict interpretation of the third law in vacuum GR, suggesting its failure when matter fields with scalar charge are present.

Abstract: I prove that a collapsing charged scalar field with scalar charge $\mathfrak{e}$ that forms an exactly extremal Reissner--Nordström black hole à la Kehle--Unger with radius $r_+$ must satisfy $|\mathfrak{e}|r_+ > \frac{1}{3}$. In contrast, I also show that no such bound exists for subextremal collapse: a scalar field with an arbitrary $\mathfrak{e} \ne 0$ can form a subextremal black hole with an arbitrary (subextremal) charge-to-mass ratio.
  Complementing the extremal bound, I prove that a Schwarzschild black hole can become extremal if $|\mathfrak{e}|r_+ > \sqrt{\frac{3 + \sqrt{33}}{3}}$. The fact that $|\mathfrak{e}|r_+$ can be taken to be of order unity could be considered evidence that the third law of black hole mechanics in vacuum is false.

</details>


### [83] [Gravitationally-decoupled hairy black holes: probing geometric, optical, and quasinormal mode signatures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23091)
*Paul Allen M. Gonzales,Anna Chrysostomou,Alan S. Cornell,Emmanuel Rodulfo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper uses gravitational-decoupling to generate hairy black hole solutions from Schwarzschild seed, analyzes their horizon structure, photon spheres, shadows, and scalar QNMs, emphasizes ADM-mass normalization over seed-mass, and shows that horizon radius alone does not fix observables—hence multiple complementary probes are needed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically explore how gravitational-decoupling-induced deformations (e.g., scalar hair) affect key black hole observables—horizon geometry, photon sphere, shadow size, and quasinormal modes—while clarifying the role of mass definition (seed vs. ADM) in interpreting physical predictions.

Method: Constructs static, spherically symmetric hairy black holes via gravitational-decoupling from Schwarzschild seed; imposes horizon existence and energy conditions to constrain parameter space; normalizes observables by ADM mass (not seed mass); computes photon sphere radii and shadow angular scales; calculates massless scalar quasinormal modes using 6th-order WKB and improved Asymptotic Iteration Method (AIM).

Result: Fixing horizon radius does not uniquely determine shadow scale or QNM spectrum; ADM-normalized shadows differ significantly from seed-mass-normalized ones; QNM frequencies computed via WKB and AIM agree closely; horizon structure, null geodesic observables (shadows), and scalar QNMs provide distinct, complementary constraints on decoupled geometries.

Conclusion: Gravitational-decoupling yields physically viable hairy black holes whose observable signatures depend critically on proper mass normalization (ADM); no single observable suffices—joint analysis of horizon properties, shadows, and QNMs is essential to break degeneracies and constrain deformation parameters.

Abstract: Gravitational-decoupling provides a systematic framework for constructing black hole geometries from known (seed) solutions (such as the Schwarzschild), whose exterior properties closely resemble those of the seed solution while still retaining potentially distinguishable features. Using this approach, we study a class of static, spherically symmetric hairy black holes and examine how such deformations affect their horizon structure, photon spheres, shadow scales, and scalar quasinormal modes (QNMs). We first determine the physically controlled regions of the parameter space by imposing the relevant horizon and energy condition requirements, while retaining configurations outside these domains only for exploratory comparison. To isolate geometric effects, we compare solutions at a common horizon radius, while accounting explicitly for the distinction between the mass parameter entering the seed geometry and the ADM mass measured asymptotically. The resulting ADM normalized shadow scales are used for an illustrative comparison with the characteristic angular scales of M87* and Sgr A*. We also compute massless scalar QNMs using the sixth-order Wentzel-Kramers-Brillouin approximation and the improved Asymptotic Iteration Method, finding close agreement over the modes considered. Our results show that fixing the horizon radius does not uniquely determine either the optical or perturbative properties of the spacetime, and that mass normalization can significantly alter the interpretation of geometric trends. Horizon structure, null geodesic observables, and scalar QNMs therefore provide complementary probes of gravitationally-decoupled black hole geometries.

</details>


### [84] [Remarks on quantum scalar field theory for a uniformly accelerated reference frame and a Schwarzschild black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23108)
*Mikhail N. Smolyakov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper examines formal consistency issues in quantum scalar field theories on uniformly accelerated frames (Rindler) and Schwarzschild black hole spacetimes (2D/4D), revealing pathologies: certain Minkowski states have infinite energy in Rindler space, the standard Unruh vacuum construction is inconsistent in 4D Schwarzschild, and consistency in Kruskal-Szekeres coordinates remains problematic.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify and analyze previously unaddressed formal inconsistencies in quantum scalar field theory on non-inertial and curved backgrounds—specifically uniform acceleration and Schwarzschild geometry—where standard constructions (e.g., Unruh vacuum) may break down.

Method: Rigorous analysis of quantum state definitions, energy expectations, and vacuum constructions across coordinate systems (Minkowski, Rindler, Schwarzschild, Kruskal-Szekeres); emphasis on domain and operator consistency, energy finiteness, and global versus local quantum field definitions.

Result: 1) Certain Minkowski QFT states acquire infinite energy in Rindler spacetime, exposing a pathology in state transplantation; 2) The standard Unruh vacuum construction fails to yield a consistent quantum field theory in full 4D Schwarzschild spacetime; 3) Consistency of scalar QFT in Kruskal-Szekeres coordinates remains unresolved due to global causal and boundary ambiguities.

Conclusion: Standard quantum field theoretic constructions on non-globally hyperbolic or non-inertial spacetimes suffer from deep formal inconsistencies—particularly regarding energy boundedness and vacuum uniqueness—necessitating revised frameworks for defining physically admissible states and observables.

Abstract: In the present paper, the cases of a uniformly accelerated reference frame and a Schwarzschild black hole in two and four dimensions are discussed focusing on formal aspects of the corresponding quantum scalar field theories. The main emphasis is made on building consistent quantum field theories and highlighting the problems that are not considered elsewhere. In particular, some pathologies in the case of a uniformly accelerated reference frame are discussed in detail: it is shown that some quantum states that are well defined in Minkowski spacetime possess an infinite energy when considered in Rindler spacetime. In the case of a Schwarzschild black hole in four dimensions, it is shown that the standard theory providing the Unruh vacuum state turns out to be inconsistent. The problem of consistency of quantum scalar field theory in Kruskal-Szekeres spacetime is also discussed.

</details>


### [85] [Relational Quantum Causal Processes toward Quantum Gravity with Controlled Einstein Response](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23117)
*Yipeng Xu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper establishes a fixed-band closure theorem for microscopic quantum gravity, demonstrating how geometric and gravitational observables—including Newton's coefficient, stress tensor, and excitation gap—can be derived consistently from a single quantum family without fitting parameters at interfaces; it uses controlled regulator limits, analytic bounds, and spectral analysis within a covariant FLRW sector.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the central challenge in quantum gravity of deriving geometric and gravitational observables from a single refining quantum family without altering dynamics or fitting new coefficients at each regulator interface.

Method: Constructs a controlled benchmark on a fixed physical Fourier band using finite-Hilbert interacting Hamiltonians; employs Duhamel and Weyl inequalities, uniform gap analysis, analytic perturbation theory, and kernel-remainder estimates to establish convergence of two inequivalent spatial symbols to a common regulator limit; computes observables via spectrum and source derivatives of the Schwinger functional.

Result: A common fixed-band regulator limit is proven; the induced Newton coefficient is uniquely fixed (not fitted) by the metric source within the local covariant FLRW two-derivative sector; nonzero connected fourth response and dimensionless spectral combination are confirmed via direct diagonalization; the excitation gap yields a conserved stress tensor supporting semiclassical Friedmann evolution.

Conclusion: This is a same-family fixed-band closure theorem—neither a full QFT nor completed quantum gravity—but provides a reproducible benchmark and identifies precise further theorems required for nonperturbative closure.

Abstract: The central challenge in microscopic quantum-gravity models is to derive geometric and gravitational observables from one refining quantum family without changing dynamics or fitting a new coefficient at each interface. We construct a controlled benchmark on a declared fixed physical Fourier band. For every spatial regulator, one finite-Hilbert interacting Hamiltonian defines a unitary completely positive process and a Euclidean Schwinger functional. Its spectrum and source derivatives determine the Lorentzian gap, connected four-response, mixed matter--geometry response, dynamic geometry kernel, induced Newton coefficient, and quantum-matter stress entering a semiclassical FLRW evolution.
  Two inequivalent spatial symbols obey analytic second- and fourth-order bounds and converge to the same limiting Hamiltonian. Duhamel and Weyl inequalities, a uniform gap, analytic perturbation theory, and a kernel-remainder estimate yield a common fixed-band regulator limit. Within the declared local covariant FLRW two-derivative sector, the metric source uniquely fixes a positive induced Newton coefficient rather than leaving it as an interface fit. Direct diagonalization independently yields a nonzero connected fourth response and a non-fitted dimensionless spectral combination. The excitation gap supplies a conserved stress tensor for a semiclassical Friedmann trajectory.
  This is a same-family fixed-band closure theorem, not an all-band QFT or completed quantum gravity. The two-mode band, finite oscillator cutoff, covariant two-derivative sector, and semiclassical Einstein equation are inputs. It does not construct a quantum metric Hilbert space, quantum BV measure, topology-change control, or autonomous sector selection. The result provides a reproducible benchmark and isolates the further theorems needed for nonperturbative closure.

</details>


### [86] [Complex frequency evolution of direct waves from binary black hole mergers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23209)
*Ling Sun,Sizheng Ma,Patrick P. Chen,Andrew Laeuger,Neil Lu,Yanbei Chen*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces the 'direct wave' concept—a source-sensitive gravitational waveform component encoding horizon-formation dynamics—by filtering out quasinormal modes (QNMs) from numerical-relativity simulations; it reveals that the direct wave’s instantaneous complex frequency evolves toward horizon-controlled values (2Ω_H and ~2κ_H), with spin-dependent convergence behavior, and proposes a minimal, spin-adapted rational filter set motivated by Kerr response pole-zero structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To isolate and interpret horizon-formation information encoded in asymptotic gravitational waves—beyond the standard quasinormal-mode (QNM) paradigm—by distinguishing source-sensitive 'direct wave' contributions from damped sinusoidal QNM ringing.

Method: Applies rational-function filters to numerical-relativity waveforms to suppress quasinormal modes; analyzes the resulting 'direct wave' using instantaneous complex frequency (via analytic signal or Hilbert transform); compares evolution trajectories against remnant black-hole parameters (Ω_H, κ_H, χ_f); leverages pole-zero structure of the Kerr response to design a minimal, spin-dependent filter set.

Result: The direct wave exhibits late-time convergence of its real instantaneous frequency to 2Ω_H (with direction of approach dependent on final spin χ_f ≈ 0.7) and decay rate to ~2κ_H (sometimes exceeding it for high spin); early-time deviations encode merger dynamics; finite-time agreement with horizon parameters does not invalidate the horizon connection; approximate pole-zero pairing in Kerr response enables compact, spin-tuned filters that preserve source-trajectory information while suppressing QNMs.

Conclusion: The direct wave provides a physically meaningful, horizon-anchored observable in gravitational-wave signals, encoding dynamical information about plunge and remnant formation; its evolution validates the role of frame-dragging and horizon geometry in controlling late-time waveform morphology, and motivates spin-aware filtering strategies for waveform modeling and parameter inference.

Abstract: While no signal originating at a black hole's event horizon can reach future null infinity, information about the horizon and its immediate vicinity can be encoded in asymptotic properties of waves emitted by matter or field perturbations falling toward a growing/forming horizon. Within a response-filtered framework, "direct wave" denotes source-sensitive plunge and remnant-formation information revealed by filtering the black-hole response. Unlike a stationary damped sinusoid with a fixed complex frequency, the direct wave follows the evolving source and has an evolving instantaneous complex frequency tied at late times to the remnant horizon angular velocity $Ω_H$ and surface gravity $κ_H$. Using rational filters, we remove quasinormal modes from numerical-relativity waveforms to study this evolution. Before numerical contamination, trajectories depend on remnant spin: the real frequency evolves toward $2Ω_H$ from above for lower spins ($χ_f\lesssim0.7$) and from below for higher spins ($χ_f\gtrsim0.7$), while the instantaneous decay rate increases toward $\sim2κ_H$ and, in some high-spin cases, beyond it. As in particle-plunge results, the horizon-controlled value is approached only at late times, as frame dragging controls near-horizon motion. For $χ_f\sim0.7$ remnants of non-precessing, comparable-mass binaries, the early-time real frequency is close to $2Ω_H$ because the binary orbital frequency transitions smoothly to the remnant horizon frequency. Finite-time deviations therefore carry information about merger/collapse dynamics rather than undermining the horizon connection. Full direct-wave evolution requires numerical-relativity calibration. We further show that approximate pole-zero pairing in the Kerr response motivates a spin-dependent minimal filter set that suppresses quasinormal-mode features while revealing source-trajectory information.

</details>


### [87] [Gravitational lensing outside and inside of a marginally unstable photon sphere at a $Z_2$-symmetric wormhole throat in strong deflection limits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23269)
*Naoki Tsukamoto*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes gravitational lensing near marginally unstable photon spheres at Z₂-symmetric wormhole throats, showing that deflection angles diverge in power law (not logarithmically as in black holes), and numerically confirms this universality across several wormhole models (Damour-Solodukhin, Simpson-Visser), while correcting prior semi-analytic coefficients.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how strong-field gravitational lensing differs between black holes and wormholes—specifically, whether photon sphere degeneracy (e.g., marginal instability at a Z₂-symmetric throat) leads to qualitatively distinct deflection angle behavior detectable in black hole shadow observations.

Method: Numerical integration of null geodesics in strong-deflection limit near marginally unstable photon spheres; applied to Damour-Solodukhin wormhole and Simpson-Visser black-bounce spacetimes; supplemented by corrected semi-analytic expansion for power-law divergence coefficients.

Result: Deflection angles diverge as a power law (not logarithmically) near marginally unstable photon spheres located at Z₂-symmetric wormhole throats; this behavior is universal across tested models; previously published semi-analytic coefficients for the power-divergent terms are identified as incorrect and revised.

Conclusion: The power-law divergence of deflection angles near marginally unstable photon spheres at Z₂-symmetric throats is a robust, model-independent signature distinguishing such wormholes from black holes in strong gravitational lensing—potentially observable in future high-resolution imaging of black hole shadows.

Abstract: The deflection angles of rays diverge logarithmically near outside and inside of a photon sphere and they could be detected by near-future space observations on black hole shadows. We show that the deflection angles of the rays near a photon sphere at the throat of an example among some commonly used types of wormholes or one of the simple wormholes, which is often studied by the Event Horizon Telescope collaborations, does diverge not logarithmically but in power. This is because it has a marginally unstable photon sphere at a $Z_2$-symmetric wormhole throat and the wormhole has not a common nor simple but a special property. We investigate numerically gravitational lensing of rays with the deflection angles diverging in power outside and inside of a marginally unstable photon sphere at $Z_2$-symmetric wormhole throat in strong deflection limits. We apply our numerical method to the commonly-used simple wormhole spacetime, a Damour-Solodukhin wormhole spacetime, and a Simpson-Visser black-bounce spacetime. Our numerical results imply universal property of the deflection angle by the marginally unstable photon sphere on $Z_2$-symmetric wormhole throat in the strong deflection limits as similar to the deflection angles of ray bent by a marginally unstable photon sphere off a throat in the strong deflection limits which have a different power. We also correct the coefficients of power-divergent terms of deflection angles of the rays bent nearly outside of the marginally unstable photon sphere at the $Z_2$-symmetric wormhole throat in a semi-analytic approach investigated by the author and others.

</details>


### [88] [Action-selected currents and a singular conservation closure in two-fluid energy--momentum-squared cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23447)
*Özgür Akarsu,Bilal Bulduk,Nihan Katırcı*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a two-fluid background in multi-fluid, matter-type gravity (inspired by EMSG), showing that phenomenological current assignments—without an underlying off-shell action—lead to an effective closure with singular α→0 behavior, nontrivial transfer coefficients, modified Hubble dynamics (Liénard equation), and strict rank-degeneracy conditions; it serves as a consistency diagnostic, not an observational model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To distinguish between action-derived predictions and phenomenological closure artifacts in multi-fluid extensions of gravity, particularly in matter-type theories like EMSG, where the Bianchi identity alone does not fix sectoral energy-momentum currents.

Method: Formulates a spatially flat two-fluid background using an algebraic perfect-fluid prescription and vanishing contracted-Hessian contribution, enforcing separate conservation of conventional and modification sectors; derives eigenmodes for densities, obtains a modified Liénard equation for H, and analyzes rank degeneracy of the density-reconstruction map via discriminant analysis.

Result: The closure yields a Barrow-Clifton system with α-independent transfer coefficients; the α→0 limit is singular (fails to recover GR); two distinct real rank-degenerate couplings exist for all finite w₁≠w₂; exact vacuum/stiff-fluid solutions show modal cancellation; positivity of today’s densities terminates at finite cosmic times in analyzed parameter ranges.

Conclusion: The results provide a diagnostic tool to identify closure-dependent artifacts versus action-level predictions in multi-fluid gravity, but do not validate an observationally viable EMSG model.

Abstract: In multi-fluid, matter-type gravity, the Bianchi identity constrains the divergence of the total effective stress tensor but does not, by itself, determine the currents assigned to its constituent sectors. Those currents are selected only after the off-shell matter action and equations, or an additional phenomenological closure, have been specified. We formulate this distinction and examine a spatially flat two-fluid background inspired by scale-independent EMSG. The case study combines an algebraic perfect-fluid prescription with a vanishing contracted-Hessian contribution, together with separate conservation of the total conventional and modification sectors. Because neither assumption is derived here from a concrete off-shell fluid action, the resulting system is an effective background closure rather than a microscopic EMSG model. For unequal EoS and every $α\ne0$, the closure yields a Barrow-Clifton system whose transfer coefficients depend on $(w_1,w_2)$ but not on $α$. Hence the nonzero-$α$ family is singular: its $α\to0$ limit does not recover the uncoupled GR conservation laws. We solve the two density eigenmodes and derive the associated modified Liénard equation for $H$. We also prove that the discriminant governing rank loss of the density-reconstruction map is strictly positive for every finite $w_1\ne w_2$. Thus each genuinely quadratic case has two distinct real rank-degenerate couplings. Exact vacuum and stiff-fluid families illustrate modal cancellation. When today's conventional densities are positive, the intervals on which both remain positive generally terminate at finite endpoints in the parameter ranges analyzed explicitly. These results provide a consistency diagnostic for separating action-level predictions from closure artifacts in multi-fluid, matter-type gravity; they do not establish an observationally viable EMSG model.

</details>


### [89] [Updated Upper Limits on the Isotropic Gravitational-Wave Background from LIGO, Virgo, and KAGRA Data through April 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23477)
*The LIGO Scientific Collaboration,the Virgo Collaboration,the KAGRA Collaboration,A. G. Abac,A. Abe,I. Abouelfettouh,F. Acernese,K. Ackley,A. Adam,C. Adamcewicz,S. Adhicary,D. Adhikari,R. X. Adhikari,V. K. Adkins,S. Afroz,A. Agapito,D. Agarwal,M. Agathos,N. Aggarwal,S. Aggarwal,O. D. Aguiar,I. -L. Ahrend,L. Aiello,A. Ain,P. Ajith,T. Akutsu,L. Albers,W. Ali,S. Al-Kershi,C. Allene,A. Allocca,S. Al-Shammari,J. A. Alvarez,S. Alvarez-Lopez,W. Amar,O. Amarasinghe,A. Amato,F. Amicucci,C. Amra,A. B. Anand,C. Anand,A. Ananyeva,S. B. Anderson,W. G. Anderson,M. Andia,M. Ando,F. Andrade-Oliveira,M. Andrés-Carcasona,J. L. Andrey,T. Andrić,J. Anglin,J. Anna,J. M. Antelis,S. Antier,T. Aoki,M. Aoumi,E. Z. Appavuravther,E. A. Appelt,S. Appert,S. K. Apple,K. Arai,A. Araya,M. C. Araya,F. Arciprete,J. S. Areeda,N. Aritomi,F. Armato,S. Armstrong,N. Arnaud,M. Arogeti,S. M. Aronson,G. Ashton,Y. Aso,L. Asprea,M. Assiduo,S. Assis de Souza Melo,S. M. Aston,P. Astone,P. S. Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper reports no detection of an isotropic stochastic gravitational-wave background (SGWB) using LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA data up to April 1, 2025 (O4), implementing new frequency-domain noise cuts that improve upper limits by 40% over prior analyses; it sets stringent 95% upper limits on Ω_GW for standard and scale-invariant spectra, updates constraints on alternative gravity models, confirms magnetic noise is subdominant, and predicts the CBC foreground at Ω_CBC(25 Hz) = (6.3^{+5.0}_{-2.2}) × 10^{-10} (90% CL).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve sensitivity to the isotropic stochastic gravitational-wave background by mitigating previously unaddressed non-stationary spectral noise features in LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA data, especially from the fourth observing run (O4), and to refine astrophysical and cosmological interpretations—including CBC foreground predictions and alternative gravity tests.

Method: Frequency-domain data-quality cuts targeting non-stationary spectral noise; reprocessing of O1–O4 data (up to April 1, 2025) with updated cuts; Bayesian inference with log-uniform prior on Ω_GW to compute 95% credible upper limits; extension to non-standard polarization modes; cross-checks for correlated magnetic noise; forward modeling of compact binary coalescence (CBC) background using GWTC-5.0 population models.

Result: No statistically significant SGWB detection; Ω_GW(25 Hz) ≤ 2.0 × 10^{-9} (spectral index 2/3) and ≤ 2.8 × 10^{-9} (scale-invariant), both at 95% credibility; 40% improvement over reanalyzed prior limits; tightened bounds on alternative polarization modes; confirmed magnetic noise below analysis threshold; predicted CBC foreground Ω_CBC(25 Hz) = 6.3^{+5.0}_{-2.2} × 10^{-10} (90% CL).

Conclusion: The absence of a detected SGWB enables the most stringent direct upper limits to date on the isotropic gravitational-wave energy density; the improved methodology enhances robustness against non-Gaussian noise, strengthens constraints on early-universe and astrophysical sources, and refines expectations for future detections—particularly the CBC foreground, which remains below current limits but is now predicted with reduced uncertainty thanks to GWTC-5.0.

Abstract: We report results from a search for an isotropic stochastic gravitational-wave background using data collected by the LIGO--Virgo--KAGRA Collaboration. The analysis uses data from the first observing run through April 1, 2025, during the fourth observing run. New frequency-domain cuts are implemented to address a class of non-stationary spectral noise features that were not effectively identified and mitigated by existing data-quality checks in past analyses. Consequently, previously analyzed data from the fourth observing run are re-processed with the updated cuts. We find no evidence for a stochastic background signal and place upper limits on the gravitational-wave energy density. In particular, for a background following a power law with spectral index 2/3 as predicted by inspiralling compact binaries, we find $Ω_\mathrm{GW}(25\,\mathrm{Hz}) \leq 2.0 \times 10^{-9}$, while scale-invariant backgrounds are constrained to $Ω_\mathrm{GW}(25\,\mathrm{Hz}) \leq 2.8 \times 10^{-9}$, both at the 95\% credible level for a log-uniform prior on $Ω_\mathrm{GW}$. Relative to the constraints from previous data recomputed with the new frequency-domain cuts, these limits improve by a factor of 1.4. We also update bounds on alternative gravity scenarios predicting non-standard polarization modes, and we verify that correlated magnetic noise sources remain below the sensitivity of this search. Combining these observational constraints with population models of compact binary coalescences informed by the latest gravitational-wave transient catalog, GWTC-5.0, we predict the amplitude of the compact binary background to be $Ω_\mathrm{CBC}(25\,\mathrm{Hz}) = 6.3^{+5.0}_{-2.2} \times 10^{-10}$ at the 90\% credible level.

</details>


### [90] [Dynamical Love numbers of analogue rotating black and white holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23496)
*Satadal Datta,Wei-Can Syu,Da-Shin Lee*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes dynamical tidal response coefficients (TRCs) for 2+1D analogue black and white holes in draining/fountaining bathtub flows, identifying their real (tidal Love number) and imaginary (tidal dissipation coefficient) parts; it finds logarithmic running of the TLN at specific parameter points for analogue black holes, and shows that for an ABH-AWH pair sharing conserved quantities, their TRCs are complex conjugates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore tidal response in analogue gravity systems—specifically, whether tidal Love numbers and dissipation emerge in fluid-based black/white hole analogues—and to contrast their behavior with relativistic black holes, especially given the physical realizability of analogue white holes in fluid dynamics.

Method: Analytical computation of dynamical tidal response coefficients (TRCs) for 2+1D stationary, axisymmetric, barotropic, inviscid, transonic bathtub flow solutions; Fourier decomposition in time and azimuthal angle; identification of TRC’s real (TLN) and imaginary (TDC) parts; comparison between draining (analogue black hole) and fountaining (analogue white hole) configurations sharing angular momentum and Bernoulli constant.

Result: The TRC is generically complex; TLN shows logarithmic running at zero frequency and other special points, while TDC vanishes there; for an ABH-AWH pair with identical angular momentum and Bernoulli constant but opposite mass flow rates, their TRCs are complex conjugates.

Conclusion: Analogue black and white holes exhibit rich tidal response structure distinct from vacuum general relativity: TLN can run logarithmically, TDC vanishes in static limit, and AWHs—unlike GR white holes—are physically admissible and obey a symmetry (TRC conjugation) with their ABH counterparts under shared conserved quantities.

Abstract: We calculate the dynamical tidal response coefficients (TRCs) of 2+1D analogue black and white holes generated by draining and fountaining bathtub flows, respectively. The parameter space is characterized by the frequency and azimuthal number of the Fourier modes and the rotation of the analogue black or white hole. In general, the TRC is a complex-valued function of these parameters. Its real and imaginary parts are defined as the tidal Love number (TLN) and the tidal dissipation coefficient (TDC), respectively. The TRC of the analogue black hole (ABH) exhibits several interesting features. At certain points in the parameter space, including the static case of vanishing perturbation frequency, the TLN exhibits logarithmic running, while the TDC vanishes. Unlike in Einstein gravity, fluid dynamics allows for the physical existence of an analogue white hole (AWH) event horizon. Time-independent, torque-free, barotropic, inviscid, axisymmetric fluid dynamical equations can yield a pair of transonic background flow solutions. The solutions in the pair, corresponding to an ABH-AWH pair, share the same angular momentum and Bernoulli constant but have opposite mass flow rates, all of which are conserved quantities. For such an ABH-AWH pair, the TRC of the AWH is the complex conjugate of the TRC of the ABH.

</details>


### [91] [From arithmetic spectra to a quantum-corrected black hole geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23528)
*Kimet Jusufi,Ankit Anand*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes an 'arithmetic gas'—a free bosonic gas whose modes correspond to prime numbers—whose thermodynamics, via entropy–geometry correspondence, yields a quantum-corrected black hole metric. The Euler product structure of the Riemann zeta function underpins the system's Hagedorn behavior and fixes logarithmic corrections to the Bekenstein–Hawking area law; the pole at β=1 sets the coefficient, while nontrivial zeros contribute only negligible log-periodic ripples. The resulting metric is a regular, two-horizon black hole with bounded temperature, cold extremal remnant, and no electric charge—resembling Reissner–Nordström but sourced by positive-energy anisotropic matter. The Riemann Hypothesis is interpreted physically as minimality of arithmetic corrections to black-hole thermodynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To derive quantum gravitational corrections to black-hole geometry from fundamental number-theoretic structures—specifically, the Euler product of the Riemann zeta function—and to interpret the Riemann Hypothesis physically via black-hole thermodynamics.

Method: Constructs an 'arithmetic gas' whose partition function is the Euler product of ζ(β), identifies it as a Hagedorn system with entropy linear in energy (hence proportional to horizon area), applies entropy–geometry correspondence to reconstruct spacetime geometry, imposes asymptotic Schwarzschild matching and first-law consistency to fix parameters, and analyzes spectral contributions of zeta zeros to corrections.

Result: A closed-form, singularity-softened black-hole metric f(r) = 1 − 2GMr/(r² + ℓ²) with ℓ² = αG/π; it features two horizons, bounded Hawking temperature, cold extremal remnant, anisotropic positive-energy source satisfying the null energy condition, and logarithmic entropy correction with coefficient fixed by the zeta pole at β = 1; nontrivial zeros induce only exponentially suppressed log-periodic ripples in area.

Conclusion: The Euler product encodes quantum-gravitational degrees of freedom relevant to black-hole microstructure; the Riemann Hypothesis emerges as a natural minimality condition on arithmetic corrections to black-hole thermodynamics, and the nontrivial zeros are physically subdominant—consistent with their role as oscillatory corrections rather than leading-order contributors to entropy or geometry.

Abstract: The Euler product of the Riemann zeta function is the partition function of a free bosonic gas whose mode energies are the logarithms of the primes. We show that this arithmetic gas, combined with the assumption that the entropy--geometry correspondence holds, leads to a quantum-corrected black-hole metric. The prime gas is a Hagedorn system. Its entropy is linear in the energy, which is exactly what an entropy linear in the horizon area requires, and the simple pole of the zeta function at $β=1$ fixes the coefficient of the logarithmic correction to the area law. The nontrivial zeros cannot play this role: their level density grows only logarithmically, and far too slowly to be extensive. Demanding that the reconstructed geometry reduce to Schwarzschild at large radius then fixes the map from arithmetic energy to horizon area and yields the closed-form metric $f(r)=1-2GMr/(r^{2}+\lz^{2})$ with $\lz^{2}=αG/π$. This describes a two-horizon black hole with Reissner--Nordström horizon structure but no Coulombic hair, a positive-energy anisotropic source obeying the null energy condition, a softened central singularity, a bounded Hawking temperature, and a cold extremal remnant that ends the evaporation. The same length scale follows independently from requiring that the first law hold exactly with the corrected entropy. The nontrivial zeros survive only as exponentially suppressed log-periodic ripples in the area, which suggests a physical interpretation of the Riemann hypothesis as the statement that arithmetic corrections to black-hole thermodynamics are as small as they can be.

</details>


<div id='hep-th'></div>

# hep-th [[Back]](#toc)

### [92] [String stars, small black holes, and the supersymmetric index](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21511)
*Erez Y. Urbach*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explores the black hole–string transition near the supersymmetric index βΩ = 2πi, where standard high-temperature effective descriptions break down. It identifies distinct near-index regimes for Heterotic and Type II string theories—Higgsed supergravity with magnetic-field-induced W-boson condensation (Heterotic) and light winding strings enabling T-dual dimensional emergence (Type II)—both supporting decoupling string star solutions. Charged versions interpolate between black holes and free strings but fully decouple at the index, challenging smooth transitions and highlighting tension with BPS two-charge black hole existence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the breakdown of the Atick-Witten high-temperature effective description near the supersymmetric index βΩ = 2πi, and to characterize the emergent physics governing the black hole–string transition in that regime.

Method: Analytical study of near-supersymmetric-index limits in Heterotic and Type II string theories; construction and analysis of string star solutions; examination of charged configurations and their thermodynamic/phase behavior; use of dualities (e.g., T-duality) and effective supergravity descriptions (including Higgsing and magnetic backgrounds).

Result: 1) Near βΩ = 2πi, Heterotic theory admits a Higgsed SU(2)_L × U(1)_R supergravity description with magnetic-field-driven Ambjorn-Olesen W-boson condensation (Hagedorn instability); 2) Type II theory exhibits a light winding-string tower, opening a new dimension in the T-dual picture; 3) Both yield decoupling string star solutions analogous to Horowitz-Polchinski but vanishing at the index; 4) Charged string stars interpolate between black holes and free strings, yet all charged phases decouple precisely at βΩ = 2πi.

Conclusion: A smooth black hole–string transition is incompatible with the existence of a stable, stringy BPS two-charge black hole at the supersymmetric index, due to universal decoupling of both black hole and string star phases at βΩ = 2πi.

Abstract: We discuss aspects of the black hole-string transition as the angular velocity approaches the supersymmetric index $βΩ= 2πi$. In this limit, the high-temperature Atick-Witten effective description breaks down. For Heterotic string theory, a new high-temperature description emerges near the index in terms of a Higgsed $SU(2)_L\times U(1)_R$ supergravity with a magnetic field profile. The Hagedorn instability manifests in the theory as Ambjorn-Olesen W boson condensation, due to the background magnetic field. For type II, a tower of winding strings becomes light near the index, which opens up a new dimension in the T-dual picture. In both cases, a string star solution is found, similar to the Horowitz-Polchinski solution, but decouples at the index $βΩ= 2πi$.
  Considering charged solutions, the string star appears to interpolate between charged black holes and the free string phase. At the index, both the charged black hole and the charged string star phases decouple. This suggests a tension between a smooth black hole-string transition and the existence of a stringy BPS two-charge black hole.

</details>


### [93] [What Neural Network Field Theory Can and Cannot Realise on a Computer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21523)
*Thomas R. Harvey*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves a no-go theorem showing that standard finite-width neural network ensembles cannot consistently realize either quantum field theories (violating reflection positivity) or effective field theories (lacking scale separation); only infinite-width limits are viable, but even then only smeared correlators are computable with controlled error—making QFT and EFT indistinguishable numerically; 1D is exceptional but still fails reflection positivity for cosine networks; two possible escapes are relaxing pointwise finite variance or exact rotation invariance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the feasibility of implementing quantum or effective field theories (QFT/EFT) via neural network ensembles—treating the network ensemble itself as the field theory—and determine under which conditions such a 'neural network field theory' is mathematically and computationally consistent.

Method: Rigorous analysis of reflection positivity and scale separation in neural network ensembles, distinguishing finite-width vs. infinite-width regimes and QFT vs. EFT interpretations; explicit evaluation of reflection positivity for the cosine network in one dimension; identification of minimal assumptions underlying the no-go result and exploration of potential relaxations.

Result: A no-go theorem: finite-width neural network ensembles with pointwise finite variance cannot satisfy reflection positivity (ruling out QFT interpretation) nor provide scale separation (undermining EFT validity); infinite-width limits permit simulation only of smeared correlators with controlled error, erasing numerical distinction between QFT and EFT; 1D evades dimensional obstructions but still violates reflection positivity at all finite widths for cosine networks; two viable escapes are abandoning finite variance at points or exact rotation invariance.

Conclusion: Standard neural network ensembles do not admit a consistent, computationally controlled realization of either QFT or EFT unless structural assumptions—such as finite pointwise variance or exact symmetry—are relaxed; the infinite-width limit is necessary but insufficient for full field-theoretic interpretation, as only coarse-grained observables are accessible.

Abstract: One aim of neural network field theory is to put a quantum or effective field theory on a computer, with the network ensemble itself as the theory. We ask how far that aim can be pushed for a function class regular enough to be computed with. Our main result is a no-go theorem with assumptions that hold for standard network architectures. We use it to separate four versions of neural network field theory, according to whether the defining object is the finite width ensemble or its infinite width limit, and whether the target we want to compute is a quantum or an effective field theory. Neither finite width interpretation is straightforwardly consistent. For finite width ensembles with finite variance at each point, the QFT interpretation fails reflection positivity, while the EFT interpretation establishes no scale separation by which the positivity violation can be placed outside its domain of validity. Of the two limit versions, one can be simulated in full and the other only in part, as only its smeared correlators are computable with a controlled error. As such, at the level of a controlled numerical computation, the QFT and EFT versions cannot be distinguished. One dimension escapes the obstruction, yet reflection positivity is shown to still fail there at every finite width for the cosine network. Two escapes from the theorem remain, giving up either finite variance at a point or exact rotation invariance, and we discuss both of these possibilities.

</details>


### [94] [On flat space limits of $AdS_4$ black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21518)
*Pedro Vicente Marto,Shradha Ramakrishnan,Stefan Vandoren*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the flat space limit of asymptotically AdS₄ black holes in N=2 gauged supergravity to probe flat space holography; it analyzes the on-shell action via holographic renormalization, identifies divergences (thermodynamic volume) for large black holes, shows semiclassical agreement for small black holes, maps BPS/extremal/non-extremal solutions across AdS and flat space, and derives a charged BPS flat-space black hole from M-theory, matching its entropy to a Carrollian-like limit of the ABJM partition function on S¹×S².


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To advance flat space holography by systematically taking the flat space limit of asymptotically AdS₄ black holes in N=2 gauged supergravity and identifying dual boundary descriptions.

Method: Holographic renormalization of the on-shell gravitational action; careful analysis of flat space limits for various black hole classes (large/small, BPS, extremal, rotating, charged); derivation of flat-space BPS solution from M-theory reduction; Carrollian-like limit of ABJM partition function on S¹×S² at large N.

Result: Large black holes induce thermodynamic volume divergence in the partition function; small black holes semiclassically reproduce flat-space partition functions; explicit mapping established between BPS/extremal/non-extremal AdS and flat black holes; a new charged BPS flat-space black hole is obtained; its entropy matches the Carrollian limit of the ABJM partition function.

Conclusion: The flat space limit preserves key holographic structure: semiclassical agreement holds for small black holes, BPS sectors map consistently, and the ABJM partition function admits a physically meaningful Carrollian limit encoding flat-space black hole entropy—supporting a viable flat space holographic duality in this setting.

Abstract: With a view towards understanding flat space holography, we study the flat space limit of asymptotically $AdS_4$ black holes in gauged $\mathcal{N}=2$ supergravity. Using holographic renormalisation, we carefully analyse the flat space limit of the on-shell action of various black holes in this setting and make explicit the states that contribute to the partition function in this limit. We find several features: large black holes lead to a divergence in the partition function which can be understood as a thermodynamic volume divergence. Meanwhile, semiclassically, small black holes reproduce the partition function of their flat space counterparts, and we clarify the mapping between BPS, (near-)extremal or non-extremal black holes on both sides for charged and rotating solutions. Finally, by studying a suitable flat limit of the BPS Kerr-Newman-$AdS_4$ black hole in M-theory, we obtain a charged BPS solution in flat space. On the dual side, we determine a Carrollian-like limit on the known ABJM partition function on $S^1\times S^2$ at large $N$, and show that it reproduces the correct entropy of the BPS charged black hole in asymptotically flat space.

</details>


### [95] [Neural Network Field Theory at Finite Width](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21588)
*Christian Ferko,Aaron Mutchler*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper shows that quantum mechanical and quantum field theories can be represented by ensembles of neural networks with random parameters, but finite-width neural networks generically fail to satisfy key axiomatic properties of Euclidean QFTs—e.g., reflection positivity or cluster decomposition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the representational capacity and limitations of neural networks in modeling quantum theories, particularly whether and how fundamental physical principles (e.g., reflection positivity, cluster decomposition) emerge—or break—in neural-network parameterizations of QM/QFT.

Method: Analytical investigation of neural network representations of QM and QFT under mild assumptions; examination of finitely-parameterized models (e.g., feedforward networks of finite width N); use of axiomatic Euclidean QFT criteria to assess preservation of physical structure.

Result: Finite-N neural network models generically violate at least one foundational property of Euclidean QFTs—specifically, reflection positivity or cluster decomposition—while infinite-random-parameter ensembles can recover full QM/QFT structure.

Conclusion: Neural networks can formally encode quantum theories only in the limit of infinite or stochastic parameters; finite-parameter NN-QM/NN-QFT models are inherently approximate and sacrifice core physical axioms, revealing a fundamental trade-off between parametric efficiency and physical fidelity.

Abstract: Under mild assumptions, any quantum mechanical (QM) model or quantum field theory (QFT) admits a representation in terms of an ensemble of neural networks with countably many random parameters. We investigate the features of NN-QM and NN-FT models with finitely many parameters, such as a feedforward network of width $N < \infty$. We find that, generically, such models must violate one of the properties of conventional Euclidean QFTs, such as reflection positivity or cluster decomposition. We present several complementary ways of understanding which features can and cannot be preserved at finite $N$, both in QM and in QFT.

</details>


### [96] [One-Loop Quantum Correction to the Kink Mass in a Lorentz-Violating Scalar Field Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21613)
*Reza Moazzemi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the one-loop radiative correction to the kink mass in a (1+1)D Lorentz-violating λφ⁴ theory with a specific spacelike cφ²(u·∂φ)² operator, requiring both mass and field-strength renormalization; it finds a finite, numerically negative Lorentz-violating contribution to the kink mass.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how irreducible spacelike Lorentz violation affects topological soliton properties—specifically the kink mass—in 2D scalar field theory, beyond what can be removed by field or coordinate redefinitions.

Method: One-loop quantum field theoretic calculation: renormalization of the Lorentz-violating λφ⁴ model; spectral analysis of the corrected kink fluctuation operator (bound/continuum modes, phase shift); vacuum-subtracted zero-point energy evaluation with counterterm Hamiltonian; use of a boundary-condition prescription preserving mode structure at O(c).

Result: The Lorentz-violating interaction necessitates both a modified mass counterterm and a nonzero field-strength counterterm; continuum phase shift is unchanged at O(c); the one-loop kink mass receives a finite, numerically negative correction linear in the Lorentz-violation parameter c.

Conclusion: Irreducible Lorentz violation qualitatively alters the renormalization structure and soliton mass in 2D φ⁴ theory, yielding a calculable, physical (finite, scheme-dependent but unambiguous within the adopted subtraction) negative shift to the kink mass at O(c).

Abstract: We study the radiative correction to the mass of the $(1+1)$-dimensional $λφ^4$ kink in the presence of an irreducible, spacelike Lorentz-violating interaction. The Lorentz-violating operator is chosen as $-\frac12 cφ^2(u\!\cdot\!\partialφ)^2$ with $u^μ=(0,1)$, which is the lowest-order single-scalar operator considered in this class of models that produces effects that cannot be removed by a linear redefinition of coordinates or the field. We perform the one-loop renormalization of the underlying quantum field theory. In contrast with Lorentz-invariant $λφ^4$ theory in two dimensions, the Lorentz-violating theory requires, at this order, both a modified mass counterterm and a nonzero field-strength counterterm. The corrected stability problem contains modified bound and continuum modes, while the continuum phase shift remains unchanged at $O(c)$ with the boundary-condition prescription adopted here. Finally, using the vacuum-subtracted zero-point energy and the counterterm Hamiltonian, we obtain the one-loop kink mass through first order in $c$. We give the finite coefficient in the subtraction prescription implemented in the accompanying thesis calculation, for which the Lorentz-violating contribution is numerically negative.

</details>


### [97] [Heterotic string couplings at order $α'^3$ in NS-NS sector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21798)
*Alireza Pahlavan,Mehdi Ameri,Mohammad R. Garousi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the eight-derivative effective action of heterotic string theory in the NS-NS sector using T-duality constraints, dimensional reduction, and field redefinitions; it fixes couplings relative to type II and reproduces known S-matrix results in the Meissner scheme, unifying gravity and Yang-Mills terms as (Tr(R² − F²))².


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the complete, T-duality-invariant eight-derivative effective action for heterotic string theory in the NS-NS sector — a long-standing problem requiring consistency with both string amplitudes and duality symmetries.

Method: Standard T-duality procedure applied to dimensional reduction on a circle; imposition of Buscher rules with higher-derivative corrections; elimination of redundant couplings via field redefinitions; comparison against Metsaev-Tseytlin and Meissner schemes; canonicalization to isolate dilaton dependence as e^{−2Φ}.

Result: T-duality uniquely fixes part of the 1349 total couplings (872 even + 477 odd parity) to match type II up to normalization; remaining couplings are expressed in terms of α′-coefficients; pure gravity terms reproduce S-matrix results in Meissner scheme; full NS-NS action admits unified (Tr(R² − F²))² form when Yang-Mills included.

Conclusion: The eight-derivative NS-NS effective action of heterotic strings is fully constrained by T-duality and field redefinition freedom; its structure confirms expected unification between curvature-squared and gauge-field-squared contributions, supporting the (Tr(R² − F²))² conjecture and validating the Meissner scheme against scattering amplitudes.

Abstract: We utilize the standard T-duality procedure to derive the classical effective action of heterotic string theory at the eight-derivative order within the NS-NS sector, which comprises the metric, the \(B\)-field, and the dilaton. Starting from the minimal basis at this order, consisting of 872 even-parity and 477 odd-parity couplings, we perform a dimensional reduction on a circle and impose invariance under T-duality transformations, specifically the Buscher rules supplemented by higher-derivative corrections. This invariance uniquely fixes a subset of the couplings to match those of type II theory up to an overall factor, while the remaining couplings are determined in terms of the coefficients at order \(α'\). For these latter couplings, we adopt both the Metsaev-Tseytlin and Meissner schemes. Subsequently, through field redefinitions, we recast the action into a canonical form in which the dilaton appears solely through the overall factor \(e^{-2Φ}\). In the Meissner scheme, the pure gravity sector precisely reproduces the known S-matrix results. In both schemes, the pure gravity terms can be expressed as the double trace \((\Tr(R^2))^2\), and when combined with the corresponding Yang-Mills terms \((\Tr(F^2))^2\), they take the unified form \((\Tr(R^2-F^2))^2\), as anticipated in the literature.

</details>


### [98] [Characteristic-cone running from on-shell residues: Identifiability on the physical EFT quotient](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21865)
*Renat Gafarov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a data-completeness test for extracting low-energy characteristic-cone running from flat-space on-shell renormalization-group residues in a 4D parity-even Einstein–scalar–Maxwell EFT up to four derivatives and four fields; it computes specific beta-function coefficients for vector–scalar residues, identifies incompleteness via finite operator registry tests (e.g., $F^4$ and $\mathcal{C}FF$), and shows that the scalar–photon contact residue fixes only one diagonal cone contribution—not the full characteristic response.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether on-shell renormalization-group residues in gravitational effective field theories fully determine the low-energy characteristic-cone evolution—i.e., whether the extracted data is 'complete' for reconstructing physical response across the admissible Wilson coefficient space.

Method: Formulates a data-completeness test using on-shell amplitude coordinates and response kernels; projects Wilson directions onto the physical EFT quotient; models background propagation via traceless response on the massless root sector; applies Schur reduction and Maxwell Ward identities for contact-term analysis; performs frame translations and helicity-specific residue extraction in Einstein–scalar–Maxwell EFT through four derivatives.

Result: Computes $\\beta_\\mu C_{\\gamma\\phi}^{(2)} = -55/(96\\pi^2)$ and $\\beta_\\mu(r_T - r_\\gamma) = -55/(48\\pi^2 M_{\\rm P}^4)$; identifies rank-two incompleteness: $F^4$ splits photon polarizations on magnetic backgrounds, $\\mathcal{C}FF$ induces analytic splitting on Petrov-I Einstein–scalar backgrounds; finds nonzero raw mixing but vanishing first-order physical off-diagonal entry for scalar–photon contact, implying residue fixes only one diagonal cone contribution.

Conclusion: The on-shell vector–scalar residue provides necessary but insufficient data to reconstruct the term-complete characteristic response over the declared coefficient domain—highlighting structural incompleteness in current amplitude-based RG residue extraction methods for gravitational EFTs.

Abstract: We formulate a data-completeness test for extracting low-energy characteristic-cone running from flat-space on-shell renormalization-group residues. In four-dimensional, parity-even Einstein--scalar--Maxwell effective field theory through four derivatives and four external fields, Wilson directions are first placed on the physical EFT quotient and background propagation is represented by a traceless response on the full massless root sector. The response is identifiable from chosen amplitude coordinates precisely when the amplitude kernel lies in the response kernel. For the published opposite-helicity vector--scalar residue we find $β_μC_{γφ}^{(2)}=-55/(96π^2)$ and, after an order-reduced frame translation, the contribution $β_μ(r_T-r_γ)=-55/(48π^2M_{\rm P}^4)$. A finite operator registry gives explicit rank-two incompleteness tests: independent $F^4$ directions split photon polarizations on a magnetic background, while $\mathcal CFF$ produces an analytic splitting along a principal Weyl axis on a Petrov-I Einstein--scalar background. For the isolated scalar--photon contact, direct Schur reduction finds nonzero raw mixing but no first-order physical off-diagonal entry, by the Maxwell Ward identity. Thus the residue fixes one diagonal cone contribution but does not determine the term-complete characteristic response over the declared admissible coefficient domain.

</details>


### [99] [Perturbative Reconstruction of Self-Adjoint Generators from Bosonic Canonical Commutation Relations: Application to the Null-Surface Formulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21947)
*C. N. Kozameh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper solves an inverse adjoint problem for a perturbative bosonic outgoing map by proving a constructive integrability theorem in the polynomial CCR algebra: order-by-order CCR preservation suffices to recursively reconstruct a formal self-adjoint generator; applied to graviton scattering in the null-surface formulation (flat sector), it yields unitary generators δT₁, δT₂ at O(ε²), fixes symmetric ordering (c=1), cancels double-contraction anomalies, and organizes 2→2 scattering into irreducible-loop, 1PR, and factorized sectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous, constructive framework for perturbative unitarity in quantum field theory—specifically for gravitational scattering—by reversing the adjoint action: given a CCR-preserving outgoing map, reconstruct its self-adjoint generator rather than assuming one a priori.

Method: Constructive recursive procedure within the polynomial bosonic CCR algebra: at each perturbative order, subtract Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff terms from the outgoing map’s coefficient, then solve the residual homogeneous CCR conditions to uniquely determine the next self-adjoint generator; applied to Ashtekar’s radiative CCRs in the null-surface formulation of gravity with Minkowski background.

Result: Proof of a constructive integrability theorem guaranteeing recursive reconstruction of formal self-adjoint generators δT₁ and δT₂; explicit determination of symmetric ordering (c = 1) for the cubic interaction kernel; cancellation of ([δa₂, δa₂†]) double-contraction via linear contraction; canonical decomposition of the 2→2 amplitude into irreducible-loop, one-particle-reducible, and factorized sectors.

Conclusion: Order-by-order CCR preservation is not only necessary but sufficient for formal perturbative unitarity via adjoint conjugation; the framework provides a model-independent, algebraic route to constructing unitary S-maps in background-free or asymptotically flat quantum gravity settings.

Abstract: We study the inverse adjoint problem for a perturbative bosonic outgoing map. Within the polynomial bosonic canonical-commutation-relation (CCR) algebra, we prove a constructive integrability theorem: order-by-order preservation of the CCRs is sufficient to reconstruct recursively a formal self-adjoint generator whose exponential implements the outgoing map by adjoint conjugation. At each order, the Baker--Campbell--Hausdorff contribution determined by previously reconstructed generators is subtracted from the outgoing coefficient, and the remaining homogeneous CCR conditions determine the next generator explicitly.
  We apply the construction to graviton scattering in the null-surface formulation (NSF), specialized to the flat solution-space sector with Minkowski metric, null generators, affine parameter, and cone measure. The NSF outgoing map preserves the Ashtekar radiative CCRs through (δa_3), corresponding to order (\varepsilon^2). At this order, the scalar CCR sector uniquely selects the symmetric ordering (c=1) for the nontrivial interacting kernel. The associated linear contraction, generated by the same cubic kernel, cancels the double-contraction contribution from ([δa_2,δa_2^\dagger]). The theorem then reconstructs the self-adjoint generators (δT_1) and (δT_2), providing a formal perturbative unitary implementation. The resulting canonical organization also separates the direct (2\to2) contribution into irreducible-loop, one-particle-reducible, and factorized sectors.

</details>


### [100] [Holographic SU(3) color superconductivity at finite baryon chemical potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22013)
*Xin Zhao,Zhang-Yu Nie,Ya-Peng Hu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a holographic SU(3) color superconductivity model featuring spontaneous global color symmetry breaking via a diquark condensate at finite baryon chemical potential μ_b; it identifies an S1+iS2 order parameter, shows μ_b enhances its Tc while suppressing p-wave (which vanishes beyond a critical μ_b with backreaction), and finds the balanced S+ phase dominates the CSC region.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a concrete holographic description of global SU(3) color symmetry breaking relevant for dense QCD and neutron-star interiors, extending beyond established SU(2) s+p competition to finite baryon density.

Method: Holographic duality construction with a bulk scalar dual to a diquark operator; systematic stability analysis over all SU(3) channels at μ_b = 0; inclusion of backreaction to study finite-μ_b thermodynamics and phase structure; identification and characterization of s-wave (S1+iS2) and p-wave superconducting phases.

Result: At μ_b = 0, recovery of known SU(2) s+p competition; at finite μ_b, emergence of S1+iS2 s-wave order; μ_b increases critical temperature Tc of S1+iS2 phase while suppressing p-wave (which disappears beyond a critical μ_b when backreaction is included); resulting phase diagram features dominance of the balanced S+ phase (S1 = S2) across the entire color-superconducting region.

Conclusion: The holographic SU(3) CSC model successfully captures non-Abelian color symmetry breaking at finite density, predicts a qualitatively distinct phase structure driven by opposing μ_b dependence of s- and p-wave instabilities, and offers a tractable framework for studying color-superconducting phases in astrophysical contexts.

Abstract: We present a holographic model of SU(3) color superconductivity (CSC) where a global color symmetry is spontaneously broken by a diquark condensate at finite baryon chemical potential. At $μ_b=0$, a systematic stability analysis over all SU(3) channels recovers the known SU(2) s+p competition. Based on that, the finite $μ_b$ further generalizes the s-wave order to an S1+iS2 structure. We find that $μ_b$ enhances the critical temperature of the S1+iS2 phase while suppressing the p-wave -- the latter vanishes beyond a certain $μ_b$ when backreaction is included. This opposite trend reshapes the phase diagram: with increasing $μ_b$, the balanced S+ phase (S1=S2) takes over the entire color-superconducting region. Our non-Abelian framework provides a concrete holographic description of global SU(3) symmetry breaking, relevant for understanding color-superconducting phases in dense QCD and may offer new insights into the physics of neutron-star interiors.

</details>


### [101] [Thermal propagator of the bosons and fermions fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22469)
*M. A. A. Ahmed,H. Zainuddin,N. M. Shah*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs scalar and fermion propagators in thermal field theory using a real-time formalism with an arbitrary parameter σ, enabling analysis of particle propagation in momentum and mixed space at finite temperature without chemical potential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To distinguish thermal field theory from classical statistical mechanics by rigorously analyzing quantum propagators in a thermodynamic environment, specifically addressing real-time dynamics at finite temperature.

Method: Uses real-time formalism (RTF) with an arbitrary parameter σ to construct scalar and fermion propagators via path integral methods, evaluating propagation in both momentum space and mixed (coordinate–momentum) space.

Result: Explicit expressions for scalar and fermion propagators at finite temperature and zero chemical potential, characterizing particle propagation behavior in both momentum and mixed representations.

Conclusion: The σ-parameterized RTF framework successfully captures quantum statistical propagation features distinct from classical statistical mechanics, providing a consistent real-time description of thermal quantum fields.

Abstract: In a thermodynamic environment, thermal field theory (TFT) describes a large ensemble of interacting particles. This may appear to be the same as in classical statistical mechanics. Therefore, we study the scalar propagator and fermion propagator by considering an arbitrary parameter $σ$ that allows for a path integral description in real-time formalism (RTF). We constructed those propagators which allowed us to know how particles moved from one point to another point in momentum space as well as in mixed space, at finite temperature without a chemical potential.

</details>


### [102] [A second rotational Killing field on gauged D=5 vector multiplet horizons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22509)
*Usman Kayani*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper classifies supersymmetric near-horizon geometries in 5D gauged supergravity with vector multiplets, under the assumption that the canonical rotational Killing vector on the horizon section is non-vanishing. It proves existence of a second rotational Killing field on compact horizons, distinguishes two moduli sub-branches (constant vs. varying), recovers known geometries as consequences of supersymmetry+compactness, constructs explicit solutions, and excludes hidden Killing-tensor symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify supersymmetric near-horizon geometries in D=5 gauged supergravity with vector multiplets—beyond minimal supergravity—without assuming global rotational symmetry, and to understand the role of moduli dynamics and isometry enhancement on compact horizons.

Method: Analysis of the first-order Killing spinor equations and near-horizon geometry constraints; use of $SU(2)$-structure formalism; study of critical points of $\|η_-\|^2$; investigation of closure conditions ($dP = 0$) and integrability of associated flat rank-two systems; explicit construction and numerical verification of horizon metrics.

Result: 1) A second rotational Killing field always exists on the open set where $Z$ and $W$ are linearly independent and extends globally under mild conditions. 2) Two disjoint sub-branches emerge: $P \equiv 0$ (constant moduli, homogeneous horizons, recovering Kunduri–Lucietti local geometries); and $P \not\equiv 0$ (non-constant moduli, solved explicitly via $U = \|η_-\|^2(h_Y N - h_N Y)$). 3) $K = Q_{IJ} C^I C^J$ is either identically zero or strictly positive; hidden Killing-tensor symmetry is excluded. 4) Both sub-branches are realized by explicit compact examples: $U(1)^3$ black holes (first) and a non-static Kunduri–Lucietti horizon with $P$ vanishing at two points (second, numerically verifying extension hypothesis).

Conclusion: Supersymmetry and compactness alone enforce enhanced isometry in 5D gauged supergravity near-horizon geometries—even without assuming symmetry—splitting into two physically distinct moduli branches. The analysis unifies and generalizes prior results, confirms robustness of the $U(1)^2$ isometry, and leaves open only a narrow black-ring parameter window for possible non-isometric solutions.

Abstract: We study supersymmetric near-horizon geometries of gauged D=5 supergravity coupled to vector multiplets, on the branch where the canonical rotational Killing vector $\tilde V$ of the horizon section $\mathcal{S}$ is non-vanishing. No rotational symmetry is assumed. On a compact, connected $\mathcal{S}$ without boundary, a second rotational Killing field, independent of $\tilde V$, always exists on the open set where $Z$ and $W$ are linearly independent, and extends to an isometry of all of $\mathcal{S}$, unconditionally if the moduli are constant and under a checkable extension hypothesis otherwise.
  With $K=Q_{IJ}C^IC^J$ vanishing exactly in the minimal theory, $K\equiv0$ recovers the known result of Grover, Gutowski, Papadopoulos and Sabra. When $K>0$, $P=Z+W$ vanishes at every critical point of $\|η_-\|^2$, and two sub-branches occur, both realised. On the first, $P\equiv0$, the moduli are constant and the isometry follows by homogeneity, recovering Kunduri and Lucietti local geometries as a consequence of supersymmetry and compactness rather than an ansatz. On the second, $P\not\equiv0$, the moduli genuinely vary; closure of the first-order system ($dP=0$) turns the Killing equation for a vector orthogonal to $P$ into a flat rank-two system transported along $P$, solved explicitly by $U=\|η_-\|^2(h_YN-h_NY)$.
  Both sub-branches are non-empty on compact $\mathcal{S}$: known $U(1)^3$ black holes populate the first, and an explicit compact horizon from the non-static Kunduri-Lucietti family, with $P$ vanishing at two points, populates the second and verifies the extension hypothesis numerically. We also prove $K$ is identically zero or everywhere positive, exclude a hidden Killing-tensor symmetry, and leave the $S^1\times S^2$ black-ring window $\tfrac13Φ^2<χ^2K\leq\tfrac43Φ^2$ unexcluded on either sub-branch.

</details>


### [103] [Genus-Two Contributions to Late-Time Correlators in JT Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22609)
*Masayoshi Sato*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the genus-two contribution to the late-time two-point correlation function in JT gravity by modeling two baby universes via a Hartle–Hawking state amplitude, reducing it to a Weil–Petersson moduli integral, decomposing $V_{0,3}$ using Kontsevich graphs, enforcing the no-shortcut condition on strip geometries, and evaluating finite geometric contributions within the strip approximation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend beyond the single-baby-universe (genus-one) analysis and systematically compute higher-genus (specifically genus-two) contributions to late-time correlation functions in Jackiw–Teitelboim gravity, which encode multi-wormhole physics and quantum gravitational effects in low-dimensional holography.

Method: Formulates a Hartle–Hawking amplitude for emission/absorption of two baby universes; reduces the genus-two correlation function to a moduli-space integral over $\mathcal{M}_{0,3}$ weighted by the Weil–Petersson volume $V_{0,3}$; decomposes $V_{0,3}$ using Kontsevich graphs; imposes the no-shortcut condition on genus-two strip geometries; applies the strip approximation to convert the integral into a finite sum over distinct geometric configurations; evaluates explicit contributions from two-baby-universe emission topologies.
