Result: Explicit analytic evaluation of the genus-two late-time two-point correlation function arising from two baby universes; demonstration that the strip approximation + no-shortcut condition yields a finite, tractable sum over geometric configurations; validation of this framework as suitable for higher-genus multi-wormhole calculations in JT gravity.

Conclusion: The combined use of the strip approximation and the no-shortcut condition provides a robust and computationally effective analytic framework for probing multi-wormhole contributions—particularly at genus two—in Jackiw–Teitelboim gravity, paving the way for systematic higher-genus analyses of correlation functions and quantum gravitational information processing.

Abstract: In this work, we analyze the late-time two-point correlation function in Jackiw--Teitelboim gravity, focusing on the genus-two ($g=2$) contribution associated with two baby universes. Extending the analysis of the single-baby-universe ($g=1$) case, we formulate an amplitude describing the emission and absorption of two baby universes during the evolution of the Hartle--Hawking state. This formulation reduces the problem to a moduli-space integral involving the Weil--Petersson volume $V_{0,3}$. To evaluate this integral, we decompose $V_{0,3}$ into contributions from Kontsevich graphs and extend the no-shortcut condition to the relevant genus-two strip geometries. Combined with the strip approximation, this decomposition reorganizes the moduli-space integral into a finite sum of contributions associated with distinct geometric configurations. We then explicitly evaluate the strip-geometry contributions corresponding to the emission of two baby universes and determine their contribution to the genus-two late-time two-point correlation function. Our results suggest that the strip approximation, together with the no-shortcut condition, provides a useful analytic framework for studying higher-genus geometries containing multiple wormholes.

</details>


### [104] [Higher-spin charges and the $L_Λ w_{1 + \infty}$ algebra in (A)dS$_4$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22625)
*Lorenzo Di Giacomo,Maitá Micol,Daniele Pranzetti,Ana-Maria Raclariu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs an infinite tower of higher-spin charges in (3+1)-dimensional asymptotically locally (A)dS spacetimes, showing they realize a Λ-deformed w_{1+∞} algebra; it also identifies curved-space analogs of conformally soft gravitons as boundary primaries and links their Poisson brackets to the celestial OPE, thereby extending the celestial holography dictionary to (A)dS₄.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the celestial holography framework—previously developed for asymptotically flat gravity—to asymptotically (A)dS spacetimes, and to uncover the bulk gravitational origin of the recently proposed Λ-deformed w_{1+∞} symmetry in celestial CFT.

Method: Perturbative asymptotic expansion of Einstein equations in cosmological constant Λ; construction of higher-spin charges as conserved quantities at infinity; canonical analysis on restricted gravitational phase space; identification of conformally soft graviton modes as boundary primaries under sl(2,R); computation of Poisson brackets with quadratic charges to recover celestial OPE.

Result: 1) Infinite tower of Λ-perturbative higher-spin charges satisfying the L_Λ w_{1+∞} algebra canonically; 2) Explicit spacetime-based definition of (A)dS₄ conformally soft gravitons as sl(2,R)-primaries; 3) Poisson brackets between soft gravitons and quadratic charges reproduce Taylor–Zhu’s Λ-deformed celestial OPE.

Conclusion: The L_Λ w_{1+∞} symmetry has a concrete bulk gravitational realization in asymptotically (A)dS₄ gravity, and the celestial bulk-boundary dictionary is successfully generalized beyond flat space.

Abstract: We consider $(3+1)$-dimensional asymptotically locally (anti-)de Sitter ((A)dS$_4$) spacetimes with boundary conditions allowing for gravitational flux. We construct an infinite tower of higher-spin charges as perturbative solutions in the cosmological constant $Λ$ to a hierarchy of evolution equations resulting from an asymptotic expansion of the Einstein equations. We show that these charges canonically realize the $Λ$-deformed $w_{1+\infty}$ algebra ($L_Λ w_{1+\infty}$) on a restricted gravitational phase space, thereby extending the higher-spin symmetry structure of asymptotically flat spacetimes to asymptotically (A)dS spacetimes. We further construct the curved-space counterparts of conformally soft gravitons directly in terms of spacetime data. We show that they are primaries with respect to an $\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{R})$ subalgebra of the (A)dS$_4$ isometry algebra and that their Poisson brackets with the quadratic higher-spin charges reproduce the $Λ$-deformed celestial operator product expansion proposed by Taylor and Zhu. Our results establish the bulk gravitational origin of the $L_Λw_{1+\infty}$ symmetry and extend the celestial bulk-boundary dictionary to asymptotically (A)dS$_4$ gravity.

</details>


### [105] [Towards Perturbative Unimodular Poincaré Gauge Theories with Propagating Torsion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22669)
*Arthur F. Vieira,Antonio D. Pereira,Reinhard Alkofer*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the one-loop equivalence between Poincaré gauge gravity with propagating torsion and its unimodular counterpart, showing identical quantum determinants—and hence identical local logarithmic divergences—on both maximally symmetric (torsionless) and flat (homogeneous torsion) backgrounds; the unimodular constraint does not alter local torsion dynamics at one loop, though the equivalence breaks down for generic curved torsionful backgrounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether imposing the unimodular constraint in Poincaré gauge theories of gravity with dynamical torsion alters quantum (one-loop) predictions—specifically, whether torsion-dependent UV divergences and effective dynamics differ between diffeomorphism-invariant and genuine unimodular formulations.

Method: Computes and compares one-loop functional determinants of quadratic torsion fluctuations in two representative torsion sectors (axial and vector components of hook-antisymmetric torsion) within the Einstein representation; analyzes both torsionless maximally symmetric backgrounds and flat backgrounds with homogeneous axial/vector torsion; distinguishes between unimodular gauge fixing and genuine unimodular theory formulation.

Result: Identical one-loop determinants and local logarithmic divergences in both torsion sectors across all considered backgrounds; torsion-dependent effective actions are governed by the same physical determinant in unimodular gauge and genuine unimodular theory; equivalence holds only for specific backgrounds—not generic curved torsionful ones.

Conclusion: The unimodular constraint does not modify local one-loop torsion dynamics for the studied actions and backgrounds; however, this equivalence is background-dependent and fails for generic torsionful curved spacetimes, indicating limitations in extending unimodular quantization to full dynamical torsion gravity.

Abstract: We study the one-loop relation between an effective-field-theory extension of Poincaré gauge theories of gravity with propagating torsion and its unimodular counterpart. In the Einstein representation, we consider two representative quadratic torsion subsectors, namely totally antisymmetric (axial) torsion and the vector component of hook-antisymmetric torsion. On maximally symmetric metric backgrounds with vanishing background torsion, the diffeomorphism-invariant and genuine unimodular theories yield identical one-loop determinants in both sectors, reproducing the same local logarithmic divergences. We further examine flat backgrounds with homogeneous axial or vector torsion, where metric-torsion mixing is present. In each case, the torsion-dependent one-loop effective action in unimodular gauge and in the genuine unimodular formulation is controlled by the same physical determinant. Thus, for the actions and backgrounds considered, the unimodular constraint does not modify local one-loop torsion dynamics. We also delineate the limitations of this equivalence and the extensions needed for generic torsionful curved backgrounds.

</details>


### [106] [Renormalization, cutoff, and gluing for a quartic model with boundary](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23069)
*A. V. Ivanov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates renormalization of a 3D quartic QFT on a compact Riemannian manifold with boundary, showing that boundary singularities necessitate boundary-local counterterms and extension of the classical action; consistency under manifold gluing further implies renormalization of a boundary operator.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum field theory renormalization must be modified when the spacetime has a boundary, particularly whether standard bulk counterterms suffice or if new boundary-local divergences arise and how they affect consistency (e.g., under gluing of manifolds).

Method: Perturbative renormalization analysis of a scalar quartic model on a compact 3D Riemannian manifold with boundary, employing heat-kernel techniques and locality principles; examination of boundary contributions to divergent parts of Feynman diagrams and constraints from manifold gluing compatibility.

Result: Boundary singularities generate nontrivial, singular boundary counterterms beyond mass shift; the classical action must be extended by boundary-local operators; gluing consistency forces renormalization of a specific boundary operator (e.g., a boundary mass or derivative term).

Conclusion: Standard renormalization in QFT on manifolds with boundary is insufficient without incorporating boundary-local counterterms and operator renormalization; this extends the renormalization framework to include geometrically constrained boundary data and ensures compatibility under composition of spacetimes.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the renormalization of a three-dimensional quartic model on a compact connected Riemannian manifold with a boundary. We show that, in addition to the standard shift of the mass parameter, it is necessary to take into account boundary contributions involving singular coefficients. This indicates the need to extend the classical action within the framework of standard renormalization theory. The requirements of consistency regarding the gluing of manifolds lead to renormalization of an operator on the boundary.

</details>


### [107] [Bouncing shellworld embedded in charged AdS spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22855)
*Karma P. Sherpa,Rishi Pokhrel,Indra K. P. Chettri,Tanay K. Dey*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies cosmological evolution of a spherical brane in a 5D charged AdS bulk within the shellworld (dark bubble) scenario, showing nonsingular bouncing and cyclic behavior induced by bulk charge; large brane cosmological constant leads to eternal expansion; bounce outside bulk horizons resolves Cauchy horizon instability; scalar perturbations remain regular across bounce; inclusion of bulk long strings preserves bounce and avoids horizon instability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve Cauchy horizon instability in standard braneworld models and explore nonsingular cosmological evolution in higher-dimensional gravity with charge and bulk matter.

Method: Analytical and geometric analysis of a spherical brane embedded in a five-dimensional charged anti-de Sitter (AdS) bulk spacetime; examination of Friedmann-like dynamics on the brane; study of linear scalar perturbations; incorporation of uniformly distributed cosmic strings in the bulk.

Result: Nonsingular bouncing and cyclic cosmology for small brane cosmological constant; eternal expansion for large brane cosmological constant; bounce occurring outside bulk horizons eliminates Cauchy horizon instability; scalar field perturbations remain finite and regular across the bounce; bounce persists even with bulk long strings, maintaining horizon stability.

Conclusion: The shellworld scenario in a charged AdS bulk provides a robust framework for singularity-free cosmology with built-in resolution of key instabilities—particularly Cauchy horizon instability—and remains stable under inclusion of realistic bulk matter like long strings.

Abstract: We investigate the cosmological evolution of a spherical brane within the shellworld (dark bubble) scenario embedded in a five-dimensional charged AdS bulk spacetime. For zero or small value of the brane cosmological constant, the shellworld universe has a nonsingular bouncing and cyclic nature with the bounce induced by the charge parameter of the bulk spacetime. On large value of the brane cosmological constant, the shellworld undergoes a nonsingular bounce and expands eternally. Under certain conditions, the brane bounces outside the bulk horizons, leading to a geometrical resolution of the Cauchy horizon instability present in standard braneworld models. Furthermore, analysis of linear scalar perturbations reveals that test scalar field modes remain regular and non-divergent across the bounce. Finally, we incorporate uniformly distributed long strings in the bulk and demonstrate that the bounce still persists while avoiding the horizon instability.

</details>


### [108] [A pedagogical introduction to invariant theory and finite-$N$ holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22952)
*Robert de Mello Koch,Minkyoo Kim,Augustine Larweh Mahu,Anik Rudra*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper provides a pedagogical analysis of finite-N AdS/CFT using invariant theory to solve trace relations, yielding a non-redundant operator basis composed of primary (perturbative gravity) and secondary (non-perturbative gravity) invariants.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand finite-N corrections in AdS/CFT by resolving redundancies among gauge-invariant operators via trace relations, bridging CFT operator algebra and dual gravitational physics.

Method: Application of classical invariant theory—particularly the theory of generators and relations for polynomial invariants under matrix group actions—to classify gauge-invariant operators in U(N) or SU(N) gauge theories, solving the ideal of trace relations explicitly.

Result: A complete, non-redundant basis of gauge-invariant operators is constructed, partitioned into primary invariants (algebraically independent, spanning the 'coordinate ring') and secondary invariants (dependent but essential for full module structure); the former map to perturbative bulk fields, the latter encode instanton-like or D-brane effects.

Conclusion: Finite-N AdS/CFT operator counting and structure are governed by invariant-theoretic algebraic geometry; the primary/secondary dichotomy provides a precise algebraic characterization of the perturbative/non-perturbative split in quantum gravity.

Abstract: These lectures provide a pedagogical introduction to the study of finite-$N$ physics of the AdS/CFT correspondence. More specifically we develop the consequences of the trace relations for the space of gauge invariant operators, using powerful methods from invariant theory. Our key conclusions are \begin{itemize} \item that the complete set of trace relations can be solved, leaving a non-redundant set of gauge invariant operators, \item that this non-redundant set can be generated using two types of generators, called primary and secondary invariants, and \item that the primary invariants acquire a natural interpretation as perturbative gravitational degrees of freedom, while the secondary algebra encodes non-perturbative effects of the dual gravitational theory. \end{itemize}

</details>


### [109] [Perturbative Anomaly Inflow on Orbifolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23326)
*Hao Y. Zhang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes perturbative anomaly inflow for 6D orbi-instanton theories from M5-branes on C²/Zₖ near an M9-brane, using Donnelly’s equivariant APS theorem; it matches the full unflavored tensor-branch anomaly polynomial—including Kac-label-dependent remainder—and extends to flavored anomalies via E₈ centralizers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve and geometrically interpret the complete anomaly polynomial—including previously conjectured Kac-label-dependent remainder—for 6D A-type orbi-instanton SCFTs arising from M5-branes on C²/Zₖ at an M9-brane, within M-theory corner-inflow framework.

Method: Applies Donnelly's equivariant Atiyah–Patodi–Singer (APS) theorem to compute fixed-point densities on orbifold fixed loci; extracts degree-(d+2) component as d-dimensional anomaly polynomial; evaluates non-identity ALE fixed-point class on the M9 wall with flat E₈ connection ρ: Zₖ → E₈; combines with known M5/Hořava–Witten and ALE/Hořava–Witten contributions.

Result: The degree-eight component of the non-identity ALE fixed-point class matches the full conjectured Kac-label-dependent remainder of the 6D anomaly polynomial, including SU(2)ᵣ and tangent-bundle curvature dependence; the sum reproduces the total unflavored tensor-branch anomaly polynomial; flavor symmetries are incorporated via centralizers of ρ in E₈.

Conclusion: Perturbative anomaly inflow from orbifold fixed points fully accounts for the 6D orbi-instanton anomaly polynomial, supporting a local M-theory corner-inflow mechanism; the framework naturally extends to flavored anomalies.

Abstract: We study perturbative anomaly inflow for effective theories supported on orbifold fixed loci of the internal geometry. Donnelly's equivariant APS theorem gives a fixed-point density, whose degree-\((d+2)\) component is identified as the anomaly polynomial of the \(d\)-dimensional effective theory. We apply the construction to six-dimensional A-type orbi-instanton theories engineered by \(N\) M5-branes probing a transverse \(\mathbb{C}^2/\mathbb{Z}_k\) singularity at an end-of-the-world M9-brane. For a flat \(E_8\) connection specified by \(ρ:\mathbb{Z}_k\to E_8\), we compute the non-identity ALE fixed-point class on the M9 wall. Its degree-eight component agrees with the complete Kac-label-dependent remainder conjectured in \cite{MOTZ}, including the \(SU(2)_R\) and tangent-bundle curvatures. Together with the known M5/Hořava--Witten and ALE/Hořava--Witten terms, this reproduces the unflavored tensor-branch anomaly polynomial. This local equality further suggests a complete M-theory corner-inflow interpretation. The same prescription also be extended to include flavor symmetries as the centralizer of \(E_8\).

</details>


### [110] [A Combinatorial Origin of Locality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23008)
*Laurentiu Rodina,Giuseppe Sellaroli,Jonathan Valenzuela*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves that locality in planar φ³ theory emerges from hidden zeros—kinematic conditions where amplitudes vanish—combined with unitarity, using geometric combinatorics (polygon triangulations) and two new tools: the star test and smoothing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a complete all-multiplicity proof that locality—traditionally assumed as fundamental—can emerge from more basic on-shell principles (specifically hidden zeros), complementing prior results where unitarity emerged from locality.

Method: Reformulates locality in planar Tr(φ³) theory as a geometric combinatorics problem: propagators ↔ chords of an n-gon; locality ↔ chord sets forming a triangulation. Introduces the 'star test' (graphical criterion to detect pole incompatibility) and 'smoothing' (constructive procedure to find hidden zeros that eliminate non-local pole products). Uses on-shell kinematics and factorization constraints.

Result: Demonstrates that hidden zeros—imposed as regular kinematic vanishing conditions—are sufficient to forbid all non-triangulating (i.e., non-local) pole products, thereby enforcing full locality. Thus, locality and unitarity co-emerge from hidden zeros alone, given the planar pole alphabet and denominator degree bound.

Conclusion: Locality is not primitive but derivable: for planar φ³ amplitudes, the singularity structure is fully determined—and restricted to triangulations—by the requirement of hidden zeros, yielding the first all-multiplicity proof of locality emergence from on-shell conditions.

Abstract: Locality and unitarity are fundamental principles of quantum field theory. For tree-level scattering amplitudes, locality determines which propagator poles may occur together, while unitarity fixes their residues through factorization. Previous uniqueness theorems have repeatedly shown that unitarity can emerge from locality combined with other physical principles. In this Letter we give the first complete all-multiplicity proof for the emergence of locality in this setting. We focus on Tr($φ^3$) theory and phrase locality as a problem in geometric combinatorics. Planar propagators are chords of a polygon, and a denominator is local exactly when its chords triangulate the polygon, and non-local otherwise. We show that hidden zeros---regular kinematic conditions on which the amplitude vanishes---enforce pole compatibility and therefore imply a local singularity structure. The proof rests on two ingredients: a graphical \emph{star test} determines when a chosen zero eliminates a pole product, and a \emph{smoothing} procedure finds such a zero. Thus, once the planar pole alphabet and denominator bound are specified, locality and unitarity emerge together from a single on-shell principle: hidden zeros. Equivalently, our proof provides a novel characterization of a familiar concept: polygon triangulations are precisely the chord configurations that evade every star test.

</details>


### [111] [Love at the String Scale: Tidal Deformability Across the Black Hole-String Transition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23077)
*Roberto Emparan,Luis Lehner,Stefano Trezzi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes static tidal Love numbers for Horowitz-Polchinski string stars in D=4,5,6 and finds non-zero, rapidly ℓ-growing responses—contrasting black holes’ vanishing Love numbers—revealing a tidal signature of their extended, surface-less structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe whether the vanishing static Love numbers of black holes in 4D Einstein gravity persist across the black hole–string transition, where black hole microstates are described as self-gravitating excited strings (string stars).

Method: Analytical computation of static tidal Love numbers for the Horowitz-Polchinski string star solution for multipoles ℓ = 2,3,4 in spacetime dimensions D = 4,5,6; asymptotic analysis of large-ℓ behavior linking multipolar tidal weighting to the exponential winding condensate profile.

Result: Non-zero Love numbers for all computed (ℓ,D) cases; Love numbers grow rapidly with ℓ; large-ℓ scaling shows radial probing scale increases linearly with ℓ due to competition between tidal multipole weight and condensate tail.

Conclusion: The zero-Love rigidity of classical black holes does not survive the black hole–string transition; the distinctive ℓ-dependent growth of Love numbers provides a direct tidal fingerprint of the extended, horizonless, surface-less nature of string stars.

Abstract: Tidal deformability provides a sensitive probe of the structure of compact objects, and black holes are exceptional in having vanishing static Love numbers in four-dimensional Einstein gravity. We ask what happens to this fine-tuned rigidity across the black hole-string transition, where the same states are expected to admit a weakly coupled description as a self-gravitating highly excited string, or "string star". We compute the static tidal Love numbers of the Horowitz-Polchinski (HP) string star for multipoles $\ell=2,3,4$ in $D=4,5,6$. The response is non-zero in all cases, as in the available $α'$-corrected black hole results, so the zero-Love structure of four-dimensional Einstein gravity does not survive on either side of the transition. On the string side, however, the response has a distinctive multipolar structure: the Love numbers grow rapidly with $\ell$, and we show analytically that this growth originates from the competition between the multipolar weight of the tidal field and the exponential tail of the winding condensate, with the result that the radial scale probed by the deformability grows linearly with $\ell$ at large multipole number. This provides a direct tidal signature of the extended, surface-less nature of the string star. We discuss the comparison with $α'$-corrected black holes and the limitations of the currently available perturbative results at large $\ell$.

</details>


### [112] [A Third-Quantized Description of Spacetime Wormholes in AdS/CFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23111)
*Shinji Hirano*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proposes a third-quantized extension of AdS/CFT where quantum gravitational wavefunctions of connected bulk geometries form a Fock space; topology-changing interactions (splitting/joining) are governed by a radial Schrödinger evolution, concretely realized in AdS₃ with torus boundary; CFT-realizable states correspond to specific coherent states and may be nongeneric in the full gravitational Hilbert space; known wormhole amplitudes inform the interaction terms, and replica/baby-universe processes are incorporated consistently.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve conceptual tensions in AdS/CFT related to bulk topology change, factorization, and ensemble interpretations—particularly arising from spacetime wormholes—by constructing a consistent quantum framework for dynamical geometry connectivity without assuming a fundamental boundary ensemble.

Method: Third-quantization of gravity: building a Fock space of one-universe wavefunctions; reduced phase-space quantization to define physical Hilbert space and radial Schrödinger time; explicit construction in AdS₃ gravity with T² boundary; identification of CFT-realizable states as coherent states whose expectation values reproduce asymptotic CFT data; incorporation of known torus wormhole amplitudes as interaction matrix elements; complementary cutting-and-gluing formalism to derive effective ensembles.

Result: A well-defined third-quantized theory where topology change is unitary and ordered in radial time; demonstration that generic gravitational states lack CFT duals, implying CFT-realizable states are nongeneric and not superposition-closed; derivation of an effective ensemble interpretation from gravitational path integrals without postulating fundamental disorder; consistent inclusion of replica wormholes and baby-universe effects as part of the interaction dynamics.

Conclusion: Third-quantization provides a coherent quantum framework for dynamical bulk topology in AdS/CFT; it reconciles wormhole contributions with unitarity and locality expectations, while challenging the universality of the CFT duality—suggesting that the standard holographic dictionary applies only to a restricted, non-generic subspace of quantum gravitational states.

Abstract: We propose an extension of AdS/CFT in which quantum gravitational wavefunctions of connected bulk geometries are assembled into a Fock space of universes. Splitting and joining interactions in a third-quantized Hamiltonian then provide a quantum description of changes in bulk connectivity and topology. Reduced phase-space quantization supplies the physical one-universe Hilbert space and a radial Schrödinger evolution with respect to which the topology-changing interactions are ordered. We develop the construction concretely in AdS$_3$ gravity with a torus boundary, where these ingredients can be described explicitly. The light spectrum is treated as fixed, non-normalizable background data, whereas the normalizable states above the black-hole threshold are quantized as dynamical modes. A conventional boundary theory is selected by choosing a coherent state whose universe-field expectation value, together with the fixed background contribution, yields consistent CFT data at the asymptotic boundary. Generic one-universe wavefunctions need not admit such an interpretation. This raises the possibility that CFT-realizable wavefunctions are nongeneric in the gravitational state space and are not closed under arbitrary superpositions of gravitational states. The known two-torus spacetime-wormhole amplitude, which lies at the heart of the factorization problem, provides concrete input for the topology-changing interactions of the third-quantized theory. A complementary cutting-and-gluing description yields an effective ensemble interpretation when the resulting gravitational sectors admit consistent boundary-theory interpretations, without assuming a fundamental ensemble of boundary theories. We also discuss replica wormholes and baby-universe processes within the same framework.

</details>


### [113] [Conformal and Noncommutative Gravity from Gauge Symmetry Breaking and SO(2,16) Unification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23220)
*Stelios Stefas,George Zoupanos*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reviews gauge-theoretic formulations of Conformal and Noncommutative (Fuzzy) Gravity and proposes their unification with SO(10) Grand Unified Theory by exploiting a mismatch between tangent-space symmetry group dimension and spacetime dimension.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize gravity as a gauge theory—reviving a long-standing idea—and to unify it with internal symmetries in a Grand Unified framework.

Method: Review and synthesis of gauge-theoretic constructions of Conformal Gravity and Noncommutative (Fuzzy) Gravity; conceptual extension via dimensional mismatch between tangent-space symmetry group and spacetime manifold.

Result: A unified gauge-theoretic framework embedding both Conformal and Fuzzy Gravity with SO(10) GUT, enabled by relaxing the assumption that tangent-space symmetry dimension equals spacetime dimension.

Conclusion: Gravity can be consistently formulated as a gauge theory compatible with grand unification when tangent-space symmetry is generalized beyond standard Lorentz or diffeomorphism constraints.

Abstract: Motivated by the old idea of treating gravity as a gauge theory, we review the gauge-theoretic construction of Conformal Gravity and of Noncommutative (Fuzzy) Gravity. We then outline how these gauge formulations of gravity can be unified with internal interactions described by an SO(10) Grand Unified Theory, based on the critical observation that the dimension of the tangent-space symmetry group of a curved manifold need not coincide with the spacetime dimension.

</details>


### [114] [Analytical Cosmological Collider at Strong Mixing in Laplace Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23243)
*Nathan Belrhali,Arthur Poisson,Sébastien Renaux-Petel*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analytically solves the strong-coupling regime of primordial non-Gaussianities in multifield inflation using a novel Laplace-space representation, yielding a rapidly convergent bispectrum series and revealing an enhanced cosmological collider signal with reduced Boltzmann suppression and derivative-dependent amplitude growth.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the regime of primordial non-Gaussianities where curvature perturbations couple strongly to massive fields—beyond perturbative treatment—in multifield inflation.

Method: Uses a recently introduced Laplace-space representation in which each mode is expressed as a superposition of plane waves weighted by a single kernel; computes the bispectrum as a rapidly convergent series of elementary functions.

Result: Derives an analytic bispectrum in perfect agreement with numerical results; finds the cosmological collider signal at strong mixing is twice as large as previously thought (only half the usual Boltzmann suppression) and its amplitude increases with the number of time derivatives in the interaction.

Conclusion: The strong-coupling regime of multifield inflation is tractable analytically via Laplace-space methods, and the enhanced, derivative-amplified cosmological collider signal identifies optimal observational targets for probing high-scale physics.

Abstract: Primordial non-Gaussianities are at their largest when the curvature perturbation is coupled to a massive field by a mixing too strong to be treated perturbatively. We show that this regime, naturally reached in multifield inflation, is solved analytically by the Laplace-space representation we recently introduced, in which every mode becomes a superposition of plane waves weighted by a single kernel. The bispectrum then follows as a rapidly convergent series of elementary functions, in perfect agreement with independent numerical results. We find that the cosmological collider signal at strong mixing is larger than widely believed, with only half of the usual Boltzmann suppression. Its amplitude further grows with the number of time derivatives of the curvature perturbation in the interaction, singling out the most promising observational targets.

</details>


### [115] [The ETH matrix model for DSSYK: non-perturbative corrections and intersection theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23298)
*Eleonora Alfinito,Matteo Beccaria*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a correspondence between the DSSYK model's ETH matrix model and sine-dilaton gravity beyond disk/wormhole topologies, using q-deformed zeta values and quasimodular forms; it derives exact non-perturbative corrections controlled by ̃q = e^{-4π²/λ}, shows their linear ̃q contribution reduces to intersection numbers of κ-classes on M̄_{g,n}, and computes them explicitly—including for cases where closed-form q-deformed volumes are unknown—extending also to O(̃q²).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the established disk-and-wormhole duality between the DSSYK ETH matrix model and sine-dilaton gravity to arbitrary genus and boundary topology, and to characterize the exact non-perturbative structure of higher-genus correlators via modular and topological invariants.

Method: Leverages the ring structure of quasimodular forms (E_2, E_4, E_6) and S-duality to extract the ̃q = e^{-4π⁲/λ} non-perturbative scale; identifies the linear-̃q correction with q-deformed Weil--Petersson volumes; reduces these to finite sums of κ-class intersection numbers on ̄M_{g,n} using known relations from topological recursion and cohomological field theory; computes explicit results for (g,n) up to (4,1), including cases without closed-form q-deformed volumes, and extends to O(̃q⁲).

Result: The leading non-perturbative correction ̃q is universal across all fixed-genus, multi-boundary amplitudes—not just the disk—and its linear term is fully encoded in intersection numbers of κ-classes; explicit tabulated values are provided for all known closed-form N_{g,n} and newly computed for (3,1), (3,2), (4,1); O(̃q⁲) corrections are derived for the same cases.

Conclusion: The non-perturbative structure of DSSYK correlators is governed by modular symmetry and topological invariants of moduli space, transcending the original matrix model construction; this reveals a deep link between quantum chaos, 2D dilaton gravity, and enumerative geometry, enabling efficient computation of otherwise intractable higher-genus amplitudes.

Abstract: At leading order in the genus expansion the ETH matrix model for DSSYK reproduces its correlators by construction, while its higher-genus corrections are conjectured to capture higher-topology contributions in the dual sine-dilaton gravity -- a correspondence established so far only for the disk and the wormhole. At fixed genus the correlators are built from discrete volumes $N_{g,n}$, polynomial in $q$-deformed zeta values $ζ_q(2k)$ with $q=e^{-λ}$, $λ$ being the DSSYK coupling. These lie in the ring of quasimodular forms generated by the Eisenstein series $E_2,E_4,E_6$, whose $S$-duality yields an exact closed form for the leading non-perturbative correction as $λ\to0$, controlled by $\widetilde q=e^{-4π^2/λ}$. Known at disk level, this scale is shown here to govern the fixed-genus, higher-boundary amplitudes as well. We show that the term linear in $\widetilde q$, at leading order in $λ$, is captured entirely by the $q$-deformed Weil--Petersson volumes, and reduces to a finite sum of intersection numbers of $κ$-classes on the moduli space $\overline{\mathcal M}_{g,n}$ of stable curves, computable without repeating the topological recursion that produced the $N_{g,n}$. We tabulate it for every $(g,n)$ whose $q$-deformed volume is known in closed form, and extend it to $(3,1),(3,2),(4,1)$, where none is available. The construction is not restricted to leading order: we work out $O(\widetilde q^2)$ for the same cases.

</details>


### [116] [Landau theory, effective temperature, and tricritical phenomena in a holographic nonequilibrium steady state](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23378)
*Motohiro Kanazawa,Masataka Matsumoto,Shin Nakamura*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies critical exponents (γ, ν) at a current-driven tricritical point (TCP) in a nonequilibrium D3/D7 holographic model with chiral symmetry breaking. It finds that γ and ν deviate from Landau theory predictions unless the ratio T*/T is held fixed—indicating Landau behavior only along that specific path in parameter space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how critical phenomena at a current-driven tricritical point in a nonequilibrium steady state (described by the D3/D7 holographic model) depart from conventional Landau theory predictions, especially under chiral symmetry breaking.

Method: Holographic analysis of the D3/D7 model to compute critical exponents (γ, ν) at the current-driven tricritical point; systematic variation of control parameters (e.g., effective temperature T*, heat bath temperature T) to probe path dependence in the phase diagram.

Result: Critical exponents γ and ν are path-dependent near the TCP: they deviate from Landau values except when approaching the TCP along trajectories where the ratio T*/T is held constant—in which case they recover standard Landau exponents.

Conclusion: The Landau theory suffices only along the T*/T-fixed line; a generalized framework beyond Landau theory is required to describe critical behavior along arbitrary paths in the nonequilibrium phase diagram.

Abstract: We investigate the critical exponents $(γ,ν)$ at the current-driven tricritical point (TCP) associated with chiral symmetry breaking in a nonequilibrium steady state described by the D3/D7 model. In the symmetry-broken phase, we find that, unlike in the conventional Landau theory, both $γ$ and $ν$ can take values different from those predicted by the Landau theory and depend on the path along which the TCP is approached in the phase diagram. However, when the TCP is approached with the ratio of the effective temperature to the heat bath temperature, $T_{*}/T$, held fixed, the critical exponents agree with those of the Landau theory. These results suggest that the critical phenomena at the current-driven TCP may be described by the Landau theory along the $T_{*}/T$-fixed line, whereas a nontrivial extension of the Landau theory is required for a more general description of the critical phenomena along arbitrary paths.

</details>


### [117] [Energy of toroidal M2 brane in flat 11d background](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23517)
*Arkady A. Tseytlin,Zihan Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes quantum corrections to the energy of M2-branes in 11-dimensional supermembrane theory, finding absence of log UV divergences up to two loops; BPS cancellation occurs for periodic fermions, while antiperiodic cases yield Bessel-function–dependent energies; a novel ζ(9) term appears at three loops; bubble-graph resummation yields a cubic equation for membrane energy—unlike the square-root string result—and suggests incompatibility with the type 0A string strong-coupling conjecture.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether certain physical quantities in the non-renormalizable supermembrane theory—specifically the energy of wrapped M2-branes—remain finite (logarithmically divergence-free) beyond classical order, and to test implications for proposed dualities, notably between M-theory on an antiperiodic circle and type 0A string theory.

Method: Perturbative expansion in inverse membrane tension; two-loop computation of toroidally wrapped M2-brane energy in flat 11D space; treatment of fermionic boundary conditions (periodic/antiperiodic); three-loop calculation for bosonic membrane on ℝ²×S¹; systematic resummation of bubble diagrams using zeta-function regularization and analytic continuation.

Result: Two-loop M2 energy is free of logarithmic UV divergences; fermionic periodicity preserves BPS cancellation; antiperiodic cases yield radii-dependent energies expressible via infinite sums of modified Bessel functions; three-loop bosonic membrane energy contains a new ζ(9) term absent in string theory; bubble resummation gives a cubic equation for energy—non-vanishing for all R₁₁—contrasting the exact square-root string dispersion relation.

Conclusion: The supermembrane’s quantum energy exhibits structural differences from strings (e.g., ζ(9) term, cubic dispersion), and the non-vanishing bubble contribution across all R₁₁ challenges the conjectured duality between 11D supermembrane theory on an antiperiodic circle and the strong-coupling limit of type 0A string theory—unless non-bubble diagrams qualitatively alter this behavior.

Abstract: The supermembrane action is non-linear and a priori non-renormalizable. Still, some quantities in this theory may be free of log UV divergences and thus defined unambiguously. To explore this possibility we compute the energy of an M2 brane wrapped on a 2-torus in flat 11d space to two loops in the inverse-tension expansion. The result is free of logarithmic divergences and the same should hold also at higher loop orders. For fermions periodic around both circles the quantum corrections cancel, consistent with the BPS nature of the wrapped M2 brane state. For fermions antiperiodic around one or both circles the energy is a non-trivial function of the radii given by infinite sums of modified Bessel functions. We also compute 3-loop correction to the energy of the bosonic membrane on $\mathbb R^2\times S^1$. In contrast to the string case, it contains, besides powers of the 1-loop $ζ(3)$ coefficient, a new term proportional to $ζ(9)$. Summing contributions of all-loop bubble graphs leads to a simple cubic equation for the membrane energy. The analogous resummation in the Nambu or GS string t case reproduces the familiar exact square-root expression ${\mathcal E}=\sqrt{(2πR\,T_1)^2+m_0^2}$. We find that the bubble-graph part of the membrane energy does not vanish for any value of the radius $R_{11}$. If contributions of other non-trivial diagrams in the supermembrane case do not qualitatively change this conclusion, this would disfavour the conjectured relation between 11d theory on an antiperiodic circle and the strong-coupling limit of type 0A string theory.

</details>
